# Vehicles

The quads, cars, Land Rovers, tractor, UTV and trailers: specs, physics, drivetrains, dashes, lights and models.

## Where things stand

What is true now, with the sections below to read for the detail. Keep it current: an item that changes how something stands fixes the line here as well as adding its section.

- **Vehicles are data.** Each one is a spec in `quadSpecs.js` built by `QuadUnit`: the regular quads (red, blue, green, yellow, orange), the extras (kids' quad, loan quad, mini moto, ATC), the tractor, the UTV, two Land Rovers (Series III, 200TDi), the girls' cars (Fiesta, Corsa), your own car (the 309, a diesel) and the vintage bike, plus the rusty barn find. A spec needs a `style` and `dashStyle` that exist, and a place in `FOR_SALE` or `NOT_FOR_SALE`. In-game names are made-up makers and models (Kestrel, Tamura, Ridgeback, Viper, Hartley, Moorland, Thorne, Ashby), never colours or real makes. "Second quad (2026-10-04)", "In-game names for every vehicle (#296 and #297)".
- **The fleet.** Every vehicle is in one random pool: four to all of them each game, at least two regular quads, and the barn find on its own roll. Missing vehicles switch off what needs them, so guard any `x === this.tractorUnit` against null. At most four start in the yard; the big ones never do. "Every vehicle in the random pool (TODO 3)", "Where the vehicles start, spread out (2026-10-11)".
- **Physics.** One Rapier raycast vehicle with a per-spec layout (wheels, radius, stiffness, driven, `outrigger`), with `scale` applied to the chassis and the model together. The mini moto leans for real (`bikeLean.js`, roll torque, lean drives the turn, and only you can fall). The ATC tips from a narrow stance and leans the chassis. Rider weight topples on slopes only. An empty quad without its parking brake rolls; with a rider it holds itself only below about 6 degrees. "A real two-wheeler model (TODO 7)", "The old three-wheeler leans in (TODO 5)", "The handbrake (TODO 16)", "Rolling back on the hill (TODO 3, follow-up)".
- **Drivetrains.** The quads are CVT automatics, except the red, green and yellow, which are four-speed manuals, as are the tractor (three-speed), the Land Rovers and the cars. A manual has false neutrals when rushed, one possibly worn gear that jumps out on a climb, crash gears on the Series (double-declutch them), and a clutch that can wear. The girls shift with `autoShifter.js` and never miss. S means backwards everywhere. 2WD and 4WD (and a diff lock) are a switch on the red, blue and the Land Rovers. "Manual quads (TODO 9)" with its follow-up, "Neutral and jumping out of gear (TODO 17)", "2WD and 4WD (TODO 3)", "The Land Rover (TODO 14)", "A worn clutch on the manual quads (TODO 50)".
- **Character dealt per game.** History (wear following the hours, one or two starting faults, some tinkering, who had it out last), one to three quirks, tyres per wheel and axle (pressure, brand, tread), a hidden strength, and the pods' places. The girls know these with `diagnoseAccuracy` and can be wrong. "A history (TODO 5)", "Quirks (TODO 6)", "A fuel gauge you can't trust (#56, was TODO 161)", "Tyres (TODO 7)", "A quad better in some places (#27, was TODO 132)".
- **Dashes and pods.** Each dash follows its real counterpart's (`dash.js`, faces drawn on canvas, redrawn only on change, drums that roll, game-hour meters). Two accessory pods (analogue and digital) show everything and are swapped with the toolbox. Lamps use symbols, not words. There's no HUD readout on a vehicle: you find out by the dash, the sound and the feel. "The dashes, planned (2026-10-06)", "The dashes, built (2026-10-06)", "Better instrument pods (TODO 18)", "Dash temperature gauges, no vehicle readout (2026-10-05)".
- **Lights.** The headlamps follow the revs (dim at idle, out when stalled on a magneto). Beams are faked cones, plus real light from the six-spotlight pool. Tail and brake lights share the supply. The cars and big vehicles have indicators. Lamps fitted at the bench add a pool light each. "Headlights that follow the revs (TODO 19)", "Night riding with lamps (#33, was TODO 138)".
- **Models.** Procedural, built with the detail kit (`src/vehicle/detail/`: tread, wear baked from the geometry's edges and nooks, decals with made-up makers only, fixings, cables, brakes, real sizes, triangle budgets, fixed points to 2 mm) and the cab kit. The chain-driven ones draw a live chain and toothed sprockets (`detail/chainDrive.js`) that follow the chain fault. `.claude/rules/vehicle-detail.md` has the method and the red quad's lessons. Each "in full detail" item records its proportion fixes. "A shared detail kit for the vehicle models (TODO 87)", "A cab and interior kit (TODO 340)", "Chain and sprockets you can see wear (#48)", and each vehicle's own section.
- **Big vehicles and cars** share `landys` (mounts, belts, seat tips, rides, shelter). The cars only go down the farm road with their owner, and borrowing costs favour. Your car is one of them with `owner: 'you'`: only you drive it, and every day starts with you sat in it, parked in a row with theirs on the pad by the Dutch barn, the girls in their own running cars or in yours (`farm/arrival.js`). A diesel or an injected engine has no choke (`hasChoke`). "Your own car (#229, was TODO 338)", "Your car: fixes from the first look (#491)". Seats sit 0.12 m in front of the backrest, and the eye-to-windscreen distance was measured in each. "The girls' cars (TODO 161)" and its passes, "Seats that ride differently (TODO 124)".
- **Brakes** can be wet (after the ford), binding (stood long) or hot: held on down a long hill they fade, and a manual's low gear holds it back instead. "Wet and binding brakes (TODO 1)", "Brakes that fade on the hill (#47)".
- **Towing and trailers.** Trailers are pure hitch models (`TrailerMotion`), not physics bodies, so they can't flip a vehicle. They jack-knife in reverse. Load is a cheap drive and brake factor (`towing.js`). Chains and the tow rope are stiff springs; the winch is the green's. The bale trailer carries a quad on its ramps. "The little tipping trailer (TODO 6)", "Reversing a trailer (TODO 78)", "Tractor to the rescue (TODO 12)", "A quad towing a quad (TODO 39)", "Loading a quad onto the bale trailer (TODO 6)". Your own vehicle's mirrors are live, drawn a mirror at a time from a camera at the glass: "Working mirrors (#61)".
- **The barn finds** (the rusty quad and the vintage bike) are restorations: bench jobs until `startBlocked` lifts. They're shown with a seeded damage set, and you right them yourself. "The barn find (TODO 1)", "The rusty quad in full detail (TODO 97)", "A vintage motorbike in the barn (#38, was TODO 143)".
- **Loading.** Vehicles settle for 2 s of physics before the sound comes on, so nobody hears them land. Each layout's `spawnSag` starts the body already sunk. "No crash of vehicles landing at the start (2026-10-09)".

---

The sections below are the record, oldest first. A new decision goes at the end.

## Second quad (2026-10-04)

- Quads are data: `src/vehicle/quadSpecs.js` lists each one's name, colour and parameter overrides, and `QuadUnit` builds the engine, bike, vibration, smoke and sound for it. Spawn points are `LAYOUT.spawn.quads`, in the same order. Adding another quad is a spec plus a spawn point.
- Red quad: the original tuning, Honda-style. Blue quad: Yamaha-style. It idles at 1,450 rpm warm (Yamaha's manual figure for its quads is 1,450 to 1,550), revs to 9,000, has a quicker throttle response, needs more choke cold, runs lumpier cold, warms in 120 s rather than 150 s, and is lighter and stronger. Its cold engine is weaker, which keeps the cold hard-braking stall (from about 18 mph) despite the faster revs; warm it never stalls from braking.
- The blue quad's recorded idle uses the rev-sweep machine's own idle pulses (`recIdleSource: 'rev'`), so it's one machine throughout and sounds different from the red.
- One AudioContext (`AudioHub`) with one worklet voice per quad. Master volume, mute, wind and latency are shared ("Sound" in the panel); everything else is per quad.
- Paint and headlight lens materials are per quad; other materials are shared.
- She can be asked onto either quad. If she's seated on one and asked onto the other, she gets off and walks over. Menu options are per quad, nearest first.
- The HUD status and the panel's live readouts follow the quad you're riding, else the one you're looking at, else the last one you used.

## Third quad: two-stroke sport (2026-10-04)

- Automatic like the others (a manual gearbox was judged too complex for now). No premix fuel yet.
- The engine model has a `strokes` setting: 2 fires every revolution. Pulse periods carry through to vibration, smoke and sound unchanged. Two-stroke character comes from a few new settings, all 0 for the four-strokes so they behave exactly as before: `skipChance` (alternate firings skipped at light throttle and low revs, the ring-ding), `idleFoulRate` (oily plugs load up at idle and throttle clears them), and on the drivetrain `powerbandRpm` / `torqueBelowBand` (weak low down, then on the pipe).
- Its idle is 1,800 rpm rather than Yamaha's 1,500 for the Blaster, because the recorded two-stroke idles around 2,200 and stretching its pulses much further slows the sound unnaturally.
- It rarely stalls under hard braking, even cold: its high idle and quick revving pull it through the braking drag. Weakening its cold engine enough to force a stall made it miserable to ride from cold, so that's left as part of its character.
- Sound: a second pulse bank (`engine-bank-2t`) cut from a two-stroke motocross bike's idle and a Honda CRM250 pulling away and backing off. The slicer now takes a bank name and named sources, and normalises every bank so idle pulses sit at the same loudness. A grain's "rpm" is defined as 120 / pulse period for every engine type, which is what the runtime matches on, so two-strokes need no special case in grain picking. The hub loads both banks and each quad picks one with `recBank`.
- Models: `buildQuadModel` takes a `style`: 'utility' (red, as before), 'utility2' (blue: brush guard, square lamps, railed racks, rounded tank, grey seat, flatter fenders) and 'sport' (yellow: sloped nose with number plate, small lamps, bumper, sharp fenders, no racks, long single seat with tail, nerf bars and pegs, expansion chamber with a high silencer, upright finned cylinder, motocross bars with a crossbar ahead of the dash, wider rear tyres).
- The sport quad is a single-seater: no pillion seat or anchor, so she isn't offered it (`quad.hasPillion`).
- Its smoke is denser and blue-tinted (a new `tint` smoke setting) for two-stroke oil.

## Start state and the yellow pillion (2026-10-04)

- Fuel taps start OFF (`startTap`). The float bowl starts full, so a quad started with the tap off runs for a few seconds, then stumbles and stalls: flicking it ON is part of starting up.
- Each game the quads are shuffled between the yard's parking slots and nudged by up to 1.2 m and 20 degrees, keeping at least 2.8 m between them. The jerry can is left at one of five likely spots (petrol tank, barn doors, paddock, silage bales, field gate) with a small shift. The seed is printed in the console and `?seed=N` repeats a layout. The player starts facing the quad she's standing by.
- The yellow sport quad now takes a passenger after all: a slim pad at the back of its long seat with a grab strap. It's a tight fit, which is the point.

## Quad bodywork, getting off at speed, ground jolts, more of her (2026-10-05)

Done in parallel by subagents in separate worktrees (bodywork, getting off at speed, her figure), merged into main.

- Bodywork: the quads are still entirely procedural, now built from moulded shapes: fender shells swept round each wheel, lofted seats, tanks and hoods, bent-tube racks and frames, lathed tyres with chunky lugs, real-looking rims, CV boots, coil-overs and engines. Each keeps its character (red: round lamps, racks, swingarm; blue: square lamps, brush guard, independent rear; yellow: number plate, nerf bars, expansion chamber, chain drive). Wishbones, swingarms and shocks are posed from the physics wheel heights every frame. Static parts are merged per material: about 36k to 46k triangles per quad (from about 10k) with fewer draw calls than before. All anchors (seat, pillion, camera, dash, wheels, exhaust tip) are where they were.
- Getting off at speed: above walking pace E is a jump to the outside of the turn (inside or behind if that's blocked) with a short stumble, and the quad carries on. A small PI cruise controller (`src/vehicle/cruise.js`, tested) holds the speed it had on the throttle only, starting from 40% of the throttle you had so it doesn't overshoot, and the bars stay where they were, so a held turn is a steady circle (about 6 m radius at 10 m/s). It lets go when it tips, the engine stops, or it's stuck below 30% of its target for 3 s, and then the parking brake takes over. You can get back on below 3 m/s. If she's on the back she shouts while it's driving itself and tells you what she thinks once it stops.
- Feedback: a riderless quad going round didn't shake. Engine shake fades at high revs (it's about 0.2 on the body-shake measure flat out, against 0.6 at a warm idle), and the terrain is too smooth at this scale to jolt it. The vibration model now adds jolts from the ground: one every 0.8 m or so on average, sized by speed, mostly small with the odd big one, into the same body, bars and head oscillators. At 9 m/s a riderless quad now measures about 1.0, like a cold idle. You feel it riding too.
- Her lines: about three times as many (around 300 spoken lines), plus new situations: gentle riding, a long ride together, you getting on in front of her, the odd warm word when you're stood together, the seat shaking hard (riding, or left sat on an idling quad; after 40 s of that she admits she quite likes it), and a favourite quad picked each game that she mentions once per ride. Body-shake level for her comments is `voiceShakeLevel` (0.95). All re-recorded with the cori voice.
- Agent test browsers spoke her lines out loud through the speakers while testing (the "muppet" heard without captions). Test scripts now stub speech synthesis.

## Shake, shouting, dashes (2026-10-05)

- Feedback: the shake had become more visible when riding, which should stay as it was; it's a quad driving itself that should visibly shake, and still didn't enough. Ground jolts now only apply with nobody on it, and much harder (`groundGain` 8): a riderless quad going round at 9 m/s measures about 2.6 on the body-shake measure (it was 0.2 without jolts; a warm idle is about 0.6). With you on it the vibration is exactly as before.
- Left on a quad driving itself, she now picks a mood each time: terrified, or loving it (`ghostFunChance` 0.4), each with its own lines while it goes and afterwards.
- Feedback: she should shout louder when you're further away. Every line gets louder with distance, `1 + 0.12` per metre beyond 4 m, up to 2.5 times, and situations marked `shout: true` (left behind, riding itself and its aftermath) are 1.6 times louder again, fade with distance at half the rate, and can be heard twice as far (50 m). A shout at 15 m comes out at about -5 dB against hearing her up close; it was about -21 dB.
- Dashes: all pods are now moulded rounded shapes (an extruded rounded outline with bevelled edges), with rounded LCD corners. The red quad's dash is three analogue dials in a pill-shaped pod: rev counter (now scaled to its 6,200 rpm instead of a fixed 10,000), speedo with a drum trip counter, and an E to F fuel gauge with a reserve zone and the tap position, with lamps set into the dials. Tried a curved hood over the blue's display, but it hid the rev bars from the seat, so it's a slim rounded brow instead.
- The voice generator caches each line's recording (`--cache`), so after editing lines only new or changed ones are recorded again.

## Dash temperature gauges, no vehicle readout (2026-10-05)

- Request: take away the helpful vehicle HUD so you have to guess what's wrong, and give each quad a temperature gauge. The top-left panel (engine state, rpm, fuel, choke, speed) is gone while on or looking at a quad; the notes for the hose, winch, toolbox and jerry can stay, since they say what keys do. The tuning panel's live readouts (backtick) are untouched, as a developer tool.
- The gauge scale lives in `src/vehicle/tempGauge.js` (pure, tested): a fully warm engine sits at 0.7 of the dial, the red zone starts at 0.85 and nothing reaches it yet, ready for the heat item. The cold zone ends where the old cold light went out (`warmLightThreshold`, 0.65 of the way to warm). Needles rest at C with the ignition off.
- Each style has its own: a tiny dial low in the red's rev counter, a dial in the green's old cold-lamp slot (cream face), and a row of five blocks on the blue, yellow and orange LCDs (blue while cold, the last one red).
- The cold lamps are gone, replaced by the gauges, so there's one place to look rather than two. This also removes "(R to restart)" and "(out of fuel?)": you have to work those out.
- Checked once in the browser at a close-up of each dash. The red and green dials are small; I didn't judge them by eye at riding distance beyond the seat-height close-up.

## Extra vehicles and a changing line-up (2026-10-05)

Item 20, part one. Jamie's calls: skip the three-wheeler (tip-over physics is the hard part), do the kids' quad and the loan quad, vary the line-up by sometimes removing one or two regular quads too, engine sounds from the existing model for now ("we might want to change them later").

- **Line-up** (`src/vehicle/fleet.js`, from its own random stream off the seed so the rest of a seed's layout doesn't depend on it): none, one or two regulars are away (35, 40, 25 per cent); none, one or both extras turn up (30, 50, 20 per cent); never fewer than three vehicles. Seven parking slots now (two added in a second row), so a full house fits. The tests count 400 seeds to check it varies.
- **Kids' quad** (`EXTRA_SPECS` in `quadSpecs.js`): `scale: 0.65` shrinks (first 0.75, then smaller at Jamie's request) the model and the physics chassis together (wheel positions, suspension mount, collider; the spec gives the wheel radius and suspension lengths already scaled). The anchors people sit from are counter-scaled so the girls stay full size on it, which looks right and a bit silly. `solo: true` leaves off the racks and pillion pad, so nobody rides on the back. A two-stroke on the yellow's pre-mix, with a pull-start; 9.5 m/s top speed against 15 to 21 for the others, and an idle of 1,100 to 1,300 rpm, lower than the yellow's and the orange's. Its sound is the two-stroke bank with a short pipe and a thin tone: a placeholder.
- **Jess's quad**: `owner: 'jess'` on the spec. Amy never picks it when bored (`mayRide`), and Jess picks it first when she can drive (`ownsQuad`). Favourite quads are only ones she may ride and has lines for.
- **Loan quad**: the blue's body in neutral silver, fuel injected (little cold enrichment, catches almost every time, warms in 70 s), smooth idle, a fifteenth of the usual wear rate (0.0008 a minute against 0.012) and a build roughness of 0 to 0.015, full tank, a bit more power than the blue. Same sound as the red, quieter, as a placeholder.
- **Checked**: unit tests; one headless run on seed 12 (the kids' quad looks right and sits on the ground, drives stably to 8.9 m/s on grass, Jess comes for it unprompted).
- **Not checked**: the girls sitting on the kids' quad (heights, legs on the pegs), the loan quad up close, how either sounds, and long play with a thin line-up.


## Second pass on the extra vehicles, the hay door and a sound check (2026-10-05)

- Loan quad: neutral silver, build roughness 0 to 0.015 and a wear rate of 0.0008 a minute (the default is 0.012). Kids' quad: scale 0.65 (from 0.75), idle 1,100 to 1,300 rpm with a clutch that bites at 2,200, so it ticks over lower than the yellow (1,500 to 1,800) and the orange (2,000 to 2,300).
- The hay door's white frame shared planes with the wall (sill top, the two reveals and the lintel underside), the same flicker the barn doors had. Each frame piece now stands 0.02 m proud of the opening's edge. Not checked: that it's gone in motion, as a still can't show flicker.
- Sound dropping out after about a minute: not reproduced. A 50 s run with six engines running, rain and a quad being driven showed the audio context running, no NaNs at the output and no worklet errors. So the hub now logs when the context changes state, logs a failing engine processor, and retries `resume()` once a second if the browser has paused it. If it happens again, the console says which.
- Toolbox couldn't be picked up: since the workbench went in, the bench's invisible interaction box (deeper than the bench) was always hit before the toolbox's own, so the prompt was always the bench's. Interactables now take a `priority`, and a direct hit on a higher priority one wins over a nearer one; the toolbox has 1. Checked headless from three distances and heights: the prompt is "Pick up the toolbox".
- Sound soak, longer: 140 s with six engines running, rain and a quad being driven; context running throughout, no NaNs, no worklet errors, output peaks 0.4 to 0.8. Still not reproduced.

## Each quad has a knack (2026-10-06)

Picked item 21 as the first with nothing done (item 20 still has the mini moto). Scope settled by the item: two knacks, hidden from the player, known to the girls.

- **Green: the double blip.** `src/engine/knack.js` (pure, tested) counts rising edges of the throttle past `knackBlipAt` 0.5 while the engine isn't running; blips go stale after `knackWindow` 8 s and a running engine forgets them. Cold (under `knackColdBelow` 0.6) with fewer than `knackBlips` done, the catch chance is multiplied by `knackMissFactor` 0.04, so it cranks and sputters but almost never fires. Everything is an engine param with a range, `knackBlips` 0 on every quad but the green (2). Over 20 seeded cold starts with full choke and 6 s on the starter, two blips first caught many more than none (test asserts a gap of 8 or more).
- **Blue: choke off sooner.** `chokeEnrichment` 1.5 (default 1.0), so full choke over-enriches as soon as it warms: it runs rich, hunts and fouls unless the choke comes off early. Its cold need (1.1) is still met, so it still starts.
- **The girls.** `StartingAttempt` has `knackChance` 0.6: at the start she may already know the trick and blips the throttle twice (`helperThrottle` on the quad, `blipHold` 0.2 s, `blipGap` 0.3 s) before cranking, saying `startKnack`. If she doesn't, a failed go can make her remember (the existing `rememberChance`). Near you cranking the cold green without the trick, `crankStruggleKnack` is the hint; near a running blue left on choke with the mixture rich, `chokeOffBlue`. Lines are in `knackLines.js`. No nicknames yet, so they say "the green" (item 35 can change that).
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless that the game loads without errors. Not checked: how it feels to blip and crank by hand, whether 0.04 is too punishing on the green in play, how the blue's rich running sounds, or the girls' lines in the running game.

## The dashes, planned (2026-10-06)

Brief for TODO 26 in `docs/briefs/dashes.md`, with SVG mockups in `docs/briefs/dashes/`. Settled with Jamie:

- Each quad's own dash goes close to the real machine it's based on: the red a Honda Foreman's speedo with hour meter and odometer, R / N / oil lamps and the key; the blue a Grizzly 660's grey-green LCD; the loan a newer Kodiak-style LCD; the green a Bayou's two lamps and key on its nacelle; the yellow a Blaster's single lamp on its headlamp; the orange bare race bars with a kill switch; the kids' quad a key. Most lose the rev counter, fuel and temperature gauges they have now.
- Jamie's idea to make up for it: two accessory pods, one older analogue, one new digital, each showing everything for whichever quad it's fitted to. Only one of each, swapped with the toolbox, and each starts every game in the toolbox or on a random quad. Not realistic (a meter wouldn't carry readings between quads), but more fun than bolting extra gauges onto every quad, which was the first plan for the red and dropped.
- Reference photos (eBay listings, not committed) were used for layouts only; the mockups are drawn from scratch.

## The dashes, built (2026-10-06)

Built to the brief above. What differs or wasn't said:

- Drawing: every dash and pod is one canvas face drawn in the mockups' own millimetre coordinates (`dashParts.js` scales the context so the code reads against the SVG), over a moulded housing. Faces redraw only when something they show changes, with the needle and bar values quantised for the gate. Pure parts are `dashState.js` (neutral, lamps, counters, usage, clock) and `pods.js` (where each pod is), both with tests.
- Hours and odometer: `hoursRange` and `odoRange` on each spec (the brief's suggested numbers; odometer about 2.5 times hours for the farm quads, 4 times for the sport ones). Hours run only while the engine does, at real speed. They use their own seeded stream (`seed ^ 0xd45b`), as do the clock (`0xc10c`) and the pod placement (`0x90d5`), so a seed keeps its old layout.
- Pod placement: the analogue is placed first among the toolbox and every quad, then the digital among the toolbox and the quads with no pod, so each lands anywhere equally likely.
- Odometer drums have four digits and tenths on the stock red and the pod, as in the mockups (the brief's text said five on the pod).
- Neutral is "ignition on, not in reverse, stopped, rpm below the clutch bite", so it lights while idling at a standstill, as the brief said.
- The pods sit on the dash's top (red, blue, loan, green), the crossbar pad (yellow, orange) or the bar clamp (kids), leaning back about 20 degrees whatever the dash's own tilt, and the kids' quad cancels its scale so the pod is full size. They stand above the dash, so they don't cover it, but they do cover a slice of the view ahead; I didn't try to lower them.
- Blaster: the real headlamp stays in the nose. Only the shell's back (lamp and key) is on the bars, so the yellow has two lamps' worth of shell; moving the lamp up was more modelling than the dash needed.
- The fuel tap's position (ON, RES, OFF) was on the old dashes and isn't on any now, which is as the brief wanted.
- No sound for fitting a pod (silent was allowed) and the girls don't remark on it yet.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless run (`.scratch/dashes/`) of every stock dash with the ignition on and each pod on the red, yellow and kids' quad, with no page errors. Looked at the close-ups and seat views. Not checked: the faces while moving (needle sweeps, lamp changes, the LCD line cycling), a pod being fitted through the toolbox panel by keyboard, or the dashes with the battery flat.

## Looking closely at the dash (2026-10-06)

- Hold `V` while riding at the controls to lean in: over 0.3 s your eyes move to 0.44 m in front of the middle of the dash (and the pod, if one's fitted, so both fit in view) and the field of view narrows from 75 to 42 degrees. Let go and it eases back. Turning to look back (`C`) wins over it, it doesn't work from the back seat, and the view is locked on the dash while it's held. `dashZoomFov`, `dashZoomDistance` and `dashZoomTime` are in the player params.
- On the kids' quad the distance isn't scaled down when a pod is fitted (the pod is full size) but is when it isn't (its dash is 0.65 scale).
- Checked once headless: red with the analogue pod (both dash and pod legible) and the kids' quad with its key switch. Not checked: the other quads, or the ride back out of the zoom at speed.

## Parking brake and torque rock (TODO 18)

- Agreed with you: Space toggles; with it on, W revs the engine and the wheels don't move; the girls set it themselves; blips rock the quad only when it is standing.
- `quad.parkBrake`: with a rider's input and the brake on, the wheel brake is `maxBrake`, the drive is cut (the existing "braking drops the drive" rule) and the quad is held still below `parkedHoldSpeed`. A girl taking the controls releases it. Getting off the front seat she pulls it on with `parkBrakeChance` (Amy 1, Jess 0.5). A toast says so when you mount a quad with it on. A dash light was not added; the toast is the only sign.
- `src/vibration/torqueRock.js` (pure, tested) spots a blip: the throttle rising by `torqueMinRise` 0.4 within `torqueWindow` 0.15 s, once until it falls back. The kick (`torqueGain` 1.2 rad/s, always the one way) goes to a new slow oscillator in the vibration model (3.2 Hz, damping 0.25) that adds to the body's roll (about 3 degrees at full) and to your head's (`torqueCamera` 0.4). It only fires with the engine running, the quad slower than 0.5 m/s, and the brake on or nobody at the controls, so pulling away never rocks it. A girl sat on it moves with the body, so she wobbles too.
- Jess's prank: a free girl with `rockPrankChance` (Jess 0.02 a second, Amy 0) within `rockPrankRange` 3.5 m of a quad her friend is sat on, running, holds its throttle for 0.4 s (so the same rock fires), at most every two minutes. Jess says `rockPrank`, Amy `rockPrankAmy`. No new state.
- Not checked in the running game: whether 3 degrees reads right, the brake holding on a slope, or how often Jess does it. Unit tests cover the blip detection, the sway and the lines.

## Stuck throttle when you get off revving

- Getting off a quad that is standing still (under the speed that makes you jump) with the throttle at `revHoldMin` 0.15 or more now leaves it revving at that throttle: `Quad.holdRev`, using the same ghost state as the runaway, so the throttle holds until it stalls or tips, you get on again or something switches it off. It uses the throttle value you had when you got off, so a blip of 0.6 stays at 0.6.
- It will pull away if it has gears to take it, like a stuck cable would. The girls treat it as a runaway because it is in the ghost state; I didn't look at what they make of it in play.
- Checked once headless by calling `holdRev(0.6)` on a running quad: throttle stayed 0.6 and rpm about 3,800 over three seconds. Not checked: getting off with the real key, or how the girls react.
- The parking brake stays on if it was on: the wheel brake only held for a rider's input before, so a stuck-throttle quad with the brake set crept off; it now holds for the ghost state too. Checked once headless: brake on, throttle held at 0.6, rpm about 2,600, moved 0 m in 12 s. Rev held without the brake still pulls away.

## The tractor (TODO 8)

Settled with Jamie before building: the item as written, in one go (the trailer, the girls driving it, neutral lights and gears jumping out, and creeping are 9, 10, 12, 14, 17 and 18); built on the quad physics as a new spec so it looks like a proper old tractor but reuses the vehicle code; the controls below; kyles's recordings for the sound; a few lines from the girls. Sitting on the back or the wheel arch is 11, so the girls don't ride it yet.

- **It's a unit, but not one of the girls' units.** `TRACTOR_SPEC` (`quadSpecs.js`) is built by `QuadUnit` (the spec's `tractor: true` picks the tractor's body, wheel layout and drivetrain). `game.units` has it, so you can ride, fuel, hear and wash it; `game.girlUnits` is the quads only, and every place the girls look at quads (driving, nicking, tea, warming, ownership, names, faults, the wear tally, the bench, the debug buttons for them) uses that. It isn't in the fleet pick, so it's there every game, parked in the yard at `LAYOUT.tractor` (-48.5, 70.5), with its own seeded idle, roughness, fuel and hours (`seed ^ 0x7ac70`). It gets no faults and no pods. The toolbox says it's too much for it; Jess's diesel mistake and the quads' petrol can't reach it.
- **Physics.** `QuadPhysics` takes a layout (wheels with their own radius and mount height, the chassis box), so the tractor has a 1.9 m wheelbase, 0.72 m rear and 0.42 m front wheels, rear wheel drive, 2,000 kg, 38 degrees of lock, and brakes about seven times the quads'. `Quad` takes a ground offset (0.8 m for the tractor, 0.36 for the quads) in place of the constant. No tip-over handling beyond the quads' `T`. The model (`tractorModel.js`, procedural like the quads, with a proxy box twice the size) has a bonnet, grille and lamps, a pan seat between the mudguards, a raked steering column, a drawbar, and the controls it animates: gear lever in its gate, hand throttle, clutch pedal, stop knob.
- **Starting.** A diesel with `compressionIgnition` (`engine/compression.js`, tested): no mixture or choke (`coldEnrichmentNeed` 0), and the catch chance depends on the glow plugs. `K` turns the key on and heats them at 1/`glowSeconds` (7 s) a second; the GLOW lamp lights once they're hot enough for the engine's temperature, which is at once above `glowWarmAbove` (0.6) and after the full seven seconds stone cold. Cranked on cold plugs it keeps `glowColdCatch` 4% of its chance (rising as they heat); once ready it catches within a second or two (`catchChance` 0.85). The plugs cool in 14 s. The lamp is lit when ready, as the item says ("wait for the light"), not lit while heating. The CHARGE lamp is lit with the key on and the engine not running. On catching it throws a cloud of black smoke (`catchBlack`), and snapping the throttle open gives a black burst (`burstBlack`, `throttleBurst` 1.7), both new smoke params. The exhaust flap (`exhaustFlap`) lifts a bit with each firing pulse and decays in about 70 ms, held part open by throttle, with a rattle on top.
- **Stopping.** The key only switches the dash and the plugs. With it off and the engine running, `keyPulled` is set: the dash goes dark and the engine carries on until `O` (the stop knob, `stopKnob()`), or it stalls or runs dry. Turned back on, it's simply on again. `tractorWatch.js` (tested) has a girl within 18 m laugh at it after 3 s with the key off and the engine running (Jess 70% of the time, Amy otherwise; once every 90 s).
- **Controls.** `Shift` clutch, `1` `2` `3` gears, `B` reverse, `0` neutral, `Q` / `Z` hand throttle up and down (0.35 a second, it stays where it is, even after you get off), `W` foot throttle, `S` brake, `O` stop knob (moved from `X` later, see "Tractor odometer, hand throttle, glow lamp"). The lever only moves with the clutch down (`changeClutch` 0.7), except neutral, which can always be found, so you can always take it out of gear to get off. The starter won't turn in gear with the clutch up (a note says so). `B` and `X` were free while riding (`V` is the dash lean, and `X` is only the winch when looking at the green), and the quads' choke keys are the hand throttle here. A readout at the bottom of the screen gives the gear, clutch and hand throttle, since the levers aren't on the dash.
- **Gearbox** (`manualDrivetrain.js`, tested, same interface as the quads' CVT drivetrain so nothing else cares). Top speeds 2.4, 4.8 and 8.5 m/s in the three gears, reverse 2.6; drive force 100%, 68% and 46% of 5,200 N; the clutch pedal goes down at 8 a second and comes up at 1.6 a second (about 0.6 s), because it's a key. Pulling away from rest drags the engine by `launchLoad` 0.25, 0.5 and 0.95 for the three gears, times the clutch bite, falling away with speed, and 90% cancelled by full throttle. That goes in as the engine's drivetrain load, with `loadDragRpm` 3,200, so third from rest with the throttle shut stalls it and first never does (`tests/tractorDriving.test.js` runs the real engine and gearbox to check: first never stalls, third at 0 stalls, third at 0.6 throttle and second at 0.25 don't). Braking while in gear loads the engine the way it does the quads, so stopping without the clutch stalls it. The gear speeds are caps on the drive, not tied to engine revs: in first at full throttle the engine still revs to the top. All the numbers are in the tuning panel under Tractor/Gearbox.
- **Dash.** A new `tractor` dash style: a rev counter (0 to 3,000 rpm with a green band at 1,400 to 1,900, which reads with the key off, as a cable-driven one would), an hour meter, a small fuel gauge, GLOW and CHARGE lamps and the key, on the scuttle tilted up at you. No pod fits it.
- **Fuel.** 45 litres of red diesel (`startDiesel` 1; the engine's `runsOnDiesel` stops it soaking it up), 12 to 34 L at the start. Fill it from the dark green can like a quad. Petrol or pre-mix in it isn't modelled (it just takes it, and Amy says nothing).
- **Sound** (`engine-bank-tractor`, made by `tools/slice_engine_audio.py engine-bank-tractor`): kyles's 637890 (steady running 14.8 to 30.6 s for the idle, the driving stretches 34 to 60.5 s for revs, shutdown 61.4 to 63.6 s) and 637891 (a second idle at 6 to 11 s, starter and cranking at 1.1 to 2.9 s). 109 idle grains between 716 and 1,428 rpm by the pulse-rate measure, 64 accel and 34 decel. The engine uses the 'pulse' sound (every firing plays a recorded idle firing at its own speed, so revs are firings coming faster), idle 760 to 840 rpm, top 2,200. 637892 wasn't used (mostly passes with Doppler).
- **Lines.** `tractorLines.js`: `tractorKey` (the key that doesn't stop it), `tractorGlow` (a girl says something once you've waited 2.5 s for the plugs: Amy "Wait for the glow light first", Jess "Come on, come on") and `tractorCaught` (it catching, 70% of the time). Three situations, three lines each, for both of them, re-recorded.
- **Checked:** unit tests (compression 11, gearbox 16, tractor driving 7, watch 9), the game loads with no page errors, the tractor stands in the yard and is the right way round, and a headless run in the seat: cold start (the starter in gear refused with its note, the lamp coming on at seven seconds, a start with black smoke), pulling away in first, second and third, braking in gear stalling it, a stop knob stopping it, the key off with the engine staying on, the dash by eye with the lean-in key, the bank loading and being given to the tractor's audio. **Not checked:** how it sounds (in particular the pitch and loudness at 2,200 rpm, since the recordings only reach about 1,400 by pulse rate, and the starter loop), how it handles on a slope, in the bogs or against the winch (it's heavy: 2,000 kg against 300), the girls' lines in play, the exhaust smoke colour against fog or rain, or the shake from the vibration block, which I took from the green quad without trying. Getting off leaves the hand throttle where it was, with the parking brake holding it, which is 18's starting point. Left alone: the girls and the tractor (11, 14), a trailer (47), and neutral lights and jumping out of gear (17).

### The tractor, after first feedback

- Idle: no hunting, wandering, stumbling or misfiring (`roughHunt`, `erraticHunt`, `huntRpm`, `stumbleRate`, `misfireBase` all 0 in its engine block) and a roughness of 0.05 to 0.08, so a warm or cold idle holds exactly its idle rpm (a test checks the spread is under 5 rpm over 30 s). The beat is still very slightly uneven (`cycleJitter` 0.03).
- Rear wheels moved out to 0.88 m and the mudguards made wider (0.58 m) and bigger (0.17 m clear of the tyre) so the wheels, with the body shaking, no longer poke through them.
- Wheels shake with the body (`wheelShake` 1.3 on the spec): each wheel takes the body's heave, pitch and roll at its own position. Only the tractor does it.
- Your arms aren't drawn on the tractor (the empty `riderHands` group is only there because `Quad` wants one).
- Dash redone as a black binnacle with three chrome-bezelled round dials: water temperature, the rev counter (hour meter inside, PTO speed marked) and fuel, with GLOW, CHARGE and OIL lamps (the last two lit with the key on and the engine stopped) and the key.
- Help panel trimmed to about two thirds of its length (it now also scrolls if the window is still too small).
- Not checked: how the wheel shake looks in motion.

### The tractor, second round

- No fuel tap: `noTap` in its fuel params (the whole tank is always reachable, no reserve, `F` does nothing and isn't in its prompt or help, and the tap part on the model is hidden because `Quad` still poses one).
- Idle 430 to 470 rpm (spec `idleRpmWarm` 450, `stallRpm` 280). `loadDragRpm` came down from 3,200 to 1,400 to match, or first gear would stall it from rest: the tests still say first never stalls, second stalls with no throttle but not with a little, third wants throttle.
- Hand throttle: `handFloor` 0.22 in the gearbox params. At 0 it's idle; the first notch jumps to 22% throttle (about 840 rpm) and the rest of the travel goes from there to full. Checked headless: idle 463, one tap of `Q` (a tenth of the travel) gave 988 rpm. If you meant something other than this by "starts somewhere more sensible", say.

### The tractor, third round

- The hand throttle goes back to plain steps (0.35 of its travel a second, linear from idle to full); `handFloor` is gone. Instead it's left at 0.15 when the game starts (`handStart` in the gearbox params), about 710 rpm, so the tractor doesn't spawn with the lever at the bottom.
- Much more black smoke: a steady dark plume even at idle (`warmDensity` 0.5, `warmGray` 0.22), a thick black column whenever it's working (new `throttleDensity` 1.4 and `throttleBlack` 0.8 smoke params), a huge burst on a blip (`throttleBurst` 4), up to 15 puffs when it catches, opacity 2.2, puffs lasting up to 7 s and growing to 3.6 m. Looked at once headless with the hand throttle up: a dense black column. Not checked: how it looks in fog or rain, or that it isn't too much from the seat.


## Manual quads (TODO 9)

Settled with Jamie before building: the red, green and yellow (the yellow so there is one two-stroke manual); four forward gears and reverse, no hand throttle; the tractor's controls (`Shift` clutch, `1` to `4`, `0` neutral, `B` reverse; `Q` / `Z` stay the choke); the girls shift for themselves and leave it in neutral on getting off; a ridden quad you leave moving or revving keeps its gear; the dashes say N, D or R. (The first version also put the gear and clutch in a readout at the bottom of the screen; it came out again, see below.)

- **One gearbox, two users.** `ManualDrivetrain` gained a fourth gear (`gears` 3 for the tractor, 4 for the quads, `topSpeed4`, `torque4`, `launchLoad4`) and `Quad` split `tractor` (hand throttle, stop knob, its model's levers) from `manual` (clutch and gears). A gear's top speed is the lower of its ratio and the quad's `maxSpeed`, so the tuning panel and the bench's limiter still limit the top gear. The two-stroke's power band (`powerbandRpm`) now shapes the manual's torque too, since the CVT drivetrain's torque curve isn't used.
- **Ratios** (m/s, per spec): red 4.5, 8.5, 12.5, 17; green 4, 7.5, 11, 15 (and the gentlest launch load); yellow 5, 9.5, 14.5, 20. Drive force 100%, 85%, 70% and 60% of the quad's maximum (green 100, 90, 75, 65), reverse 3 to 3.5 m/s. Launch load is lowest in first, so first never stalls from rest with no throttle (a test runs the real engines for all three), and fourth stalls without a lot of throttle. Engine revs still aren't tied to the road speed, as on the tractor, so the revs stay up through the changes.
- **Controls.** On a manual quad `W` is only throttle and `S` only brake; which way it goes is the lever's (`reverse` follows the gear). The starter interlock is the tractor's. The choke, fuel tap and pull-start keys are as before, and the girls can crank one by taking it out of gear themselves.
- **The girls** (`autoShifter.js`, pure, tested): from throttle, brake, speed and whether she wants reverse she picks a gear (up at 85% of a gear's top speed with the throttle open, down below 70% of the gear below's), holds the clutch down for 0.4 s for each change with the lever moving part way through, and dips the clutch when stopped or braking slow so it doesn't stall. She never crunches a gear or stalls it; that is for later (the other skills, 33). She goes into reverse only once stopped. Her tunables are under "Her gear changes" in each quad's tuning folder.
- **Getting off.** When she stops driving, the quad goes to neutral with the clutch up. When you get off, nothing changes: still moving or revving it carries on in the gear it had (the runaway and stuck throttle are as before); stopped, it is simply left in gear, as the tractor is.
- **Dashes.** The LCDs showed H for forwards; they now show D (the loan quad's column reads D, L, N, R, P), and the pod shows D as well as N and R. The lamp dashes already had R and N lamps, now driven by the lever on a manual quad.
- **Checked:** unit tests (gearbox ratios, no stall in first on all three, her shifter) and once headless with a stand-in driver on each of the red, yellow and green: starts in neutral, goes 1 to 4 as it speeds up, drops to first and holds the clutch down as it stops on the brake, and is in neutral when the driver is removed. Also the status readout and keys on the red from the seat. **Not checked:** a real girl driving one (my attempts had her walk to it and never get there in the time), the red from the seat at speed, how the shifts feel, the sound through a change, the gear letter on the dashes by eye, or a manual quad on the steep bank.

### The tractor's tickover, evened out

Jamie: it didn't sound even and thumpy like the recording. Measured and fixed; idle kept at 450 rpm (it starts with the hand throttle a little open, about 710).

- **What the recording is.** kyles's tractor is a four-cylinder at about 1,000 rpm: firings at 33 a second in a cycle that repeats every 0.121 s, one cylinder louder than the rest (autocorrelation of the envelope 0.54 at one cycle).
- **What went wrong.** The slicer followed single beats and landed on whichever cylinder was loudest nearby, so idle "cycles" came out 0.084 to 0.168 s (three, four or five firings) and started on different cylinders. The pulse sound then played a random one each cycle, stretched by a different amount each time, so the loud beat moved about and the pitch wobbled. At 450 rpm it picked from the eight longest, the worst cut. Rendered offline through the real worklet, the regularity at its own cycle was -0.06 at idle and -0.01 at about 710 rpm, against the recording's 0.54.
- **The fix.** `idleCycles` in the bank's config: `whole_cycle_cuts()` cuts the idle into whole cycles, each placed where the next cycle best matches the average cycle and then moved to 6 ms before its strongest beat (114 to 129 ms long; anything over 6% off its neighbours is marked not ok). The bank gets `readThrough`, and the worklet's pulse sound then plays idle grains in recording order, jumping only at a not-ok grain or the end of a stretch. Re-cutting alone or reading in order alone each did little (0.16 and 0.15 at idle); together, built: 0.45 at idle, 0.68 at 710 rpm and 0.82 at 1,325, as loud as before within about 10%. The rev grains are unchanged (the pulse sound doesn't use them). The bank's WAV is unchanged; only its index moved.
- **Shake.** The vibration model took one kick per engine cycle, a single's thump: 3.75 a second at 450 rpm, ringing the body at its own 6.5 Hz. New `firingsPerCycle` (vibration params) spreads each pulse into that many kicks evenly through its period, carrying the late ones into the next update; the tractor has 4, so it shudders at 15 Hz at idle. Its shake now eases off above 600 rpm (`idleRpm` 600, `smoothRpmRange` 1,000), or the four-fold kicks held the body about 10 mm off centre at 1,300 rpm. By the numbers at idle: 1.6 mm of shake at 15 Hz (was 2.6 mm at 7.5 Hz) and more in the bars (1.7 mm from 0.6).
- **Checked:** unit tests (whole cycles in the bank and `readThrough`; four even kicks a cycle across updates), and offline renders (`.scratch/tractor-sound/`, untracked: the recording, the game before, the prototypes and the built version). **Not checked:** how it sounds in the running game alongside the quads, or how the 15 Hz shudder looks at 60 frames a second.

### Manual quads, after first feedback

- **They stall when slowed in a high gear.** New `lugSpeed` in the gearbox params (0.6 on the three quads, 1 on the tractor, which behaves as before): the drag on the engine now carries on up to 60% of the gear's top speed rather than only from rest, and braking with the clutch out only loads the engine below that speed (so a hard stop from speed doesn't stall it until it has slowed right down). Launch loads are now 0.1 to 0.15 in first (it never stalls from rest, or at any speed) and 0.6 to 0.7, 0.85 to 0.9 and 1 in second, third and fourth. The old engines shrugged this off, so the three specs also got a much bigger `loadDragRpm`: red 9,000, green 7,500, yellow 32,000 (a two-stroke idling at 1,800 takes a lot of dragging down to its 350 stall). Throttle held open saves it (relief 90%).
- Measured with the real engines, clutch out, a held speed: in third and fourth at 15% of the gear's top speed with no throttle, all three stall. Second stalls on the yellow, red and green at rest. Fourth at half its top speed doesn't stall. The yellow is the most stally (fourth at 30% of top speed too), the red and green need to be a lot lower. Left alone: how it feels to ride with real keys, and whether the red's second at rest is too easy to stall for pulling away (it only does it with no throttle at all).
- The girls' shifter is unchanged: she downshifts before the lug region is deep and dips the clutch when braking slow, so she doesn't stall (checked again headless on all three: 1 to 4 and back to first with no stalls).
- `tests/quadCharacter.test.js` applies a full braking load to every spec to check nothing stalls on hard stops; the manual quads are now excluded from that, since stalling there is the point.
- **No readout.** The gear and clutch line at the bottom of the screen is gone, for the manual quads and then the tractor too (you go by feel; the tractor's hand throttle setting is only on its lever). This supersedes the tractor readout described under TODO 8.


## Tractor to the rescue (TODO 12)

- **The chain** (`src/items/chain.js`, `chainPull` pure and tested): same pattern as the green's winch, but with no reel. `X` looking at the tractor on foot takes it off the drawbar pin (1.5 m behind its middle), `E` on a quad hooks it to the end nearer the tractor, `E` on that quad unhooks it. It is a stiff spring (40 kN/m, at most 28 kN against the winch's 6.5 kN) that only pulls once the slack set at hooking is used up, so you back the tractor up close and drive off. The hook only reaches 5 m from the drawbar, otherwise it pulls out of your hand. The hooked quad has its brakes off (`towed`), which is what makes towing a dead one home work. A quarter of the pull drags back on the tractor (`reaction`); at 2,000 kg it barely notices. The trailer can't be hitched while the chain is out, and the chain can't be taken while it is.
- **Sunk too deep for the winch.** The winch could pull any quad out (6.5 kN against 3.2 kN of suction at most), so nothing was too deep for it. The bog's hold now climbs from sink 0.8 (`deepAt`) to a further 2.8 times the quad's weight at full sink (`deepSuction`): about 11 kN for a 290 kg quad, above the winch and well under the chain. Below 0.8 nothing changes, so the earlier bog tuning stands (a test checks the ordering winch < deep hold < chain).
- **Checked:** unit tests; headless, a red quad forced to sink 0.85 in the field bog: the chain, dragged by the tractor at 1.5 m/s, pulled it clear in about 6 s. **Not checked:** the winch failing on a deep quad (my headless run didn't make the winch pull at all, so that run proves nothing about it), driving the tractor with real controls against it, how the chain looks, towing a quad across rough ground or uphill, or whether sink really reaches 0.8 and over in play (wheelspin has to dig a long time; sitting in a bog only settles it slowly). Left alone: the girls using the chain, and any lines about it.

## Neutral and jumping out of gear (TODO 17)

Not asked before building: the item as written. Jamie, part way through: no neutral light on the tractor (you can see the lever); the quads' dashes already light N from the lever, so nothing was needed there.

- **False neutral** (`manualDrivetrain.js`). `pedalDown` counts how long the pedal has been down far enough to change gear (0.7). A change made before `rushTime` (0.2 s) has a `falseNeutralChance` (45%) of missing the gear: the lever drops to neutral, `select` returns `'missed'` and the player gets a note. Holding Shift for a moment first is always clean. The girls' shifter passes `careful`, so she never misses (clumsy novice shifting stays with the other skills, 33). Pressing the clutch and the number together, which is what most people do, is the rushed case, so expect to miss now and then until you learn to hold it.
- **Worn gearbox.** `pickWornGearbox` (seeded, `seed ^ 0x6ea4`, so `?seed=` repeats it) picks one manual vehicle from the fleet's manual quads and the tractor, one of its forward gears above first (first is for climbing the bank, so it would be unfair there) and a wear of 0.5 to 1. In that gear, with the clutch engaged, throttle at least `jumpThrottle` (0.5) and the nose up by at least `jumpSlope` (sine 0.1, about 6 degrees), it jumps out at `jumpRate` (0.6) a second times wear times how much steeper than the minimum (up to double). So a fully worn gear on a 12 degree bank goes in about a second, a half worn one in two to three. It goes to neutral, with the note "It jumped out of gear". Level ground and the clutch down never trigger it. The game logs which one at the start.
- **Checked:** unit tests (rushed and clean changes, careful driver, timer reset, the jump and its conditions, rate against wear, the pick). **Not checked:** how it feels on the real keys (the 0.2 s and 45% are guesses and may be too harsh), a worn gear on the actual bank, or a girl driving a worn one (she'd just reselect, but I haven't watched it). Left alone: no girl line when it happens, nothing at the bench to fix it, and nothing tells you which vehicle is worn.

## Creeping in low (TODO 18)

Settled with Jamie: a straight line at a pace the hand throttle sets, working all the way below walking pace, built on the same idea as the quads that carry on after you jump off; no bale throwing (too much for now, so no loose bales and nothing for the girls to load); a girl notices it, runs after it and stops it, but sometimes it reaches the hedge; works with or without the trailer.

- **When it creeps** (`vehicle/creep.js`, pure, tested in `tests/creep.test.js`): the tractor, running, nobody on it or driving it, in first gear, hand throttle at `minHand` 0.05 or more, parking brake off, upright, not on the winch. Neutral, second and reverse never creep, so leaving it in neutral (what the girls do when they get off) still parks it. Before this the parking brake held an unattended tractor whatever the throttle said; `Quad.fixedUpdate` now releases it while creeping.
- **How fast**: `creepSpeed` is the hand setting times `speedPerHand` 2.4 m/s (first gear's top speed), never under `minSpeed` 0.15. At the start setting of 0.15 that is 0.36 m/s, about 1.3 km/h; a girl walks at 1.3 m/s, so she catches it easily. It is done by a `speedLimit` argument to `ManualDrivetrain.update` that lowers the gear's top speed (the drive fades to nothing as it nears it), not by braking: braking while coupled stalls the tractor. The throttle itself is still the hand setting, so the engine note follows the lever as before. I haven't measured how closely it settles on the limit in the running game.
- **Against the hedge**: held under `stuckSpeed` 0.1 m/s for `stuckTime` 4 s while creeping, it stops itself with the stop knob (so a girl who finds it finds it stalled, with the throttle still open).
- **The girls** (`npc/tractorRunaway.js`, pure, tested; lines in `tractorRunawayLines.js`): after `noteDelay` 4 s of creeping the nearest free girl within `noteRange` 30 m remarks on it, once. `distanceToHedge` ray-casts along its heading to the field square (`fieldHalf` less the hedge thickness). At `noticeDistance` 18 m from it, plus or minus up to `noticeSpread` 8 m, the nearest free girl (Jess about half the time when it's close) runs after it at 2.4 times walking speed. Within 2.4 m she shuts the throttle, puts it in neutral and pulls the stop knob; there is no climbing-on animation. `missChance` 0.25 a time, nobody is looking and nobody goes, and when it stalls the nearest girl says so and the other blames her two seconds later. Everything is in the tuning panel under "Tractor creep" and "Tractor runaway".
- **Checked:** unit tests only (the rules, the speed, the stall timer, the hedge distance, the events, the drive cut-off at the limit). **Not checked:** it running in the game: the real speed the tractor settles at, the girl's run, or the hedge stall.


## Horns (TODO 3)

Item 3; the item settled what happens, so I chose the key and numbers without asking.

- **Key:** hold `L` (free, and `H` is help). It works on any quad and the tractor, with the engine off too. Held down, the horn sounds; the sound is built the first time a horn is used (`setHorn` in `rideAudio.js`) and goes through the quad's own sound path, so a girl's beep has distance, Doppler and the barn's echo like her engine.
- **Voices:** `hornVoice` in `src/npc/horns.js`. Quads: two buzzy sawtooth tones near 620 and 780 Hz, quiet (0.22), low-passed at 2.6 kHz, with a 95 Hz wobble on the level; each quad's pitch is shifted up to 10% by its id so they don't all match. The tractor: 196 and 247 Hz, 0.55, less buzz. A spec can give its own `horn`. `hornVolume` sits in the Sound tuning.
- **Answers:** a fresh press (quiet for 0.15 s first) within 35 m of a girl sat on or driving a quad makes her beep back after 0.4 to 1.1 s, one or two beeps, then she won't answer again for 3 s. Jess always answers, Amy 60% of the time. Holding the button down is one beep, not a stream of them.
- **Creeping up:** Jess driving between 2 and 9 m behind Amy's rear arc (dot product 0.7 or more), with Amy driving at 2 m/s or more, has a 15% chance per second of blasting the horn for 0.7 s. First one no earlier than 120 s into a game, then 240 s apart. Amy "jumps" 0.12 s in, which is only her line (`hornJump`) for now: her quad doesn't swerve. The tunables are under "Horns".
- **Not done:** the quirk for a horn that works when it likes is item 6. No battery cost for the horn.
- **Checked:** unit tests only (answers, ranges, creep-up geometry and timing, voices, lines). **Not checked:** what the horns sound like in the running game, or how often Jess gets into position.

## Gear on the digital pods

A digital pod fitted to a manual quad (red, green, yellow) showed "D" when in gear. Both digital pods now show the real gear (R, N, 1 to 4) from the dash state, and keep R, N and D on the automatics (`gearShown` in `src/vehicle/dashState.js`, unit tested). Not checked by eye in the running game.

## A history (TODO 5)

Settled with Jamie: the hour meters and odometers already exist (every quad has them whether or not a pod is fitted), so this item only seeds the rest. Starting wear is a per-quad range, faults at the start are fairly random, one to two of them.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/history.js` (tests in `tests/history.test.js`), seeded from `seed ^ 0x4157` so `?seed=` repeats it. `game.js` `applyHistory` applies it after the usage readings, so it doesn't disturb the layout or the other seeded streams.
- Wear: each spec has a `wearRange` (red 0.1 to 0.35, blue 0.08 to 0.3, yellow 0.1 to 0.35, green 0.25 to 0.55, orange 0.04 to 0.2, kids 0.02 to 0.12, loan 0 to 0.04, all under the 0.8 where an engine turns erratic). Seventy per cent of where it lands follows where the quad's hour meter sits in its range, the rest is luck. The wear ledger counts it as nobody's, as before. The tractor has none yet.
- Faults: one roll per game picks the fleet's chance of a starting fault between 0.15 and 0.6, so some games are mostly sound and some a mess. Each quad then rolls against it, gets one fault (a second, different one 25% of the time) at a level of 0.15 to 0.55, using `Faults.leave`. They behave and get fixed like the ones that develop.
- Tinkering: a quarter of quads (not the kids') come with the throttle stop out 1 to 3 turns, the mixture 1 to 3 clicks rich, or both. It is applied through the toolbox's own `applyTune`, and `unit.leftovers` seeds the workshop, so opening the toolbox shows the quad as it is with stock meaning the factory settings, and Reset undoes it.
- Who had it out: 10% nobody, otherwise Jess about half the time and Amy the rest.
- What the girls know: each girl settles once per quad what she believes, right with her `diagnoseAccuracy` (Amy 0.9, Jess 0.5). A wrong rider swaps to the other girl; a missed fault or tinkering is simply never mentioned. Stood by a quad, `watchHistory` brings up one topic at a time (90 s apart, each once per quad, `historyChance` 0.02 a second) and only while it is still true: a fixed fault or put-back stop isn't mentioned. Lines are in `historyLines.js`, written for the colour and each nickname like the other quad lines.
- The `DEFAULT_HISTORY_PARAMS` apply once at the start of a game, so they aren't in the live tuning panel (`HISTORY_PARAM_RANGES` is there for when a debug "new game" exists).
- Meters: checked in the code that the hours (engine running), odometer (distance) and trip all advance in `Quad.updateDash` for every unit every frame, ridden or not. The debug panel's engine folder now shows hours, odometer and trip for the quad you're on or looking at. The history itself is too long for the panel, so it is only logged to the console at the start of a game (in the per-vehicle block below), until the console item (TODO 57) can query it.
- Not checked in the running game: how the girls' history lines land, whether the starting faults feel right on a first ride, or that the debug readouts move (unit tests and a production build only). The voice banks needed re-recording for about 340 new lines.

## Tractor odometer, hand throttle, glow lamp, stop knob key

- Odometer: the tractor now starts with 5,000 to 15,000 miles (`odoRange`), pulled towards where its hours sit in 3,500 to 7,500 (`pickRelatedReading`, the same 70% pull as quad wear). Its dash has no odometer, so it is only in the debug panel; a pod could show it if one is ever fitted to the tractor (not done: the pods only go on the quads for now).
- Hand throttle: left anywhere from 0.05 to 0.45 each game (`handStartRange`, seeded with the tractor's other variation), where it was a fixed 0.15. The gearbox's `handStart` stays as the fallback.
- Glow lamp: the real way round. It is lit while the plugs heat and goes out when they're ready, and stays out on a warm engine, a running one or with the key off. `EngineModel.glowLit` is the lamp; what was `glowLamp` ("plugs ready") is now `glowHot`, which the girls' starting and the waiting remarks use. HUD help and Amy's `tractorGlow` lines say "wait for the light to go out" (those three lines need re-recording).
- Stop knob: `O` instead of `X`, which is the tow chain and the winch. I didn't use `C` because it holds the look-back view while riding, and you pull the knob while riding. `O` was free in the game (the toolbox uses it only with the panel open).

## Quirks (TODO 6)

Settled with Jamie: build all ten, one to three per quad from the seed, only on quads with the part, the girls warn from what they know, and nobody fixes them (the stiff tap frees up with use).

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/quirks.js` (tests in `tests/quirks.test.js`; `QuirkSet` per quad, `quad.quirks`; tunables under "Quirks" in the tuning panel, live, since they are read every frame). Picked from `seed ^ 0x9a1c` after the pods are placed. One quirk 50% of the time, two 35%, three 15%, never both fuel gauge ones. The debug panel's engine folder lists each quad's quirks; the console prints them with the rest of the start values (below).
- Where they can land (`featuresOf`): the two fuel gauge quirks and the cold needle only on a quad whose dash shows them (the blue and loan quads' LCDs) or that starts with a pod fitted. The senders live on the quad, so a pod moved onto another quad later reads that quad's lies, but the quirk isn't redealt. The tractor has none for now (it has no choke or tap, and the item is about the quads). The kids' quad has no split seat.
- Gauges (`QuirkSet.dash`, applied to the dash state in `Quad.updateDash`, so the dashes, pods and the girls reading the lamp all see the lie): reads high is level to the power 0.6 (a quarter tank reads 44%, and empty and full are right, so the lamp comes late); sticks holds a value picked at the start (0.2 to 0.9) and, moving, has a 1% a second chance of a knock that jumps it to the real level before it sticks again; the cold needle is capped at engine temperature 0.12 with no red zone.
- Choke: with it running, shake (the vibration model's intensity, 0..1) adds a step of the lever each 4 s at full shake, up to 0.5, so a quad shaking hard gets noticeably choked in a minute or two.
- Tap: needs three presses of `F`, each within 2 s of the last, and each turn takes 0.35 off the stiffness, so presses needed run 3, 2, 2, 1 (it rounds). A note says "The fuel tap is stiff: keep working it". The girls' own tap turning isn't affected. The note channel (`quad.say`) used to show only for the manual quads; it now shows for all.
- Seat: rain over 0.25 outside cover adds 0.08 a second of wet and it dries at 0.0015 a second. Sitting on it at over 0.3 soaks you (a toast and the nearest girl within 10 m reacts, `seatSoaked`) and takes 0.3 off the wet, so it doesn't repeat every time you get on. Girls' own seats are not modelled.
- Horn: pressing rolls whether it works (55%); a working one cuts out at 0.7 a second and a dead one splutters in at 0.9 a second while held. The girls only answer a beep they actually hear. Applies to the girls' beeps on that quad too.
- Brakes: full braking pulls the bars 0.05 rad left (the same channel as a puncture's pull, so the two add up). The squeal is a pair of sawtooth whistles through a narrow band-pass, wobbling a little and falling in pitch as it slows (built the first time it's needed, in `rideAudio.js`), when braking over 1.5 m/s.
- Headlight: the lens dims by up to 85% for about 0.12 s every 1 to 4 s on average 2.5 s. There's no light cast, only the lens glow, as for the lights today.
- The girls: `watchQuirks` in `companionVoice.js`, like the history. Each settles per quad what she believes with her `diagnoseAccuracy` (Amy 0.9, Jess 0.5), and with probability (1 - accuracy) / 2 is convinced of one the quad hasn't got. She warns once per quirk, a minute apart, stood by it or within 10 m as you get on it (three times as likely). Lines in `quirkLines.js` say "that one" rather than the colour, so there are only 22 of them.
- Not checked in the running game: how any of it feels or sounds (the squeal and the horn's stutter are first guesses), the choke creep rate, how often it rains onto an open seat in a real game, or the lines. Unit tests and a production build only. Re-record the voice banks for the 22 new lines.

## Tyres (TODO 7)

Settled with Jamie: per-wheel pressures, per-axle brand and tread, found out by the gauge and by feel, a puncture shows on the gauge, no swapping tyres, no new girl lines.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/tyres.js` (`TyreSet`, tests in `tests/tyres.test.js`; tunables under "Tyres" in the tuning panel, read when the tyres are dealt). Dealt from `seed ^ 0x7e55` in `game.js`. Wheel order is the physics': FL, FR, RL, RR.
- Pressure: stock 4 psi plus a shared bias of up to 0.5 either way and up to 0.4 each wheel; one wheel in four games is a further 0.8 to 1.6 out. Rounded to 0.1, between 1.5 and 7.
- Brands per axle, matched 40% of the time: Trackmaster (grass 1, mud 1), Mudclaw (grass 0.93, mud 1.2) and Roadrunner (grass 1.06, mud 0.88). Tread per axle, 0.6 to 1 at the front; the rear 0.55 to 1, or bald (0.2 to 0.4) one game in four. No tread costs up to 15% grip on grass and the track and up to 40% in mud and puddles.
- Where it acts: `QuadPhysics.applyControls` multiplies each wheel's grip by `TyreSet.grip(i, mud, puddle)` (pressure, brand, tread, blending to the mud values by how muddy or puddled the ground is). Stiffness is set per wheel from its pressure in `applyParams`, and rolling resistance from the mean pressure. The psi effects that used to be applied to the whole quad's params in `applyTune` moved here, with the same constants (`psiGrip`, `psiMudGrip`, `psiRolling`, `psiStiffness` still live in the toolbox tunables). The one thing that no longer follows pressure is the engine load's fallback grip estimate (`tyreGrip` and `mudGrip` in `engineLoad.js`), which is only used without the per-wheel info.
- The toolbox: the Tyres row's value is the four gauge readings; the setting is still the stock 4.0 psi and moving it pumps all four to that value (so it also levels them). Reset to stock levels them to 4.0. `T` (pump and patch) and the bench tyre job pump them up too, to the toolbox setting or to stock respectively, whether or not there was a puncture. A puncture takes up to 75% off its wheel's gauge reading as it goes flat, but not off its real pressure, so the grip doesn't improve when a tyre goes flat (the puncture's own pull and drag still apply).
- Debug panel: a Tyres folder for the quad you are on or looking at (the four pressures, draggable, plus the gauge, brands, treads and per-wheel grip on grass and in mud), and the console block per vehicle now has a tyres line.
- Worked out from the averages, not measured in play: a quad's mud grip comes out about 8% down on before and its grass grip about 4% down, with a wide spread between quads (a bald rear in mud is about 30% down). Not checked in the running game: how any of it feels, how visible the pull from uneven pressures is, or whether the bogs got harder. All the values are first guesses.

## The handbrake (TODO 16)

Settled with Jamie, all as recommended: only empty quads lose the automatic hold, every quad starts braked, the existing rates for the girls, no rescue, blame from a real tally, "it" lines, brake state in the debug panel and console.

- `Quad.update`'s `idle` (the hold that applies `parkingBrake` and `setParked`) now reads: with someone at the controls (rider's input, a ghost carrying on, a driver or the tractor's creep), stopped with no throttle, as before; with nobody, only if `parkBrake` is set, or it is the tractor. Before, any stationary quad held itself. An empty quad with the brake off has only rolling resistance and the physics' damping, so it stays on the flat and rolls on a slope as far as the ground lets it.
- Every quad starts with `parkBrake` on (the tractor too, harmlessly). Amy's `parkBrakeChance` is 0.9 now (it was 1), Jess's stays 0.5. When you get off a quad without the brake, a one-off toast says `Space` sets it. Getting off at speed leaves the brake as it was and counts as not braked.
- `src/vehicle/handbrake.js` (pure, tests in `tests/handbrake.test.js`, tunables under "Handbrake"): `RollWatch` calls an empty, unbraked quad that goes faster than 0.5 m/s rolling away, once until it has been nearly still for 3 s. `quad.lastLeft` (`{ who, braked }`, set when you or a girl get off the front seat) says whose fault it is; no `lastLeft`, or one who braked, blames nobody. A tally per person is kept. Pillion girls count as attending.
- Reactions: the girls within 18 m (not driving) respond through `rollReactions`. Blamed on you, the nearest says `handbrakeYou`; on a girl, her friend says `handbrakeJess` or `handbrakeAmy`, and 2.5 s later she owns up (`handbrakeOwn`) if she's near; if only she's near she owns up at once; no known culprit, the nearest says `handbrakeRoll`. Nothing physical happens (the girls don't chase it), so once it stops in the hedge or bog the existing stuck behaviour applies. `handbrakeLines.js`: each girl has the four lines she can use, two each (Amy cross then sorry, Jess gleeful then not sorry).
- Debug and console: each quad's `parkingBrake` line in the engine folder (on or off and who last left it), and a `parking brake` line in the start block.
- Not checked in the running game: how far and fast a quad actually rolls on the bank and the yard slopes (the physics decides), whether the starts are on slopes steep enough for the brake to matter, or the girls' lines. Unit tests and a production build only.

## Headlights that follow the revs (TODO 19)

- `src/vehicle/headlight.js` (pure, tests in `tests/headlight.test.js`; tunables under "Headlights" in the tuning panel, shared by every quad): `headlightLook` gives a brightness (0..1) and colour from the engine each frame, replacing the old on-with-the-key lens. Running: 0.3 at idle rising to full by 55% of the way up the rev range, from a yellow (0xffb347) to a near white (0xfff2cc). Cranking and not yet running: 0.15 and yellow. Stalled with the key on: out (a magneto has nothing to give). Key off: out.
- Flicker: from engine roughness plus erratic over 0.2, up to 0.6 above that, the light dips by up to 70% with two wobbles at different rates, so it stutters rather than pulses. It multiplies with the flickering-headlight quirk's dips.
- Steady: specs with `steadyLights: true` (the loan quad, and the tractor) stay full and white whenever the key is on.
