A real beam would be its own item. Not checked in the running game: how the dimness reads in daylight (it may be hard to see at idle in the sun) or the flicker rate.

## Standing still on a slope (TODO 46)

- Cause, found with a scratch Rapier run (the player's character controller settings on a heightfield slope, no input, 120 Hz): while grounded the player asks for a downward speed of 1 m/s each step to stay on the ground, and Rapier slides the controller a little along the slope in answer. It is a steady creep, about 2 mm/s at 10 degrees, 6 mm/s at 30 and 9 mm/s at 50, so 0.1 to 0.5 m over a minute standing there.
- Fix: `src/player/standing.js` (pure, tested): grounded, no movement key down, not stumbling and under 0.05 m/s horizontally counts as standing still. Then the horizontal speed is zeroed, no downward push is asked for, and any horizontal movement the controller computes is thrown away (the height still follows the ground). The same run with that gave no drift on any slope from 10 to 55 degrees. Walking, jogging, stumbling, landing and being in the air are untouched.
- Not checked in the running game: that standing on the jump ramp or on a quad's collider feels right, which only the scratch run covered in the open.

## The mini moto (TODO 10, part one)

Jamie's calls: do both the mini moto and the old three-wheeler, one at a time, the moto first. The moto rides like the quads (the self-balancing option, not a falling-over model), is solo, turns up at random like the other extras, is a 50cc pull-start two-stroke, and uses the kids' quad's sound for now. The ATC will use the yellow's sound, since both are manual two-strokes.

- **Physics** (`MOTO_LAYOUT` in `miniMotoModel.js`): the same raycast vehicle with four wheels, but two are in a line (front at z 0.5, rear at z -0.45, the rear driven) and two are hidden outriggers either side (`outrigger: true`, 0.38 m out, `driven: false`, `gripScale: 0.35` so they don't scrub in corners). A first try with the outriggers 0.24 m out and the default centre of mass tilted 27 degrees within a second and was on its side within three: the roll stiffness from the outriggers (2 k x squared) was barely more than gravity's push over. Now 0.38 m out, stiffness 60 (the quads are 28), and the centre of mass 0.22 m below the chassis origin, which gives a static tilt of about 4 degrees on the parking slope, 1 to 3 degrees at speed and about 10 m/s in 4 s.
- **Looks**: the body leans into corners for show (`userData.leans`, 0.3 rad at full lock and 6 m/s or more, eased over about 0.2 s) in `quad.js`; nothing is simulated. The bars, dash (key switch) and thumb choke are the quads' own, scaled to 0.85. The headlight turns with the bars, the fork legs with the front wheel.
- **Flat tyres**: a puncture can only land on the two real wheels (`Faults.tyreWheels`). The tyre gauge in the toolbox still lists four readings, so two of them are the hidden outriggers'. Not tidied.
- **Fleet**: the extras are now three, with chances 30, 40, 20 and 10 per cent of none, one, two or all three, and the line-up is capped at seven (`maxVehicles`) so a full house still fits the seven parking slots.
- **Names**: the girls' quad nicknames (Jess names it Pogo or the bumblebee) add lines to be recorded, as the loan quad's did.
- **Checked**: unit tests; one headless run on seed 2: the moto loads without errors, shows its prompt, starts on the starter, reaches 10 m/s and stays upright through left and right turns. Not checked: a girl riding it (seat height, legs on the pegs, whether her riding pose looks right on a bike this small), the pull-start by hand, how it sounds, being pushed, a puncture, and the bog and mud look from the hidden wheels' spray.

- **Second pass**: no starter motor (`noStarter` engine param: `R` does nothing, the HUD hint is gone and a girl goes straight to the cord whatever the battery); a pod fits on the bar clamp; the lamp has a shell and brackets and the loose side panels became an airbox cover joined to the frame. Wheels now shake on every quad (`wheelShake` default 0.6 of the body's heave, pitch and roll; the tractor stays at 1.3). The outriggers' grip went from 0.35 to 0.05, and the moto keeps its steering at speed (`steerSpeedReduction` 0.02) with the driven rear losing half its side grip when spinning. Measured at 10 m/s entry, full left: radius 6.2 m coasting and 6.3 m on full power, so the sim doesn't show power-on understeer; Jamie feels one, so the handling still needs a real two-wheeler model (lean-driven turning). Not done.

- **Idle and clutch**: a real pit bike idles fast, so the moto now idles at 1,800 to 2,200 rpm (`safeIdle` 1,400, warm idle 2,000), with a centrifugal clutch biting at 3,200 and locked at 5,000 (it was 2,200 and 4,000, which was too near the idle). Lower `idleRange` and `idleRpmWarm` in its spec to settle it. The ATC, when it's built, should idle around 1,300 to 1,800.
- **Asking the girls**: the existing wheel menu already offers "Drive it", "Follow me on it", "Start it" and "Stand next to it" for the moto (no "Take me for a ride" or "Get on it", as there's no back seat), so nothing new was needed. Checked once headless: Amy walked to it, got on, started it with the cord and rode it round the yard. Not checked: Jess, her legs on the pegs close up, or how she takes corners (the handling is item 59).
- **ATC**: solo, and the yellow's sound. Part two.

## The old three-wheeler (TODO 10, part two)

Jamie's calls: solo, the yellow's sound, an idle that's realistic but easy to turn down, girls can drive it from the wheel menu, and a proper two-wheeler or ATC rollover model is a separate item (59).

- **Physics** (`ATC_LAYOUT` in `atcModel.js`): the raycast vehicle with three wheels, one at the front centre (z 0.62) and two at the back (0.9 m track, z -0.55), the rear pair driven. The front wheel carries about twice the load of a rear one, and with the same stiffness it sat 12 degrees nose down at rest, so a wheel can now have a `stiffness` multiplier in the layout (front 2): it rests within 2 degrees of level.
- **Tipping**: no special code, just a narrow stance, a centre of mass at -0.06 (the quads' default is -0.08) and the steering lock cut with speed more than the quads' (`steerSpeedReduction` 0.12). Full left lock from a 13 m/s roll-in rolled it to 96 degrees over about 2 s and it was flagged tipped; from 9 m/s the tilt stayed under 7 degrees. A first try with a higher centre of mass and the quads' steering tipped it at 9 m/s; it tripped over its own front wheel and that felt unfair.
- **Engine**: a worn 200cc two-stroke: wear 0.35 to 0.65, 800 to 2,400 hours, idle 1,300 to 1,800 rpm (`idleRange`; warm 1,550), rough (0.45 to 0.7), no starter motor (`noStarter`) so only the cord and the girls go straight to it, a manual four-speed box (4, 8, 12 and 16 m/s) and a pull-knob choke. Sound: the yellow's two-stroke bank with a longer pipe.
- **Tyres and flats**: the tyre set and the faults now follow the layout's real wheels (`TyreSet.useLayout`, `Faults.wheelSides`): the ATC's gauge reads "F, RL, RR" and the moto's "F, R", a flat tyre lands on a real wheel and pulls to its side. The moto's hidden outriggers no longer show up in the toolbox. This also fixes the moto's tyre note above.
- **Fleet**: four extras now, 28, 35, 22, 10 and 5 per cent of none to all four, still capped at seven vehicles.
- **Checked**: unit tests; headless on seed 4: it loads without errors, Amy walked to it, started it on the cord, drove it through the gears at 5 m/s, and the screenshot shows her on it. Rollover as measured above. Not checked: the cord start by hand, shifting by hand, the girls tipping it (her driving brain doesn't know it tips), either girl's legs and feet on a seat this size, Jess, how it sounds, and bogs and mud with three wheels.
- **Look** (second go): the first model was boxes and clipped its own tyres. Now the tank and seat are lofted, the fenders are moulded shells (`shellGeometry`, the quads' own method) standing 0.15 m clear of the tyres, the front fender turns with the wheel, the barrel is finned, and the colour is a chalky, sun-bleached Honda red. The tyres are 0.3 m wide. Checked from three angles headless; no clipping seen.
- **Pods** fit both the mini moto and the ATC (each model has a `podMount` on the bar clamp): `fitPod` returns true on both and the pod shows in a screenshot.
- **Roughness showing 1.0**: not a regression. At the start every engine is cold, and the live roughness adds a cold term (0.55 to 0.7 on the quads, in `coldRoughness`) to the build roughness and wear, so anything with the two summing past 0.4 clamps to 1.0 until it warms. Checked on seed 4 at the commit before this work (`25d41ab`) and now: the same, blue 0.24 + 0.17 + 0.62, yellow 0.45 + 0.24 + 0.6, green 0.56 + 0.40 + 0.7, orange 0.89 + 0.19 + 0.6, all 1.00. The per-vehicle build roughness (`baseRoughness`) is still random and different for each. Left alone; see the summary for the options.

## The barn find (TODO 1)

Jamie's answers: it takes an additional slot (not one of the seven yard slots), 20% of games, bench jobs work where it stands, the charge takes about five minutes, a botched job still counts as done (it leaves the usual rough fault), and the tarp is a first job of its own. It is called the "rusty quad" everywhere it shows up.

- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/barnFind.js` (tests in `tests/barnFind.test.js`; tunables under "Barn find"). `pickBarnFind` takes its own stream (`seed ^ 0xba2f`, so the rest of a seed is untouched): a 20% chance, a donor from the five regular quads, and `minJobs` 3 to `maxJobs` 4 of the six jobs (a pull-start-only donor never gets the battery). `BarnFind` holds the state: tarp, jobs done, the charger, and `startBlocked` until the tarp is off and every job is done.
- It's a `QuadUnit` like the tractor: in `units` (so you can ride and fuel it and the barn fumes count it) but not `girlUnits`, so the girls never choose it, it has no pod, quirks or history, and no random faults roll for it. Spec: the donor's with id `rusty`, brown (0x6b4426), hours 1,900 to 2,800, 4,200 to 7,800 miles and wear 0.55 to 0.8. It stands at `LAYOUT.rusty` (back of the barn, clear of the bay, the bales and the ladder), brake on.
- Starting: `EngineModel.startBlocked` zeroes the chance of catching however it's cranked. Cranking it before it's ready toasts the first remaining cause. Some of the jobs have a real effect before they are done: the tyre job's start puts a puncture and a near-flat tyre on it, the carb one a gummed carb fault, the fuel one fouling, the battery starts at 3%. The seized brake just holds the parking brake on until freed.
- Bench: `Bench.unitInBay` falls back to the rusty quad while it still has work. Four new jobs in `benchJobs.js`: `tarp`, `fuel` (a windowed flush), `brake` (a windowed hold on the caliper) and `battery`, which is only the clip-on; `BarnFind` counts it once the charger has had `chargeSeconds` 300. The charger raises the battery steadily to 95%. The bench list for it is the tarp plus what it needs, with a tick on those done and a percentage while charging.
- Look: `rustyLook.js` copies each material (the quads share some) and pulls it towards rust on the metal and dust on everything; the tarp is a lumpy blue box over it that disappears with the tarp job. No assets.
- First start: on the first catch with everything done, the exhaust burst is held on for `smokeSeconds` 4, and both girls (in earshot) cheer (`rustyLines.js`, `rustyTarp` and `rustyRuns`). They also react when the tarp comes off. Amy then switches it off by the usual fumes rule, which I left alone.
- Left out: the girls riding it or having a view on it afterwards, a wheel entry for it, hands-on animation, and the logbook's starting entries mentioning it. Checked: unit tests, a production build, and once headless on seed 13 that the game loads with no errors, the rusty quad is in place under its tarp, won't start while blocked and does start (with the first-start flag set) once every job is done. Not checked: how it looks (dust, rust, tarp, placement against the bales and ladder), the bench list in play, the feel of the new holds, and the new lines (browser speech until the banks are committed).

Follow-up: draining the varnished fuel empties the tank, float bowl and oil (`FuelSystem.drain`, oil set to 0), so after the job you have to fill it from the petrol can, or the pre-mix can for the yellow and orange, before it can start. The toast says so. A rusty quad whose plan has no fuel job keeps its ordinary starting fuel.

### The rusty quad in full detail (TODO 97)

Done without photos (Jamie's call, with screenshots from me instead); every call below was his answer to the numbered questions: **brown stays**, **cosmetic damage with a few broken parts** (no holes cut in the meshes), **one shared damage set applied by part name and surface** rather than five hand edits, and **only what shows from outside**.

- `src/vehicle/rustyDamage.js` (pure plan, tested in `tests/rustyDamage.test.js`; look drawn on canvases from a seeded stream, no assets). `planDamage(random, donorId)` deals each donor its own: the three with racks (red, blue, green) lose one front slat and have another hang broken, and the rear rack has one hanging; a nest only on the yellow (the orange's shroud hides its engine, and no donor shows a plug, so there is no plug lead to swap); 16 to 22 rust patches, 7 to 11 cracks and 6 to 9 webs (`DEFAULT_RUSTY_DAMAGE`).
- **Broken racks are made with the model** (`decay` through `QuadUnit`, `Quad` and `buildQuadModel` into `rack()`: `skip` leaves a slat out, `hang` makes it half a bar and a drooping end), since the merged racks cannot be cut afterwards.
- **Everything else is laid on after `weatherModel`** (now called with `rust: 0.5, dust: 0.25`, so the metal goes further to rust): patches and cracks are planes placed on whatever the body offers, found by ray against the donor's own meshes (tops from above, flanks from the side; never the seat, the wheels or the tarp), so any donor takes them. The patch texture has orange and brown blotches with dark pits and flecks; the crack is a jagged dark line with a pale dried edge and forks. Webs are corner fans hung at the grips, the racks' corners (where it has racks), the arches and the seat's back. Each wheel gets a pale grey cloned rubber, and a ring of sidewall cracks either side.
- **The jobs show** (`RustyLook.sync`, called from `updateBarnFind` each frame and on a load): the tyre job swaps in fresh black rubber and hides the cracks; the carb job removes the nest; the tarp, which is now 1.25 m tall (the donors' bars and racks stood through the old 1 m one), slides 1.9 m off to the side and slumps over 1.3 s, then is gone (a loaded farm skips the slide). Not shown: the fuel, plug, brake and battery jobs (nothing about them can be seen on any donor from outside).
- Seeds for each donor: 7, 8, 12, 16, 17 and 38 give the green's, 15, 25 and 35 the red's, 48 and 49 the blue's, 50 the yellow's, 13 and 27 the orange's. Screenshots of each, tarp on and off, the rack, the wheels before and after, and the nest are in `.scratch/rusty-shots/` (untracked).
- **Second round** (Jamie: the tarp too boxy; mud and webs overhanging the wheels; webs hanging horizontal and not joined to anything; rips in the seat; rusty wheel centres; the tyre cracks too faint). **Tarp**: no longer a box. It is a cloth draped over this donor's own shape (`buildTarp(model)`: five rays on each point of a 22 by 32 grid find the highest thing there, the heights are spread two cells out and softened, the cloth is never under what it covers, and the border comes down to the ground with folds over the lifted part), so the bars, racks and wheel arches make their own humps. **Patches and cracks follow the surface** (`projectedPatch`): each is a grid of points dropped onto the body along the normal, kept only if it lands close and facing the same way, and a triangle is kept only if all three corners landed, so a patch stops at a fender's edge and never hangs in the air over a tyre (a test checks that no vertex is more than a few centimetres off the body). **Webs hang**: the texture is now a half web with its point at the top middle and the plane stands upright with that point on a grip, a rack rail or the seat's underside, so it falls straight down; none lies flat. **Seat rips**: two or three splits in the seat's cover (a jagged slit, foam showing yellow-brown, threads), more on the rider's seat than the pillion's, following the seat's top. **Wheels**: the rims and hubs are rust-brown with patches eaten into their faces; the tyre cracks are now a polar map on a ring that follows the sidewall's real profile (read from the tyre's vertices), with about 130 thick forked cracks with pale edges, so they read from a few metres. The tyre job still hides them. The barn tarp's ground footprint is a little wider than the quad's.
- **Looked at, not tuned further**: the rust patches' orange is bolder than a long-neglected finish would be on the paint; the cracks on the tyres are short straight dashes; the rims are tinted but still look more grey than rusted. **Not done**: the flat tyre's sag (the existing puncture state shows it), and a mouse nest on any donor but the yellow.

## The diesel UTV (TODO 2)

Settled with Jamie before building: always in the game, parked by the paddock (a unit like the tractor, so not one of the girls' quads); an automatic with a dash lever for reverse; girls ride in it but don't drive it yet, and you can't ride in the bed; the girls squabble over the middle seat and one rides in the bed, and tipping it slides her off; red diesel only, petrol makes it run badly; Deere-style green. The model was to be accurate and detailed.

- **It's a unit, built from `UTV_SPEC`** (`quadSpecs.js`; `QuadUnit` reads `utv`). `game.units` has it, `game.girlUnits` doesn't, so the girls' driving, nicking, tea and the rest never see it. Placed at `LAYOUT.utv` (-52.5, 40.5), between the paddock and the quads' rows, where you start can see it. No pods, no faults, no history, no toolbox tinkering (the toolbox only pumps the tank out). The quirks module leaves it out of the choke and split-seat quirks.
- **Physics** on the quad physics with its own layout: 1.96 m wheelbase, 1.32 m track, 0.34 m tyres, 760 kg with a driver, four-wheel drive, 11.2 m/s top (25 mph), 3.4 m/s reverse, 32 degrees of lock. A touch over twice a quad's mass with about twice the drive force, brakes and inertia, and a softer rest length (0.2 m) with 0.22 m of travel. Not tuned against anything: these are first values. The steering wheel turns 6 times the road wheels' angle (`steerGain` in the model's userData, the quads' bars stay at 0.85).
- **Engine**: a three-cylinder diesel with the tractor's glow plug start (`compressionIgnition`), 850 rpm idle, 3,200 max, 3,100 redline. Two new engine params: `keyStops` (the key switches a running diesel off, where the tractor carries on until its knob) and `petrolFussy` (the soak numbers in `diesel.js` run on the petrol share of the tank, `contaminant()`, instead of on the diesel share). With the existing numbers a tenth of petrol is a rough running engine, a fifth stalls it and it won't restart. A diesel no longer gets a run-on when the key is turned off. Its sound reuses the tractor's recordings (`engine-bank-tractor`) with `pulseRefRpm` 850.
- **Fuel**: 30 L, 3 L reserve, no tap, red diesel from the bowser and can. Pouring a petrol can in makes whoever's near shout (`utvPetrolWarn`, via `mixWrongFor`), and once the engine is soaked past 0.2 and running, someone near says `utvPetrol` once. The toolbox on the UTV pumps the tank and carb out (`Quad.drainFuel`, soak cleared) if there's any petrol about; after that it needs red diesel.
- **Controls**: `T` tips the bed (stopped, from the seat or within 3.2 m on foot), `B` is the dash lever, a new reverse style `dashLever` that wants it under 0.5 m/s (`controls.js`). `utvBed.js` (tested): 7 s up, 5 s down, won't rise above 0.8 m/s, lowers itself if you drive off above 2 m/s, the tailgate drops once the bed is 8% up or while someone sits on it, and whoever is on it slides off from 55% up.
- **The model** (`utvModel.js`, procedural like the others, not tested in unit tests since it needs a canvas): a ladder frame with wishbones and coil-overs, a bonnet with stripes, grille and headlamps (lit by the headlight code), a bumper with tow loops, front fenders, a floor with ribs, a dash panel with a glovebox, grab handle and cup holders, a bench in grey cloth with seams and the three belts stowed down the back, a black roll cage with a cab guard and side bars, a moulded roof, the steering wheel and column with the dash on top, a brake and accelerator pedal, a parking brake handle and the dash lever, a silencer and tailpipe, a hitch receiver, tail lamps, yellow rims on chunky tyres, and a black ribbed bed with wheel wells, tie-downs, a front wall and a tailgate on a hinge. The dash (`utv` in `dash.js`) is a speedo with an hour meter, fuel and water dials, GLOW, CHARGE and OIL lamps and R / N / D.
- **The girls** (`utvRide.js`, tested; `utvMount.js`): the same machinery as the hay trailer and the tractor's mudguards (`boardTrailer`, `onTrailer`, `sliding`), with three slots: `left` and `mid` on the bench, and `bed`, which is the open tailgate (she sits facing backwards with her legs hanging). `standPoint` and `slideOffTrailer` take the slot and a direction now. Stop with it running and there's a 60% chance a girl sets off; with both near it's both 45% of the time. Two coming means a row over the middle 70% of the time, Jess winning 60%; the loser sits at the window, or goes to the bed (Jess 35%, Amy 2%). One coming on her own goes to the bed 30% of the time for Jess and 3% for Amy. The other girl says something about the bed once, 1.5 to 3.5 s later; the one in the bed complains every 18 to 40 s above 6 m/s. They climb out when you get off unless you asked them in from the wheel (Amy refuses the bed), and get bored and leave after 2 to 5 minutes. About 60 new lines (`utvLines.js`: eleven situations, three lines each), recorded.
- **Left out**: the girls driving it (it'd want the pedal-to-lever hand work of `companionDriving.js`), you riding in the bed, a payload (bales, the can, the toolbox in the bed), 2WD and 4WD (TODO 3), a trailer on its hitch (TODO 6), and a winch.
- **Checked**: 34 unit tests (`utvBed`, `utvRide`, `utvEngine`, `utvMount`); the game loads with no page errors; headless screenshots of the UTV from the front, back and the seat, with both girls on the bench, one on the tailgate, and the bed tipped with her sliding off and Jess's line showing. **Not checked**: how it drives and handles (nobody drove it), the engine's sound, the glow start in play, the girls boarding by themselves on a stop or squabbling, the lamps on the dash lit, the petrol soak in play, and the girls' lines by ear.

Follow-up: the bench is 1.14 m wide so it sits between the cage posts instead of through them; the rear hoop and cab guard moved forward to z -0.58 with their feet on the seat base, and the bed's wheel wells start behind them. The seat belts stand off the cloth (they z-fought it). The tail lamps moved from a floating spot on the frame to the rear ends of the bed's sides, so they tip with it. The dash lever stands on a plate on top of the dash. The dash has a red PARK lamp (parking brake, lit with the ignition on). Checked by screenshot from the side, rear and seat.

## 2WD and 4WD (TODO 3)

Settled with Jamie before building: only the red and blue get the switch (the green and loan quads stay fixed four-wheel drive, the rest fixed rear-wheel); `G` flips 2WD and 4WD with a physical switch on both; the blue gets a diff lock on `U`; 2WD is the default; it persists per quad until a new game; the girls forget it.

- **Logic** (`driveMode.js`, tested): `DriveSwitch` holds `2wd`, `4wd` or `lock` (the lock only where `hasLock`, and it implies 4WD, so `G` from the lock goes straight to 2WD). `driveEffects(mode)` gives what the mode does that step. Specs opt in with `driveSwitch: true` (and `diffLock: true` on the blue); `QuadUnit` passes it to `Quad`, which makes `quad.drive`. Quads without one keep the spec's fixed `fourWheelDrive` and never see any of this.
- **What it does**: 2WD drives the rear axle only (`QuadPhysics.frontDriven` overrides the spec's `fourWheelDrive`, which the physics already knew how to do) and the bars respond 1.2 times as quickly. 4WD drives all four and costs 4% of the drive (`fourWdLoss`), at the bars' own speed. The lock costs the same 4%, takes 12% more drive before the tyres give up (`lockGrip`, through a new `Traction.gripScale`), responds at 0.8 and only turns 88% of full lock. I didn't measure these in the running game (see below), just picked values with the right ordering; the tuning panel's "2WD and 4WD" folder has them all.
- **Switch and pods**: a rocker in a little housing inboard of the right grip on the red and blue (`driveSwitchRocker`, eased with the mode), and a red button beside it on the blue (`diffLockButton`, sinks when locked). The stock dashes show nothing (neither quad's dash had a 4WD lamp to begin with; I first added one to the red and the blue and Jamie had them taken out again): the switch shows the mode. The accessory pods carry it instead: the analogue gets a green 4WD lamp (and a red LOCK on the blue) and the digital a 4WD label with a bar under it for the lock, only when the fitted quad has a switch (`fourWd` and `diffLock` in the dash state are `null` for one that hasn't). The loan quad's always-lit 4WD label is untouched.
- **The girls**: `updateFourWd` in `game.js`. Taking the wheel of a switched quad, she remembers with her own `remembers4wd` chance (Amy 0.65, Jess 0.25; the default is 0.4) and flips it to 4WD if she does. Otherwise a `StuckWatch` waits for 2.5 s of throttle over mud (0.35 or more, track mud counts) at under 1.2 m/s in 2WD, then she flips the switch. A girl in 4WD already, or moving, never trips it. Amy tells Jess off (`fourWdToldJess`) and Jess crows at Amy (`fourWdToldAmy`), each only if the friend is within 18 m (the handbrake's hearing range), then the one who forgot owns up (`fourWdOwn`). Lines are in `fourWdLines.js`.
- **Left out**: the girls putting a quad back to 2WD when they get off (it stays as left), and the girls using the diff lock. The UTV and tractor have no switch.

## The little tipping trailer (TODO 6)

Settled with Jamie before building: a second, small trailer (the bale trailer stays tractor-only); hitches to the tractor and any quad with a rack; handling changes kept cheap; bales left out (they aren't pick-up items yet); girls can be asked in through the wheel as well as hopping in by themselves.

- **It's the bale trailer's cousin, not a physics body.** `TipTrailer` (`vehicle/tipTrailer.js`, model in `tipTrailerModel.js`) reuses `TrailerMotion` (the axle dragged behind the hitch on a rigid 1.7 m drawbar, the body on springs) so it can't flip a quad or be driven into, and the UTV's `UtvBed` for the tub (`riseSeconds` 4, `lowerSeconds` 3, tips from standing, lets itself down above 2 m/s, `slideAt` 0.5). It stands by the paddock at `LAYOUT.tipTrailer` (-49, 33.5) with its tongue towards the yard.
- **Who can pull it.** `canTow(quad)`: the tractor (drawbar pin, 1.5 m back) or a quad with a rear rack (the ones with a `mugAnchor`: the red, blue and green utilities; hitch 1.25 m back and 0.48 m up). The mini moto, ATC, race quads and UTV can't. `J` hitches when the hitch is within `hitchRange` (1 m) of the ring and unhitches when you're under 0.5 m/s. `J` on the tractor now works for either trailer; one at a time per puller.
- **Handling, cheap** (`vehicle/towing.js`, pure, tested). No physics: `towLoad` is 90 kg for the tub, 55 per girl in it and 18 per can or toolbox; `towEffect` takes 0.16% of the puller's drive and 0.09% of its brakes per kg (floors of 45% and 60% left), so an empty tub costs a quad about 14% of its drive and 8% of its braking, and a girl and three things about 29% and 16%. The tractor feels 15% of that. `Quad.tow` holds the two factors, applied to the drive force and the pedal brake in `quad.js`. Reversing used to just follow the hitch pin; it jack-knifes now ("Reversing a trailer (TODO 78)"). Tunables under Towing, Tipping trailer, Trailer tub and Trailer riders.
- **Carrying.** `E` at the tub with a jerry can or the toolbox in your hands stands it in one of three spots (`load`); you pick it up again the usual way. Things in the tub ride its tilt. `T` (stopped, from the seat of the puller or within 3 m on foot) tips the tub, and from half way up everything in it slides out behind in half a second, landing on the ground as an ordinary dropped item. The pranks and requests code reads an item's world position now, in case it's in the trailer.
- **The girl in it** (`npc/trailerRide.js`, pure, tested; the sit, climb and slide states are the bale trailer's in `companionHayRide.js`). One seat, on the tub floor. Stopped with it hitched and the engine running, a girl who's stood about within 30 m has a 40% chance to hop in after 2 to 6 s: Jess when both are near (85%), Amy alone 8%. The other girl says it's against the rules; a hard bump (over 0.45 m/s of bounce) gets a shriek, no more than one in 10 s; above 4 m/s she complains every 15 to 35 s; she climbs out when you get off or unhitch, or after 90 to 240 s. Tipping the tub with her in it sends her off the back into the mud, and the other girl laughs. **Through the wheel:** a "Trailer" slice on either girl's wheel (get in, get out); asked in, she goes at once and stays until asked out. Amy goes in when asked.
- **Lines** (`trailerLines.js`, merged into the main sets): `trailerBoard`, `trailerAsked`, `trailerTell`, `trailerBump`, `trailerShout`, `trailerTipOut`, `trailerTipLaugh`, three lines each for each girl, recorded.
- **Checked:** unit tests for the towing numbers and the ride logic; headless, with the red quad backed to the ring: the prompt, `J` hitching, Jess asked in (she walked over and sat down), a can and the toolbox loaded, a drive and bend with her aboard (the towing factors dropped to 0.71 drive and 0.84 braking, and came back to 0.86 and 0.92 once she and the things were out), then `T` tipping the tub, the girl sliding off with Jess's line, the cargo gone from the tub. **Not checked:** the hitch height against each rack quad's real bumper, how it feels to drive, reversing it, the tractor pulling it, Jess hopping in by herself, the girls' lines by ear, or the tub in a bog. Left out: bales, the UTV's receiver, and the girls driving with it on.

## Hand throttle from the ground (2026-10-07)

- Looking at the tractor, or the Land Rover, with nobody on it or driving it, its hand throttle moves with the keys it has on the seat (`Q` up and `Z` down on the tractor, `]` out and `[` in on the Land Rover's dash knob), at the same 0.35 a second (`stepHand` in `vehicle/handLever.js`, pure and tested, now also used by the seat code). The prompt chip row for the tractor lists Q Z. It stays where it is left, as before, so you can raise the revs to warm it or let it creep off.

- Things were overlapping the sides because each was set down at the angle you were facing, and the toolbox is 0.5 m long in a tub 1.04 m wide inside. Now they all stand in a fixed row across the front (x -0.32, 0, 0.32, 0.3 m from the axle, `CARGO_SPOTS`), the cans and toolbox turned a quarter turn so their long side runs along the tub and the roundish leads left square (`CARGO_YAWS`). The footprints are in `CARGO_FOOTPRINTS`, and a test (`tests/towing.test.js`) checks that any item in any spot stays inside the walls and the front, and that the widest two neighbours clear each other. The girl's seat at the back is untouched.

It never starts with a pod, so the seeded placement is unchanged. Checked: unit tests only; not tried at the bench in the running game.

## The Land Rover (TODO 14)

Settled with Jamie before building: the starting handle with its kick-back, yes; a manual four-speed; the girls ride beside you on a bench for three as in the UTV, not in the back, and don't drive it; a Series III 88-inch pick-up in Bronze Green with a white roof, always in the yard; a petrol quad's wear, faults, battery, choke, quirks and toolbox; its sound from the shortlist (`docs/briefs/vehicle-sounds.md`). Jamie's brief also asked for 2WD and 4WD, a diff lock, a hand throttle and towing the little trailer.

- **A unit like the UTV, built from `LAND_ROVER_SPEC`** (`quadSpecs.js`, `landRover: true`; model in `landRoverModel.js`). `game.units` has it, `game.girlUnits` doesn't, so the girls leave it alone except to ride in it. It parks at `LAYOUT.landRover` (-54, 71.5), north of the barn doors: the first spot (-50.5, 67.5) was on a straw bale, and it spawned on top and flipped, so a test now keeps its spot 4 m from every bale and 6 m from the other vehicles. Unlike the UTV it's dealt what a petrol quad gets, by hand since it's added after the fleet's dealing: wear from `wearRange`, tyres, one to three quirks (the Series dash counts as a gauge dash, so a sticking fuel gauge can land; the seat quirks can't, it's a vinyl bench), a sump, and faults with the belt switched off (`prone.belt = 0`). No fuel tap (a Series has none), so no sticky float, which I'd offered and Jamie had agreed to.
- **Starting handle** (`crankHandle.js`, tested). Stand within 1.2 m of the hole in the front bumper (ground distance) on foot and the prompt shows your grip: `1` thumb round the handle (the default, and it kicks back on 30% of swings), `2` thumb alongside the fingers (never). A swing is the engine's pull (`pullStart` with `crankHandle`): hold `P` and let go, 0.35 to 0.9 s for a good one, turning it over at 0.6 of the starter's speed for 0.7 s. A kick-back stops the turn, leaves your hand hurting for 4 s (no swinging), says why, and the nearest girl within 15 m says something (`landyKick`, three lines each). The engine reports a swing as `handCrankOn` rather than `starterOn`, and the worklet plays a one-shot clunk for it instead of the starter loop.
- **Gearbox**: the manual drivetrain, four speeds, and two new gearbox params: `crashGears` (2: first and second have no synchromesh) and `crashSpeed` (1 m/s). `select` takes the vehicle's speed and returns `crunch`, leaving the gear where it was, when a crash gear is asked for faster than that. Tested.
- **Hand throttle**: a knob on the dash, `]` out and `[` in (Q and Z are its choke, which the tractor hasn't got), moving 0.35 a second like the tractor's, and holding the throttle at least that open. `Quad.handLever`; starts pushed in.
- **4WD and diff lock**: the existing drive switch (`G`, `U`), on the yellow and red knobs by the gear lever, and lamps on the dash. Starts in 2WD.
- **Towing**: `canTow` and the trailer's hitch point now take any model with a `towHitch` (the Land Rover's jaw, 2.02 m behind its middle); it counts as heavy for the load, as the tractor does.
- **Dash** (`series` in `dash.js`): a speedo to 90 mph round 260 degrees with a five-drum odometer, a combined fuel and water gauge, and IGN, OIL, CHOKE, BRAKE, 4WD and DIFF lamps (the dash state gained `choke`). Checked at full resolution in a screenshot from the seat.
- **Sound** (`engine-bank-landy`): the Jeep's idle beats every 60 ms in groups of four, about 500 rpm, so the Land Rover idles at 520 to 580 (a Series 2.25 idles about there) and the idle is cut into whole four-beat cycles read in order; the cycles came out at 470 to 530 rpm. `pulsePitchFollow` 1, so as it revs each whole cycle is squeezed into the engine's cycle and all four beats sound. The starter borrows the quad's; the handle's clunk is one swing from craigsmith's crank-start car (31.95 to 32.45 s); the shutdown is the Jeep stopping (55.2 to 57 s).
- **Numbers, first guesses**: 1,350 kg, 2.23 m wheelbase, 0.38 m tyres, 7,000 N of drive, 14.5 m/s in top, 4,200 rpm, 45 litres burning 0.0003 l per revolution (about 40 minutes flat out), heavy slow steering. `recVolume` 0.6 is unmeasured.
- **Size and smoothness, after Jamie tried it.** Built to a real Series's measurements it looked small beside the girls, so the spec's `scale` is 1.15 (the model and its physics chassis together; the seats, stand points, starting handle and towing jaw follow, the people don't grow). It also ran too rough: the engine model is a single cylinder, and a misfire or a lumpy beat on a four loses a quarter of the cycle where on a single it loses the lot. So its roughness effects are about 40% of a quad's (`misfireRoughness` 0.06, `cycleJitter` 0.07, `pulseStrengthJitter` 0.18, `roughHunt` 0.12, `erraticHunt` 0.07, `stumbleRate` 0.2) and it starts less rough (`roughnessRange` 0.1 to 0.2). Wear still roughens it as it does a quad. Neither change checked in the running game.
- **Four cylinders, second go, after Jamie tried it again.** It still ran too rough, revving sounded strange, its tyres sat in the ground and it flickered behind the seats.
  - *The engine fires once per cylinder.* A new engine param, `cylinders` (default 1, the Land Rover 4), makes the cycle four evenly spaced firings, each its own pulse. Each firing's chances (catching, popping, stalling on water or diesel) and its kick to the revs are a quarter of a cycle's, so a misfire costs a quarter of the beat. In a seeded test at the same roughness the idle's rpm spread is under 70% of a single's. Smoke and the run-on are shared out the same way. Its vibration's `firingsPerCycle` goes back to 1, since the pulses already come four to a cycle.
  - *Roughness cut much further*: `wearRange` 0.04 to 0.12 (was 0.2 to 0.45), `roughnessRange` 0.04 to 0.1, `wearPerMinute` 0.0003, `misfireRoughness` 0.03, `cycleJitter` 0.04, `pulseStrengthJitter` 0.1, `roughHunt` 0.04, `erraticHunt` 0.03, `stumbleRate` 0.1.
  - *Sound*: the strange revving was `pulsePitchFollow` 1 squeezing a whole 230 ms four-beat cycle into the engine's cycle: at 3,000 rpm it played six times too fast, resonances and all. The idle is now cut into single beats (`cycleSplit` 4, 392 beats of about 55 ms, labelled 1,810 to 2,190 rpm, four times the engine's), one played per firing at nearly its own speed (`pulsePitchFollow` 0.25). In an offline render through the worklet the beat rate follows the firing rate (19 Hz at idle, 61 Hz part open, 140 Hz at 4,200 rpm) while the sound's centre of brightness only rises from about 1.7 to 2.5 kHz.
  - *Sitting in the ground*: the physics took the layout's wheel radius and the suspension length unscaled, so at `scale` 1.15 it stood on 0.38 m wheels while the model drew 0.44 m ones, 9 cm into the ground. A layout's own wheel radius is now scaled with the chassis (only the UTV and the Land Rover set one; the UTV is scale 1), and the spec's `suspensionRestLength` is 0.23 and `wheelRadius` 0.437. A test checks the physics ground meets the model's.
  - *Flicker behind the seats*: the rear window was a plane laid on the cab's rear panel, the same depth exactly. The panel is now four pieces round an opening with the glass set in it, and the seat backs are 4 cm further forward so their tops, leant back, stay clear of it.
  - *No room to get off*: the spots you step off to are a quad's (1 m to the side, 1.7 m behind, 1.8 m in front) and at 1.15 scale the Land Rover's physics box reaches 0.92 m to the side and 2.07 m fore and aft, so every spot was inside it. Each spot is now pushed out until you stand clear of the vehicle's own box (`vehicle/dismount.js`), never pulled in, so the quads are unchanged; the tractor's spot behind moves out from 1.7 to 1.88 m.
- **Idle pace and top speed, third go.** Jamie: the idle still sounded a bit quick, and it should top out at 50 to 55 mph.
  - *Idle*: the Jeep's beats in the bank run at a median of 500 rpm (474 to 528 for the middle 80%), but the Land Rover idled at 520 to 580, so every beat came 4 to 16% early and was clipped short. It now idles at 470 to 510 (`idleRpmWarm` 490), and a test keeps its range within 8% under and 5% over the recording's median.
  - *Gearing*: 4.5, 8, 14 and 24 m/s (was 2.6, 5, 9 and 14.5), even steps of about 1.75 like the real box's, with `maxSpeed` 24. The drive fades as (speed / top) squared, so flat out it settles just under 24 m/s, about 52 mph; not measured in the game. Torque shares unchanged. `steerSpeedReduction` 0.08 (was 0.05), so at 50 mph it has about 11 degrees of lock, not 15.
- **Half pace, fourth go.** Jamie: still far too fast, try half speed. A new audio param, `pulseDivide` (default 1), sounds only every Nth firing and gives it N firings' time, so with `pulseStretch` 1 each beat is stretched to fill it: the recording at 1 / N of its pace and lower. The Land Rover has 2. Offline through the worklet the warm idle beats at 9 Hz (was 19) and flat out at 70 (was 140), as loud as before. Found while checking: started cold it only catches on full choke, and the choke holds it at about 1,130 rpm through most of its five-minute warm-up, 2.3 times the recording's pace, which is probably much of what sounded fast. The choke is unchanged for now.
- **A third, and a steadier idle, fifth go.** Jamie: half pace sounded far better, maybe slower still, a third; and the recording wanders up and down while it idles. `pulseDivide` is 3. Mapped through the Jeep recording in half-second windows: its beat holds at 60 to 61 ms from 7 to 20.5 s, but the old 3 to 27 s cut took in the start, where the beat is 58 to 59 ms, and 21 to 27 s, where it jumps between 40, 48 and 55 ms and the level drops by a third. Following each beat from 6.5 to 21 s, the windows starting near 15.4 s have the evenest timing and strength (strength varying 7 to 9% against 14% over 7 to 20.5 s), so the idle is now just 15.4 to 18.4 s: 45 beats. There its pattern repeats every three beats (correlation 0.28 at 182 ms against 0.13 at four beats), so it's cut in threes (`cycleSplit` 3) and still read in order. Offline the idle beats 5.3 times a second, its loudness steady to about 5% from one 200 ms block to the next.
  - *Cutting off*: Jamie heard it hit a hard edge and cut off at idle. The worklet never slowed a beat below 0.45 of its speed, and a third of the pace needs about 0.32 (a 61 ms beat filling three firings, about 190 ms), so each beat stopped after about 135 ms with a dead gap before the next: silent 23% of the time in the render. That floor is now a setting, `pulseSlowest` (default 0.45, so the quads are unchanged), 0.25 on the Land Rover; re-rendered, nothing at idle is near silent.
- **Checked**: unit tests (crank handle, crash gears, the spec, towing, quirks, the crank's sound event, its parking spot); headless, the game loads without errors and screenshots from the front, back, side and seat. **Not checked**: driving it (gears, the crunch, 4WD in mud), the starting handle in play, the hand throttle, towing the trailer with it, the girls climbing in, its sound by ear or its loudness against the others, and the toolbox panel on it.
- **Double-declutching the crash gears (TODO 77).** A crash gear (`crashGears`) used to take only a near stop. Now you can also change down into one on the move the real way: into neutral, clutch up for `declutchTime` (0.2 s) so the box turns freely, rev the engine to what the gear's road speed needs (speed over that gear's top speed, as a share of idle to flat out), clutch down, select. `select` takes `revs` and answers `mismatch` (gear stays out, a different message) when they are more than `matchTolerance` (0.2) apart, and `crunch` as before when you did not go through neutral with the clutch up. `neutralFree` is the drivetrain's count of that time. The girls are unchanged (Amy never grinds, Jess always does: `grindsGear`). Tuning is `declutchTime` and `matchTolerance` with the other gearbox params. Tested in `manualDrivetrain.test.js`; not tried by hand in the game, so the tolerance is a first guess.

## Rolling back on the hill (TODO 3, follow-up)

- Jamie: stopped on the hill with the brake off, the quad didn't roll back. Since the handbrake item (TODO 16), a quad with someone at the controls, stopped and off the throttle, held itself on any slope (the `parkingBrake` drag and the physics' parked hold). That was there to stop the raycast wheels creeping down the farm's gentle slopes. Now (`holdsItself` in `src/vehicle/handbrake.js`, tested) it only does so where its nose is up or down less than `selfHoldSlope` 0.1 (about 6 degrees, a sine). Every slope on the farm is gentler (the yard is 6 to 8%), and there the tyres' rolling resistance would hold a real quad anyway. On the hill's banks an automatic's clutch is out at idle, so it rolls back unless you hold `S` or set the parking brake. The tractor and the manual quads, sat in gear, still hold on any slope.
- Checked headless, you on the red quad facing up a 34% bank: off the throttle it rolled back 11 m in 3 s and stopped on the gentler ground below; holding `S` it slid under half a metre.
- The ford drowning the engine is as the drowning model has it (`src/engine/water.js`): the ford's water counts as deep as a puddle's middle, and it's about 7 m across. Under 4 m/s nothing gets in; at about 5 m/s it splutters; from about 6.5 m/s (15 mph) a crossing takes in enough to stall it. Left as it is.


## A steadier four-cylinder shake

- Request: the Land Rover's shake was too random for a four-cylinder, too like the quads.
- Each firing kicked the body, bars and head with a random size and a random left or right sign, which suits a lumpy single and not an even four. A new vibration param, `steadiness` (0 to 1, default 0 so the quads and the others are unchanged), pulls the kicks towards a fixed pattern: every kick the same size, the side-to-side parts rocking left then right in turn. The Land Rover has 0.85, so at idle it shudders in a regular rock at the firing rate (about 16 a second at 490 rpm) rather than jittering.
- The engine side was already calm (`pulseStrengthJitter` 0.1, `cycleJitter` 0.04), so only the vibration needed changing. A test shows the spread of the body's roll is under 60% of the random default's.
- Not changed: the tractor and UTV, which are also multi-cylinder; say if they want it too. Not checked in the running game, only in unit tests.
- The UTV (a three-cylinder) has `steadiness` 0.6, a tad rougher than the Land Rover's 0.85, at Jamie's request. The tractor is still random.

## Every vehicle in the random pool (TODO 3)

Decided with you: all twelve vehicles in one pool (the barn find stays its own roll), four to all of them each game weighted towards the middle, the tractor, UTV and Land Rover dealt places like the quads, anything missing just switches off what needs it, and the bale trailer stays fixed but moves out of the hedge.

- **Count and pool** (`src/vehicle/fleet.js`, own random stream): `pickCount` averages `spread` (2) dice between `minVehicles` (4) and the whole pool, so the mean is about 8 of 12 and every count turns up (a test over 600 seeds). `minRegulars` (2) swaps extras for regular quads that missed out, so there's always a quad for each girl and a home quad. Before, the regulars were thinned and the extras added, three to seven vehicles in all, and the tractor, UTV and Land Rover were always there at fixed spots.
- **Places** (`src/world/spawns.js`): the tractor, UTV and Land Rover go through `randomiseQuadSpawns` with the quads (the home quad is always one of the quads, in the yard). They keep 4.6 m from their neighbours (quads 2.8 m) and choose their away spots first, never a spot marked `tight` (the Dutch barn bay and the road). There are 7 parking slots, so with more vehicles than that the overflow is left out on the farm (12 away spots in all). Their `LAYOUT` entries are gone.
- **Missing vehicles**: `tractorUnit`, `utvUnit` and `landyUnit` are null when not here, and the mounts and the tow chain are null with them. The per-frame updates (tractor remarks, runaway, mudguard ride, go on the tractor, UTV, Land Rover) and the girls' wheel slices are skipped; the hay ride, the hitch and the tipping trailer still run. The bucket list already dropped items for a vehicle not in the fleet (`needs`). Watch for `x === this.tractorUnit` comparisons: with nothing riding and no tractor that's `null === null`, so each is guarded.
- **Bale trailer**: now at (-42.5, 72.5), not (-44.5, 77.9), whose body ran into the north hedge at z 80. Fixed whatever the tractor's doing: you drive over and back up to it. A test keeps it 6 m off the hedge.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load for each of three seeds (70: no big three; 2: Land Rover only; 20: all twelve) showing no page errors over six seconds of frames; it caught a null `chain` in the achievements snapshot. Not checked: where each of them ends up in play (a big one left on a slope or against a hedge), the tractor or UTV dealt a slot in the yard against the quads' rows (a girl getting one out), the hay trailer's new spot by eye, and playing a game with no tractor.

## The UTV as a Mule-style Ridgeback Yak 4010 (TODO 99)

From eight photos Jamie put in `.scratch/quads/utv/` (the front, a three-quarter from the rear, front three-quarters with and without a bed cover, the cluster, the dash and switchgear). Settled up front: **a Mule 4010**, **door nets**, the dash lever arrangement kept, and **seat belts that look better and work well, with a proper buckle**. `muleBody.js` has the numbers and `muleDetail.js` the parts.

- **Proportions**: the old model was 3.34 m long and 1.70 wide against the real 3.0 by 1.55. The bed is shorter (tailgate at z -1.64, was -1.8) and the tail lamps moved off it, the mirrors are inside the cab's width, and `utv` is in `IN_PROPORTION`. **A fixed point moved on purpose**: the tailgate seat `bedSeat` from z -1.95 to -1.81 (and its stand point from -2.75 to -2.61), to follow the shorter bed; `tests/fixedPoints.json` regenerated for the UTV only.
- **Body**: hood, body, ribs, grille (the kit's diamond mesh), lamps, bumper and flares, a windscreen (clear glass in a frame, `opacity 0.14`, no depth write), a grey roof, the rear hoop with a mesh net and a bar the belts hang from, the lamps on a plate across the roof's back, a green steel bed with beads and a chained tailgate, a hitch with a ball, and dark rims with four nuts inboard of the tyre's face (the tractor's lesson). The maker's name is the made-up Ridgeback, through the decals kit.
- **Door nets** (`doorNetLeft`, `doorNetRight`, `poseUtvDoors`): a frame and mesh hinged on the rear post, swinging out 1.35 radians when the UTV is nearly stopped (under 0.5 m/s, eased) and shut when it moves, so the girls step in over the sill without walking through them.
- **Belts** (`buildBuckledBelt` in `detail/belts.js`): flat webbing with a fine twill and stitched edges (`ribbonGeometry` gained an opt-in UV), a steel D-loop bolted on the cage's bar, a reel housing in the seat's crease with a rounded cover and bolts, a steel tongue plate with the webbing's slot and the latch hole (`tongueGeometry`), and a buckle on a webbing strap with a slot, a steel frame and a red release button with an arrow. Stowed, the webbing hangs from the loop over the seat back's top and down its face with the tongue parked on a stop button; worn, the sash and lap runs end in the tongue's slot with the tongue standing just above the buckle.
- **Second round** (Jamie: the doors always open; the lamp bar on the roof not attached; the badge not central on the back; the exhaust through the frame; no windscreen; the mirrors, the wiper and the grab handle floating; the grille not showing; the belts too high; the handbrake not like a car's; the glovebox lid). **Doors**: they swing out for 2.4 s when you get on or off (the aboard state changes), and while a girl is stepping in, climbing down or walking up to climb (`UtvMount.boardingNow`), only while it is nearly stopped; otherwise shut. **Windscreen**: the glass was there at 14% opacity and could not be seen; it is 30% with a thicker frame, its foot is up on the dash's top (it started inside the dash), the mid bar I had added across the driver's view is gone. **Wiper**: a pivot on the dash's top at the glass's foot, a short arm and a blade on the glass (it was 0.6 m long, from nowhere). **Mirrors**: their foot is on the front post at its height. **Lamp plate**: 6 cm behind the rear hoop and bolted back to it by four stays. **Badge**: centred on the gate. **Grille and lamps**: the front body was covering the grille (it ended 4 cm proud of it) and the lamp housings stood out beside it with nothing to hold them; the body now widens in a nose block over the tyres' front (z 1.3 to 1.4, 1.46 wide) which carries the lamps at its corners and the grille on its face, with the badge on the lip above. **Exhaust**: the silencer sat on the rail; it is inboard of it now, between the rail and the transaxle, and the tailpipe runs over the rear cross member (moved back 5 cm). **Grab handle**: stood where the dash's top is not (the loft is lower out there); it and the cup holders now take their heights from `loftTop(MULE_DASH ...)`. **Belts**: the loop 6 cm lower and the sitter's hips taken 5 cm into the cushion in the belt's path. **Handbrake**: the kit's car-style lever with a grip and a release button, on a bracket on the sill with a boot, pulled up for on. **Glovebox**: a larger flush lid with a recessed handle and the warning sticker, in the dash's face. The dash's cluster and console are still the old ones.
- **Third round** (Jamie: the bumper textureless, doors should open at the affected side only and hinge at the front, belts still above the loop, indicator and reverse lamps to light like the brake lamp and look off until then, the rear lamp bar narrower with the lamps in a row, headlamps clashing with the bumper). **Doors**: `doorSwing.js` (pure, tested; `DoorSwing`) takes who is aboard at each side and where a girl is boarding (`UtvMount.boardingSides`, by each slot's door), and opens only that side's net, for 2.4 s after you get on or off or while she steps in, never above 0.5 m/s; it is written for the cars' and Land Rovers' doors to use when they move. The nets are hinged on the front post (they were hinged at the back) with the free end swinging out, and have two hinges. **Belts**: the stowed webbing's top ran above the loop (it went 0.5 m up from its parking place with nothing to stop it): it is capped 7 cm under the loop, and a test checks no stowed belt is higher than its loop. **Lamps**: the indicator is dark amber and the reverse lens smoked grey until lit (the signal code already lights them as it does the brake lamp, from the same supply), the rear plate is 0.98 m wide (was 1.12) with a row of three at each end, outer to inner the indicator, brake and tail, reverse. **Headlamps**: moved in 4 cm and up 7 cm, the grille with them, clear of the bumper's top (a test checks). **Bumper**: it has its own material with a moulded grain (a bump map), two ribs along its face, a flat for a number plate, a pocket each end and bolts through its top lip.
- **The dash and controls** (answers up front: the speedo with its odometer and the hours on a separate unit; keep F / N / R and leave out H / L range; the rockers stay in a row; we can see everything else as needed). `buildUtv` in `dash.js` is rewritten after the photos: one grey moulded binnacle (wider than the old pod, so the column shroud is 0.31 wide to carry it) with a round chrome-bezelled speedo to 40 mph in white on black with an orange needle and the odometer's drums, four lamp pockets (a bezel each: glow plug amber and charge on the left, oil and parking brake on the right), a strip under the speedo of the rest of the tell-tales (water too hot, seat belt, headlamp green or blue for main beam, both indicators, 4WD or diff lock), and a separate fuel and hours unit in its own bezel at the left end (five lit bars with E, 1/2, F under them over an LCD of the hours). The key is real geometry now (a bezel, a barrel and a key that turns a quarter with the key), at the right end. **The gear lever** is a bent stalk out of the console in a gaiter to a fat black knob, in a gate plate with three notches (F, N, R). Nothing the game drives has lost its place; the dash's 4WD note and gear column in the old LCD moved to the strip and the console. To stay in the triangle budget (80k) the springs have three turns, the tyres 14 blocks and the net mesh a 6 cm pitch. **Not seen**: the strip's symbols close up in play, the cluster from the driver's seat with a girl at the wheel, or the key's travel.

- **Third round on the dash** (Jamie: the indicator stalk's texture odd, the stowed belts wrong, the front body too wide, the key weird and wanting OFF, ON and START). **Stalk**: the symbol plane on its end is left off the UTV's (`addSignalStalk`'s `symbol: false`): a clean end. **Belts**: described properly this time: the webbing now runs from the D-loop on the rear bar (moved up to 1.42, above the seat back's top) over the top edge and flat down the backrest's face (it follows the backrest's lean) to the reel, which now stands out of the face low in the crease as the bottom anchor, with the tongue hanging on the webbing just above it; a test checks the webbing reaches the reel and goes no higher than its loop. Seen from the front, each hangs top to bottom. **Front body**: the nose block is 1.30 wide (was 1.46, 8 cm each side), the lamps 8 cm in and the bumper 12 cm narrower; the wheel arches stand out as before. **Key**: a flat blade with teeth in a chrome barrel and a wide black rubber bow with a ring, the binnacle 2.4 cm longer to the right for its legend; it has three positions, OFF at the top, ON half-way round and START at the end, eased to whichever the engine is doing (`cranking` is in the dash's state now), with OFF, ON and START printed round it.
- **Fourth round on the dash** (Jamie: the front body in to the lamps, the stalk's wear texture, the key turning the wrong way and START off the end). **Front**: the nose block is 1.04 wide, its edge the lamps' outer edge, and the lamps are at x 0.41, so each sits 4 cm from the grille's frame. **Stalk**: the symbol is back; what was wrong was the wear layer's pattern, which is fixed in the model's space and so stayed put as the stalk swung; the mud coat now leaves `signalStalk` clean (as it does the bars), on every vehicle that has one. **Key**: it turns clockwise as the driver sees it (it was anticlockwise); the legend is 11 mm further in with a smaller type so START ends inside the binnacle. Not seen: START printed on the render (the pillar hides that corner from my angles).
- **Fifth round, and the last of item 99's list** (Jamie: the lamps a little more inboard, the key starting on START, the mud not moving with the tipping bed, and yes to a bed cover, mud up the sides and wear on the cluster). **Lamps**: 18 cm wide now at x 0.385, 4 cm from the nose's edge. **Key**: it started at an angle because its head lay across (x) and not up (y); the head is up the key now, so at OFF it points up. **Mud on the bed**: the mud coat found its pattern from each vertex's position in the vehicle's space, so a part that swings moved through a pattern fixed there; a group can now say `userData.mudRest` (where it stands at rest, in the body's frame) and its meshes (and what hangs on them) take their pattern from their own coordinates plus that, so the dirt is stuck to them: the bed's at the hinge, the tailgate's at its own hinge. **Bed cover**: three hoops across the bed and a grey canvas sheet over them down to the rim with two straps, in the bed's frame (it tips with it), open at the tailgate end and stopping short of it so the tailgate opens and a girl sits on it; it covers what is in the bed, which has not been dealt with (a jerry can in the bed is hidden under it). **Mud up the sides**: dried splash bands (a texture of splats, thickest at their foot, with a few drips) on the bed's sides, its tailgate and the body's flanks, a millimetre off flat faces, the first two stuck to the parts that move. **Cluster wear**: yellowed, scratched glass and dust over the speedo and the fuel unit (the tractor's `agedGlass`), pitted bezels in the dull steel, a grimy lower edge and scuffs along the moulding's top. To fit the triangle budget the bed's floor ribs are five and the cover's hoops short.
- **Sixth round** (Jamie: the bed awful, remove it (taken to mean the bed cover I had just added: it is gone); the bed's mud still looks fixed in space). **The real cause of the mud**: my last change gave the swinging parts their own shader text, but three shares one compiled program between materials with the same `customProgramCacheKey`, and the key did not include the rest offset, so the bed was given the fixed pattern's program and nothing changed. The offset is in the key now, and `tests/mudRest.test.js` checks the swinging part gets its own copy, key and shader text. Looked at with the bed down and tipped: the dirt stays on the panel.
- **Seen**: renders of the front, both rear quarters, from the front through the cab and the belts stowed and worn from the front. **Not seen**: a girl or you in the seat with the belts on (the webbing's fit on a body), the door nets while a girl climbs in, the lamps lit, or the tailgate seat. **Not done**: the dash and console are the old ones (the cluster is still an LCD and the console pod unchanged), mud up the sides and a bed cover.

## The 200TDi as a Defender 90 at its real size (TODO 100, second half)

From the photos in `.scratch/quads/landrover200/` (a side-on, a front, a rear and a front three-quarter of a 90 station wagon, the dash from the seat, a close-up of the cluster, the back bench and the front seats). Settled up front with Jamie: **a Defender 90 station wagon**, with a rear bench kept though a real 90 wouldn't have one (no 110 with rear doors); **a tailgate like the photos, swinging open and shut on T**; **a new colour** (he disliked the faded pastel green): Atlantic Blue with the white roof of the photos; **different enough inside to look a more evolved version of the Series, but broadly similar**. Numbers are in `defenderBody.js`, the model in `defenderModel.js`, as the Series' are in `seriesBody.js` and `seriesModel.js`.

- **Size.** The 1.15 scale is gone (`DEFENDER_SPEC` lost `scale`) and it has its own layout (`DEFENDER_LAYOUT`: wheelbase 2.36 m, track 1.486 m, where the Series' is 2.23 and 1.31), used by `quadUnit.js`, the traffic view and the test helper. Measured: 3.877 x 1.818 x 1.950 against the real 3.883 x 1.79 x 1.963 (`REAL_SIZES.landrover200` was already right), all within 8%, so `landrover200` is in `IN_PROPORTION`. From the side photo with the wheelbase as the ruler: bumper face at z 1.82, rear door face at -1.82, the spare's cover out to -2.03, the roof cap 1.75 to 1.95 high, doors 0.92 m long hinged at 0.52, the screen's foot at (1.27, 0.58) raking 0.36 rad (the Series' is 0.27).
- **Body.** A black bumper with a rubber step; a black grille with slats between wings that each carry a black lamp surround (round headlamp, sidelamp above, indicator below), a louvre and a repeater on the wing's side; black wheel-arch flares (a half ring extruded 7.5 cm out, `flare` in `defenderModel.js`, not the Series' thin torus); the bonnet's raised ridge (a loft); the white roof cap as a loft with a dark alpine light along each side; a long window each side behind the door; the rear corner posts with a narrow window each, the lamp block on each (round red and amber, a clear reverse lamp on the nearside and a red fog lamp on the offside, a smoked or dark lens each until lit as the Series'); a black step under each door; coil springs and radius arms, not leaf springs. Small rounded boxes (radius under 7.5 mm) are plain boxes here, a third of the triangles each: the first build was 120k triangles against the budget's 100k, it is 82k now.
- **Doors.** The front doors are the Series' (groups hinged at the front, `poseSeriesDoors`, the same `DoorSwing` logic) with a black upright holding the glass's front edge and a trim card with a pull and a pocket. **The back door** (`backDoor`) is hinged on the offside edge (x -0.5; in the photo its hinges are on the right seen from behind) and carries the spare in its black cover (only the cover is drawn: the tyre under it was 4k triangles nobody sees). `poseDefenderBackDoor` swings it 1.75 rad out behind, so the spare goes with it. T uses the Series' `Tailgate` class (0.9 s, not above 0.8 m/s, thrown shut above 2.5 m/s), registered in the Tune tab as "200TDi back door"; `updateLandRover` runs the T key for either Land Rover. It does not carry cargo (item 185 is the Series' tub and, later, this load space).
- **Inside.** A moulded charcoal dash across the whole cab (the Series' is an open olive shelf over a gap): a slab from the screen's foot back to z 0.18, a straight fascia, a closeout panel down to the floor, demister grilles in its top and a padded roll along its back; the pod where the old `dash` fixed point is (so it did not move) with an eyebrow over it; the centre stack with five rockers over the radio (a head unit with orange digits, presets and a knob each end, in the clean materials) and two heater knobs; red-to-blue heater sliders at the driver's end; three rockers under the pod; a glovebox and a grab handle on the passenger's side. The wheel is a thick rim on two spokes with a big padded hub (the horn pad) carrying the badge. Levers on a moulded console (a big rubber gaiter on the gear lever, short ones, none reaches the dash), pedals hung on arms as the Series', the handbrake at x -0.22. The seats have bolsters and headrests on posts; the back bench, wheel boxes and a carpeted load floor are as before; all six belts are the kit's `buildSeatBelt`. A grey headlining under the white roof.
- **The cluster** (`buildDefender` in `dash.js`, after the photo): three small gauges in a row at the left (volts, water, fuel), the big speedometer at the right (to 110 mph, labelled every 20, white on black, orange needle, odometer), the warning symbols in two rows under the small gauges, the hazard button beside them. The rev counter is gone (the photo's cluster has none). The key is real and turns, the Series' `addIgnitionKey` moved into a shared helper. The volts gauge shows a steady reading running and a little less not.
- **Badges.** The shared badge says MOORLAND; the front strip, the wheel and the spare's cover carry it. **Fixed points**: none moved (seats, cameras, wheel, grips, pod, tow jaw and the rear bench's seats and cameras are where they were). **Cleaned up**: the Series' and the old 200TDi's branches in `landRoverModel.js` (about 500 lines) are gone; `buildLandRoverModel` only chooses. **Not done**: carrying things in the back (item 185), the radio's face close up at night, a bonnet that opens, rain on the glass or working wipers (items 175 and 210), the Series' remaining detail.
- **Second round** (Jamie: the grille and the spare fighting; the lamp surrounds too tall; the rear lamps between body and pieces; see-through boot; gaps at the screen, doors and the roof's back edge; the dash too deep with no foot room; headrests; the cluster's dials and lamps too close and out of line; belts on the rear window and no rear buckles; the rear arches). **Fighting**: the grille's panel face was on the bonnet's front plane (1.72) and its apron overlapped it (the bonnet now ends at 1.69, the apron sits under the panel); the spare's cover had its front face on the door's back face (it is 1.5 cm behind it, a stub bridging). **Lamp surrounds** 0.32 by 0.30 (were 0.34 by 0.36), inside the wing's width and under its top. **Rear lamps**: in a black block 0.12 wide on each corner post at the window's x, under it with a 5 cm gap, the round stop and tail lamp, round indicator and the reverse (nearside) or fog (offside) lamp evenly down it; the posts' windows are real openings now (panel in pieces) so you see through them. **Boot**: a solid block from the chassis up to a carpet floor at 0.91, level with the back bench's cushions (it was a 4 cm slab at 1.0 with air under it). **Gaps**: the door's upright and glass front edge follow the screen post's line (they were 5 cm behind it, the hinge being 6 cm ahead of the post's foot); a padded roll up to the screen's bottom rail covers the bulkhead's top; the roof cap's bottom edge is 3 cm into the body's rail with a squarer lower edge (a rounded one left a wedge at the rear corners where the door's top meets the body). **Dash**: a shelf 0.28 m deep (was 0.38), its face stopping at y 0.92 with nothing under it at the driver's end (the old closeout panel ran down to the floor in front of the pedals, which stood inside it), the glovebox and the stack coming down from it, a plastic lining on the bulkhead behind the pedals, whose arms reach it. **Headrests**: the front two are integral 0.30 by 0.20 pieces sunk into the backrests' tops (small floating ones on posts before); the back bench has none. **Cluster**: gauges 14 mm radius in a row 43 mm apart with their centres in line, the speedo 30 mm radius centred on the pod's middle (it was 36 and 4 mm from the top), the symbols in two rows of five in columns under the gauges. **Rear belts**: a C pillar (a solid panel 0.16 m wide across the side window at z -1.43) carries the upper loops, the reels sit on the wheel boxes, and the buckles are in the crease between cushion and backrest (they were behind the backrests, so hidden). **Rear arches**: I could not find a clash at rest (the tyre's top at ride height is 11 cm under the arch's, measured), so the flares now stop just above the horizontal rather than just below it, which clears the tyre's front and back edges from the side. **Do the centre stack's switches do anything?** No; like the Series' toggles they are for show. Wiring the hazards rocker to the hazards and the lights rocker to `P` is small and not done.
- **Third round** (Jamie: the rear wheels cut through by the arch, the gap at the screen, gaps behind the doors and at the back of the side windows, no rear plate, places with no mud or wear, wire the centre switches, does the battery gauge work, the same radio as the Series, no black panel under the glovebox, do the pedals move, fighting on the wing). I did not measure last time; this time each was measured. **Holes**: a new test fires a grid of rays at each side and the back from outside (the grid offset so no ray runs along a seam) and fails if one hits the inside instead of the skin. It found: a 10 cm hole between the B pillar's back face and the side window's front post (the window began at z -0.55, the pillar ended at -0.45, `DEF_SIDE_WINDOW.z0` is now the pillar's back face, and the front post is gone); a hole behind the window's rear post out to the rear face (the post is now from the window to the rear face); and, once those were fixed, pinholes at the corners of the door and the corner posts. The pinholes are why the last round did not fix anything you could see: **rounded boxes butted together leave a notch where two rounded corners meet, and where three do it goes right through**. The 200TDi's `rbox` is a plain box for any radius up to 12 mm now (watertight, and a third of the triangles each, so the model is smaller too), rounded only for the big soft things (seats, hub, pad). The Series has the same notches in places (the test's quick run on it found some at its B pillar); it is not changed, and says so here. **Rear arches**: the wheel box inside was a solid block (x 0.56 to 0.80, y 0.61 to 1.11) with the tyre (x 0.62 to 0.86, up to 0.96 at full bump) standing inside it, so its side face cut the tyre in two when you looked through the arch. It is a block with a round hole (`archBlock`, radius `DEF_ARCH.r` 0.47 centred where the wheel is at full bump, 7 cm of plastic over it); a test checks the numbers. **Screen and dash**: the padded roll is up to the screen's bottom rail (1.21 centre, 0.14 tall, the rail starts at 1.27). **Plate**: it was behind the tow mount (a 1 m wide beam behind the crossmember hid all but a yellow sliver); the mount is 0.24 wide and the plate on the crossmember's face at x 0.4 (`DEF_PLATE`). **Mud and wear**: the wear layer grimes creases, edges and nooks, so a flat panel with no neighbour has none, and the door (many overlapping pieces) was brown while the quarters, wings and roof were clean blue. The big merged paint mesh and the named body panels now have `wearNookMin` (a floor of patchy grime, `detail/wear.js`), 0.5 on the paint and 0.3 on black. **Live materials**: the mud coat copies every material, so a change the game makes to the original (a radio display lighting, the Series' sidelamps lighting with the headlights) never showed on the copy: `model.userData.liveMaterials` is a list the coat leaves alone, with both in it. (The Series' sidelamps were silently not lighting in the game since I made them light.) **Switches**: the stack's hazard, lights and radio rockers tip with the hazards, the lights (`lightLamps`) and the radio (`radioOn`), through the dash's own update; the other two on the row and the three on the face do nothing yet. **Volts gauge** reads the battery (`voltsFraction` in `dashState.js`, 11 to 13 V standing from flat to full, 14 V and a little running, on 8 to 16); the charge lamp worked already. **Radio**: one piece, `buildRadio('dab', ...)` in `detail/radios.js`, used by both Land Rovers (the Series' head unit as in its photo), its display dark until the radio is on with the key; a new kind of radio is one more style in `RADIO_STYLES`. **Pedals**: `addPedals` in `landRoverModel.js` (both Land Rovers) makes three groups pivoting at the top of their arms (`pedalClutch`, `pedalBrake`, `pedalAccel`) that `poseLandyControls` swings with the clutch key, the brake and the throttle. **Glovebox**: the bulkhead lining is only behind the driver's pedals; under the glovebox is open, for legs. **Wing**: the arch panel's top edge and the front face's were on the same plane as the flat top's face (1.06): 4 mm under it now (the Series' too).
- **Fourth round** (Jamie: the rear arch still cut, fighting at the back, sides and door pieces, a gap between the B pillar and the roof, the dash lights out of line, the black panel under the glovebox, the pedals the wrong way round, the wipers on the window). Two new checks, both in `tests/defenderModel.test.js`, because the renders could not show these. **Fighting**: `tests/helpers/coplanar.js` (`coplanarOverlaps`) finds every pair of axis-aligned faces of the model that face the same way, lie within 2 mm of one plane and overlap, keeping only those a ray from outside reaches first (so not those inside a joint), and the test fails on any over 8 cm2. It found: the roof cap's side against the body's rail; the alpine light's black frame against the cap's side (frame removed, the glass has a polygon offset); the scuttle's side against the wing's; the back door's rubber against its panel; the corner post's window frames; the door's capping, rear upright and rail, and the B pillar, rail and posts sharing outer faces (each is now a different thickness, 4 to 8 mm, so no two faces are on one plane); the roof is 4 cm wider (1.72) so its lower edge stands outside the rails. **Gap at the B pillar**: it stopped 4 cm short of the roof with the rail beside it only reaching the door; the pillar goes up into the roof and the rail runs on over it, and a second ray test covers y 1.685 to 1.735 at 1 cm steps. **Rear arch**: found by testing the model against the tyre volume (radius 0.37 at the wheel's rest and full-bump heights, 10.5 cm either side of its centre): the footwell's carpet (it ran back to z -0.8, inside the tyre's front edge) and the plastic trim panel I had added on the rear side under the window, which crossed the whole arch. The carpet stops at -0.78, the panel is gone, and a test keeps every body piece out of the tyres. **Dials**: all four on one centre line (y 52 on the pod), the speedometer r 28 (it was 30 and sat 6 mm off the others), the symbol panel moved down so its rows sit under the gauges' centres (x 82, 103.5, 125, 146.5, 168, the first four under the three gauges' centres and between them), the hazard button in the last column. **Glovebox**: what I removed last time was the box hanging under the dash and the lining behind it; what you saw was the glovebox's lid (a black rectangle) and the dash face above it. The lid is a flat part of the face now with nothing under it. **Pedals**: the clutch was at x -0.55, by the door, and the accelerator at -0.25, by the console, which is the left-hand-drive order. The driver sits at -0.42 on the right, so the clutch is at -0.25 (nearest the console, his left) and the accelerator at -0.55 (by the door). **Wipers**: the spindle is 3 cm up the bottom rail (the glass starts at 0.06), the arm 0.12 rad up onto the glass; the Series' too. The Series' sides and back have the rounded-box notches the 200TDi's had (the same ray test finds some at its B pillar and rear panel's edges); not fixed.
- **Fifth round** (Jamie: a gap at the back of the wheel arch inside the boot; the dials and lamp panel up and right; the symbols not centred on each other; the speedo right; fix the Series too). **Cluster**: dials up 3 and right 5 (centres y 49, the gauges at x 87, 130, 173, the speedo at 243), the symbol panel with them. The symbols were not centred because each is drawn off its own origin: the headlamps' (lights, main beam) ran from 2 to 8 mm right of it while the glow coil is centred, so each kind has a measured nudge (`nudge` in `buildDefender`) putting its drawn middle on the column's. **Boot**: the ray tests found no hole at the back of the arch itself (they did find a 5 mm slot between the rear seat box and the back panel at z -1.24 to -1.29, closed). The wheel box now runs 4 cm further back, into the corner post, in case the gap seen was behind it; if there is still one, say where. **The Series** has had the 200TDi's three checks run against it: it now builds its panels from plain boxes (no pinholes), and the ray tests of the sides and back and the coplanar faces test are in `tests/seriesModel.test.js`. They found: a 1 cm slot between the B pillar and the quarter glass above the sill (the glass now runs 2 cm into the pillar at each end); the door's capping, rear upright and the sill sharing outer faces with the door's panel; the scuttle's side sharing faces with the wing's and the deck; the dash shelf's lip sharing its front face with the shelf; the screen's side post sharing a face with the hinge pillar; the valance's edge with the wing's inner wall. All given different thicknesses or offsets of 4 to 12 mm.
- **Sixth round** (Jamie: the arch gap beside the rear seats is still there, the tail lamp block fights, huge gaps both sides). **Arch gap**: my last two fixes closed slots that were not it, and none of the ray tests could see it because they all ran from outside. It is the wheel arch seen from inside: the hole in the wheel box is 0.47 radius, the tyre 0.38 and standing at 0.45 to 0.58 centre height, so from the seats you look past the tyre through the body's arch (0.42) to the sky. A real car has a metal liner there. Each wheel box now has a thin half-pipe liner at radius 0.44 to 0.45 (clear of the tyre at full bump), x 0.56 to 0.80, over the tyre. **Fighting at the tail lamps**: I had extended the wheel box 4 cm back so its rear face (-1.84) was on the corner post's rear face (-1.84); the box now ends 1 cm short (-1.83). The window frame's bars across the post overlapped the side bars' faces at their corners (y 1.18 and 1.72): they are 4 mm deeper.
