The coplanar test missed the first because it only counted overlaps of 8 cm2 or more where this one was a strip. **Lesson**: the checks I wrote do not see anything from inside the cab; the first thing to do with a gap complaint is shoot a view from where the player is looking (the `.scratch/pp/defshot.mjs` camera's y is the model's less 0.62, which I had wrong for the first three tries).
- **Seventh round** (Jamie: the gap is still there, then: "it needs to be deeper, down behind the seats and to the boot floor"). That was the clue. The wheel box's hole (the cavity round the tyre) was open on its inner face, so from the boot and the seats you looked into it sideways: down behind the seats to the ground, and across the car above the tyre to the sky. The half-pipe liner I added before closed only its top. The box now has an inner wall, a thin plate over the whole inner face from the floor to the top and the length of the box (`archBlock`), so the cavity is enclosed; the liner and the disc I tried in between are gone. The check that found it: `.scratch/pp/gameray.mjs`, a ray from each seat's eye to every point of the side below the sills, in the game with the real tyres (they are added at run time, so unit tests had none): 4 to 17 see-through points per seat before, none after; `tests/defenderModel.test.js` has the same with a stand-in tyre. The wall's first version shared its top with the block's and its end with the post's rear face, which fought in the boot; it is now 4 mm under the top, clear at the ends and 7 mm inside the block's inner face. The window capping's faces and the tail lamp frame's bars were also within 3 mm of other faces; clear now, and a test covers the boot and arches down to 1 cm2.

## The trailers in full detail (TODO 101)

Done with no photos, at Jamie's say-so ("it should be straight-forward for the pair of them"), so from what was already there and what such trailers look like, with the numbers kept: every seat, cargo spot, ramp, winch, hitch ring height and collision footprint is where it was (a test checks the seats and spots).

- **Size** (`TRAILER_SIZES` in `realSizes.js`, a test within 8%): the bale trailer stands for a 10 ft by 6 ft 6 in single-axle farm flatbed (4.47 m overall, 2.56 over the tyres, 1.55 to the top of the headboard, 0.84 m tyres) and the little one for a 5 ft by 3 ft 8 in tipping quad trailer with a ball coupling (2.79 by 1.58 by 1.08, 0.6 m tyres). They are what the models measured already, so nothing is resized; the figures are typical, not from a particular maker.
- **Shared parts** (`trailerParts.js`): the wheel is the kit's tyre (`treadTyre`, 'farm' and 'atv') on a pressed-steel rim with lips, hub, grease cap, valve and four nuts (`boltsMesh`); the lights board is two of the kit's tail lamps with triangle reflectors, a plate and brackets; the jockey wheel has its clamp, tube and collar, fork, treaded wheel and handle; `addChain` (links, alternate ones turned), `addCable` (`cableGeometry`), `addLeafSpring` (arc, shorter leaves, eyes, centre bolt) and `addUBolts`.
- **Bale trailer**: ladder of rails and cross members under the planks, an angle round the bed's edge, coach bolts at three cross members on every other plank, stakes in pockets bolted through, a tube headboard (posts, four rails, two diagonals, a top plate) with a dark board behind it, over-run coupling with a damper tube, a handbrake lever and quadrant with its cable, a breakaway cable, a safety chain, the lights' lead and socket, a chassis plate. **Tipping trailer**: the pin through its hinge lugs, a catch with its release lever and the bar it hooks, a gate hinge pin, bolts through the front posts, a ball coupling (head, cup, locking handle), chain, lead, plate, leaf springs on U-bolts, the lights board on the chassis behind the tub.
- **Mud and wear** (`effects/trailerCoat.js`, new): the quads' `MudCoat` on a trailer's model with a stand-in for the quad it expects (the frame falls back to the model itself where there is no `bodyRoot`), its own mud state fed by the world's mud and water under its own two wheels (`updateMudBuildUp` at the quads' rates, speed along it, nothing thrown standing still), wear 0.5 on the bale trailer and 0.35 on the little one, baked a piece a frame with the vehicles' (`game.bakeWear`). Settable in the Tune tab ("Bale trailer wear", "Bale trailer mud", "Tipping trailer wear", "Tipping trailer mud"). **Not done**: hosing them down, keeping their mud from day to day (`farmState.js`), a trailer left muddy by the tractor that pulled it (it only picks up its own).
- **Triangles**: the budget for each trailer is 18k (it was 15k, models were 4k to 6k): the bale trailer is 16k and the little one 11k, mostly tyres (1.9k each), plank bolts (1.4k) and the lamps' fluted lenses. Parts are merged (`mergeStatic`) except the ones the game poses (ramps, tub, gate, seats, wheels). The tyres are 12 blocks round, not the cars' 26.
- **Fighting**: the same coplanar test as the Land Rovers' (`tests/helpers/coplanar.js`) runs on both: it found the stakes sharing a face with the capping, the lamps' board with the frame behind it, the ramps' lips with their plank's edge, the tub's capping with its posts and with the gate's rail, the front wall with the sides, the coupling's head with its body.
- **Second round** (Jamie: the small trailer's front metalwork bland; the "spring" under its hitch not joined to anything; the bale trailer's hitch not joined by metalwork and its bars the wrong way; mudflaps not joined; the plate unsupported; chevrons pointing down; the ramps swinging round and through the floor). **Bland**: the A-frame, coupling and head are galvanised steel (`galvanised()` in `trailerParts.js`: a canvas of zinc spangle, scratches and dull patches), with gussets bolted at the coupling, bolted plates where it meets each rail and a cross brace. **The "spring"** was the safety chain, hanging in the air from nothing: each end is now a welded eye plate with a ring on the metal (`addEye`), the chain between them. **Bale trailer's A-frame**: the bars were yawed the wrong way (opening towards the front) and started short of the rails; two beams (`beam()`) now run from the front of each main rail, narrowing to the coupling, with a plate at each rail end, a cross brace, gussets; the little trailer's the same, from its rails. **Mudflaps** hang from a bracket plate clamped behind the guard's rear lip (they were 13 cm below it and 4 behind), bolted, down to 12 cm off the ground. **Plate**: a steel holder behind it with a lip to rest on, hung from the lights board on two stays. **Chevrons**: the arms' yaw ran the wrong way round, so the V's apex pointed forward at the top of the tyre and DOWN at the front of it; flipped for every chevron kind in the kit (`atv`, `balloon`, `tractor`, `allTerrain`), so the quads' and the UTV's changed with it. **Ramps**: they were built to swing from lying forward under the bed, which took them down through the deck, and with their cleats on the underside once open. They lie on the bed (visible, face up, behind the bales), slide back until their upper ends are at the back edge (t to 0.6), then drop at the free end (0.6 to 1), never below the deck on the way (`poseRamps`, a test checks it); the closing is the same in reverse.
- **Third round** (Jamie: the bale trailer's left tail lamp should be the right one's mirror image; the ramps need a better texture). The lamp cluster is asymmetrical (red on one half, amber over clear on the other) and the left board position was a copy, so its red was outside; its scale.x is -1 now, red inside on both (this also mirrors the little trailer's board). **Ramps**: the walking face is chequer plate (`chequerPlate()` in `trailerParts.js`: lozenge bars in rows a quarter turn apart, lit on their tops and shaded below, brushed aluminium between, scuffs; repeating every 12 cm along a plane laid on the plank under its lips).
- **The winches** (Jamie: the bale trailer's was bad, check the green quad's). Both were a drum and a lump: the trailer's a box with a cylinder through it, the green quad's a drum under a flat black slab with a sparse spring for rope, a tiny hook. One shared piece now (`detail/winches.js`, `buildWinchUnit` and `addWinchHook`): a base plate with the housings' feet, the drum on its shaft between two flanges wound with rope (rings in two layers, a strand-lay texture), a gearbox housing with its bolted flange and the free-spool lever, the motor can with end cap and two terminal studs, tie rods over the drum, a solenoid box with leads to the motor and the battery, and the rope out to a hook stop and a hook. The green quad's keeps its hook and fairlead fixed points (a test checks), its roller fairlead and mounts (the slab cover over the drum is gone, so the drum shows). The trailer's is the same at one and a half times the size, on a plate bolted to the deck, with a hawse plate its rope runs out through; `winchEye` and the name `winch` are kept. The trailers' budget is 22k (the bale trailer 21k with the winch).

## The Fiesta as a Mk1 at its real size (TODO 102)

From the photos in `.scratch/quads/fiesta/` (a yellow side-on, red, blue, beige and turquoise three-quarters and front and rear views, the dash from the seat and the cluster close up, the front seats, the back bench, the boot with the hatch up). Settled up front with Jamie: **the 1.15 scale removed** (get it right instead: `FIESTA_SPEC` lost `scale`, its wheel radius is 0.27 and rest length 0.16); **the hatch lifts on `T`** (the Land Rovers' `Tailgate` logic); **the new checks run on it from the start** (rays at the sides, back and front, coplanar faces, nothing in the tyres, nothing seen through from the seats); **brown inside, very 70s, and a little brown outside too** (the body is a 70s brown, 0x7a4f30, where it was a faded pale blue). The Corsa is next, when its photos arrive; its code in `carModel.js` is untouched (the Fiesta's branches there are unreachable now and go when the Corsa gets its own model).

- **Files.** `fiestaBody.js` (numbers: `FIESTA_BODY` is `CARS.fiesta`, so the layout, stand, proxy and eye functions did not change), `fiestaModel.js` (`buildFiestaModel`, `poseFiestaHatch`), `carModel.js` delegates to it for `'fiesta'`. Every fixed point is where it was (a test and `fixedPoints.json` check).
- **Size.** 3.65 x 1.63 x 1.45 against the real 3.565 x 1.567 x 1.36 (wheelbase 2.29): each within 8% (`landrover200` and `fiesta` are in `IN_PROPORTION` now). The height is 6% over because the body stands 5 cm on its wheels and the roof is crowned.
- **Body.** The side was one extruded panel with the arches cut in; it is now the front wing (nose to the door's hinge) and the rear quarter (the door's rear edge to the tail), with the door in a group of its own between them, hinged on its front edge (`doorLeft` and `doorRight`: `poseSeriesDoors`, the same `DoorSwing`, opening to 1.1 rad), carrying its glass in its rubber, the chrome handle, the door mirror (a square chrome housing at the front top corner, on the door), the shut lines, and a brown card inside with its arm rest and pull, map pocket, winder and lock button. Black sill moulding between the arches, a thin black moulding along the waist, a repeater on the wing, a fuel flap on the right quarter, mudflaps. Square-edged panels throughout (plain boxes up to a 12 mm radius), as the 200TDi's.
- **Front and back.** Black slatted grille with the maker's oval (Thorne) between two rectangular headlamps with their indicators under and round the corner, a black and chrome bumper with the plate and Amy's L plate, slot vents at the bonnet's rear edge; at the back the tall tail lamps in the body's corners (stop and tail over the indicator and the reverse or fog lamp, dark until lit as the Series'), the black bumper with chrome ends, the cargo sill with its seal. **The hatch** is one group on the roof's rear edge (the glass in black rubber, the deck, the tail panel to the cargo sill, the pull, the rear wiper and the plate): `poseFiestaHatch` swings it 1.3 rad up and back and stretches and aims two gas struts (`hatchStrutLeft`, `hatchStrutRight`, from the C pillars' insides) between the body and it. T uses the `Tailgate` class, 0.9 s, not above 0.8 m/s, thrown shut above 2.5 m/s, "Fiesta hatch" in the Tune tab.
- **Inside** (brown vinyl and check cloth, Jamie's 70s): the seats are brown vinyl (a canvas of grain with a pleat seam across it) with a tan and brown check cloth cushion cover and back inset and vinyl bolsters, the back bench the same with two check panels; carpet, a beige headlining with two visors, brown door cards, a parcel shelf of brown board, a carpeted boot with side trim over a solid block and the arch humps inboard of the tyres. The dash is the black moulding with its long shallow tray along the passenger's half (the photos'), the binnacle's hood, vents at the ends, a centre stack with the cassette radio, a bank of rocker switches under it (the hazard switch first and the radio's last are wired: they go down with the hazards and the radio; the three between are for show) and the heater's sliders. The wheel is a thin black rim on two low spokes with a big hub carrying the oval; the pedals are right-hand drive (`addPedals`, the clutch by the tunnel) and move with the clutch key, the brake and the throttle (`poseCarControls`, shared with the Corsa); the key is in the column's right side and turns (`addIgnitionKey`, now exported from `dash.js`). The belts are the kit's `buildSeatBelt`.
- **Cluster** (`buildFiesta` in `dash.js`, after the photo): a brushed silver panel in a black moulding; the speedometer on the left (to 100 mph, km/h inside in a smaller ring, the odometer under the boss, a yellow needle), the gear lever's H (R 1 3 over 2 4) in a black window in the middle over a block of slots and five lamps (oil, charge, indicators, main beam, choke), the round combined gauge on the right (fuel above, water below, a black boss, a yellow end on the hot side). No rev counter. The panel is 240 by 72 mm at a scale of 1.5 where the old brown one was 208 by 92 at 1.35.
- **Radio.** `RADIO_STYLES.cassette` in `detail/radios.js` (a silver faceplate, tuning scale, cassette door, push buttons, two knobs), the shared piece in its second style; the scale lights amber while the radio is on. It is a `liveMaterials` entry, so the mud coat leaves its display alone.
- **Checked.** Tests (`tests/fiestaModel.test.js`): the doors, the hatch and its struts, the pedals, the radio and rocker and key, holes in the sides below the belt and through the cabin up to the roof, in the back and the nose, faces fighting (over 8 cm2), nothing of the body inside the tyres at rest or at full bump, nothing seen through below the sills from any seat with stand-in tyres. They found: the fuel flap built 20 cm wide (width 1.70 until fixed), the rear quarter trim and the boot's arch hump passing through the rear tyres, the sill moulding and the vents sharing faces. **Second round** (Jamie: metal going through the rear wheel): the carpeted floor was the full width of the car all the way back and cut through the bottom of both rear tyres, and so did the boot's floor block. The floor is full width only as far as z -0.75 now, then 1.1 m wide between the rear tyres (as the boot's block is); the tyre test samples each triangle more finely (8 by 8) and checks the tyre's centre height at rest as well as at bump, so it would have seen it. **Third round** (Jamie, 14 points): the body's lower edge now rises from the arch to the nose and the tail (0.31 m, level with the bumper's top: `NOSE_LOW`) instead of running straight at the sill; each door has a steel frame round the whole glass (front upright up the raked edge where the tube was, top rail, rear upright); the wheels have a steel disc and hub on their inner side (they were hollow from behind); the tail lamps are small (0.22 by 0.2) and live in the hatch's lower corners so they lift with it (the hatch is 1.45 wide, inside the quarters, and the old lamp housings that stood proud of the corners and above the tail are gone); the boot's carpet, floor block and arch trim had faces within 3 mm of each other (the fight at the carpet's edge: widths 1.12, 1.1 and 1.06 now, the humps and the arch trim off each other and off the inner wings) and the fighting test is tighter (3 mm, 3 cm2); the rear seat's back is 0.4 tall and low (it was 0.55 and went up through the parcel shelf, and the atlas on it), the shelf is 0.3 deep at z -1.32, and the atlas is a thin book with a drawn cover (blue, title, a map's lines) lying on it, short of the glass; the big block behind the gear lever is a slim stack panel (0.24 by 0.26 by 0.1); the cluster's fuel and water symbols are in the middle of the gauge, above and below its boss; the choke is out of the radio's way (on the driver's right of the column); the cluster's choke lamp is the handbrake lamp now (the choke is visual only); the black bars across the doorway with a door open were the body-side moulding running the whole length of the car: it is in three pieces (wing, door, quarter) and the door's goes with the door. **Fourth round** (Jamie: floating front belts, the shelf above the seat tops, a hole under the boot floor, the atlas flickering out, the seats too high): the front belts' loops are on the inside of the B pillar (the glasshouse's half width at the loop's height less the pillar's radius, z on the pillar) and their reels low on the quarter's inner face just behind the door; the parcel shelf is gone (it was above the seat backs; the boot is open to the cabin) and the atlas lies on the rear seat's cushion, opaque and 4 mm above its book's top (the label was transparent and 0.5 mm over it, so it dropped out); the boot's carpet and block run to 1.5 cm short of the tail (they stopped 6 cm short, leaving a slot to see the ground through); the front seats' shells are 0.5 tall (0.68). **Fifth round** (Jamie: a gap behind the rear wheels in the boot floor, the choke invisible, black metal through the boot's sides, struts wrong and through the glass): the boot's carpet and block were 1.1 wide behind the tyres (their rear edge is at z -1.415) and now span the boot (1.46 and 1.42 wide, from z -1.42 to the tail); the choke was behind the wheel's rim from the seat (a rendered view from the driver's eye shows it now: a black knob with a pale face just outboard of the rim, 0.32 m from the column); the black metal was the back seat belts, whose loops and reels floated in the quarter window and whose reels sat in the tyre tops (the loop is on the C pillar's inner face now, the reel on the quarter's inner face above the arch at y 0.74); the struts' hatch ends were on the hinge, so they hardly changed length and lay along the pillar through the glass: they are on the glass frame a third of the way down, and stretch from 0.14 m shut to 0.52 open. **Sixth round** (Jamie: fighting in the boot's corners; black posts at the boot's sides that were not the belts; the choke catching a door trim): the posts were the boot's side trim, whose length was a slip (`c.tail * -1 - 1.4 - 0.2` is 0.04 m): a 3 by 4 cm post 0.5 tall on each side; it is a panel from the seat back to the tail now (found by ray-picking from the screenshot's pixels: `.scratch/pp/pick.mjs` prints what a pixel hits, which the belt-hiding guess would not have). The corner fighting was the block's bottom face with the inner wings' (0.3), the arch roofs' rear faces with the inner wings' (-1.52) and the fuel flap with the waist moulding (all offset now; a check of the boot with the hatch up to 4 mm finds nothing over 2 cm2). The choke is 3 cm further in and 5 cm higher, clear of the door card's arm rest. **Seventh round** (Jamie: the boot's back corners stick through the closed hatch; the wheel centres look missing): found by ray-picking the screenshot's pixels again: the boot side trim (x 0.70 to 0.73, to z -1.60) showed through the 2 cm slot between the hatch (half width 0.725) and the quarters' inner faces (0.7475). The trim is 3 cm further in and stops 8 cm short of the tail, and the hatch spans to the quarters (1.49 wide). The wheels' centres were only nuts on a flat disc: each has a raised steel boss and a domed chrome cap now. **Eighth round** (Jamie: the driver's side wheels still wrong): the kit's `buildRoadWheel` with `side: -1` mirrors its offsets but not its lathed face's winding, so the driver's wheels showed a black, dished-through face. The wheel is built for +x and turned half a turn about the vertical for the driver's side (I had only looked at the passenger's side before). **Not done**: the arch's shape is the same arch with a lower edge change (it is not a flared lip as in the photos).
**Not done**: the Corsa's own model; a bonnet that opens; wipers that work and rain on the glass (items 175 and 210); the speed of the hatch's struts; the three rockers that do nothing; a spare wheel under the boot floor; the headlamps' lenses are dark until the lights are on (as the Series'); the pillars are tubes, not the real section.

## The Corsa as a Corsa B at its real size (TODO 103)

From the photos in `.scratch/quads/corsa/`. Same decisions as the Fiesta's: **the 1.15 scale removed** (`CORSA_SPEC` lost `scale`; wheel radius 0.274, rest length 0.16), **doors that open**, **the hatch lifts on `T`** (`Tailgate`, "Corsa hatch" in the Tune tab), **the new checks from the start**. Character kept: red gone pink, Jess's loud additions.

- **Files.** `corsaBody.js` (numbers: `CORSA_BODY` is `CARS.corsa`), `corsaModel.js` (`buildCorsaModel`, `poseCorsaHatch`); `carModel.js` delegates to the right model for each car and the old shared builder is gone (`sideShape` and the layout helpers stay). `quad.js` picks the hatch pose from `model.userData.carKind`. Fixed points did not move.
- **Size.** 3.77 x 1.63 x 1.47 against the real 3.729 x 1.608 x 1.42 (wheelbase 2.44): `corsa` is in `IN_PROPORTION`. The screen top and roof edge were lowered to 1.37 and 1.35 so that, on the 5 cm ride lift and the crowned roof, the height is 3% over.
- **Body.** Wing, door and quarter as the Fiesta's, a smooth spline upper line, the left wing in grey primer, black plastic arch trim (a half ring 1.6 cm proud), sills, bumpers (deep, wrapping the corners but stopping short of the tyres) and large rounded door mirrors; faded paint on the roof and bonnet. Headlamps are pale lenses in black housings with the amber indicators round the corner, the grille a slim slot with the maker's round badge (Ashby). The hatch is one group on the roof edge carrying the glass, the spoiler, the sticker, wiper, tall tail lamps and the yellow plate; two struts. A big chrome exhaust under the right of the bumper.
- **Inside.** Jess's sports buckets (charcoal cloth with a fine weave and red threads in black vinyl bolsters, headrests built in; first made with lurid zigzags, changed after Jamie's first look), a grey fleck rear bench with a plain band, a charcoal rounded dash with louvre vents at both ends, a centre stack (clock, hazard switch between two vents, three heater knobs, the shared radio piece in a new `headunit` style with an orange display that lights with the radio), a lights knob that turns with the lights, a small three-spoke wheel, a real key in the column (as the Fiesta's), a billet gear knob, the kit's pedals in chrome (`addPedals` gained `padMat`), the kit's belts, the boot with the bass box. Boot floor and block are 1.2 wide between the rear tyres and full width only behind them.
- **Cluster** (`buildCorsa`): tacho left, speedometer right (to 130 mph), fuel and water stacked between, lamp strip along the bottom; the canvas key went with the real one.
- **Checked.** `tests/corsaModel.test.js` (the Fiesta's set: parts, doors, hatch and struts, pedals, radio and key, rays at sides, back and nose, faces fighting, nothing in the tyres, nothing seen through from the seats). They found the bumper corners sharing faces with the wing and inside the front tyres, the arch trim sharing a plane with the body-side moulding, and the boot floor inside the rear tyres. A render found the headlining poking through the roof's edges (the roof is a thin crowned sheet). Seen: front, rear and side views and the dash from the driver's seat; not seen: the hatch open, the key turning, night lights, the girls getting in.
- **First look, nine points** (Jamie): *rounder bodywork* (the wing, quarter and door skins lean in towards the roof by 0.05 rad about the sill, `TILT`, as a group, and the hatch's tail panel and deck are drawn in to follow; the bonnet is a crowned loft that narrows round the nose in plan; the roof is crowned and flush with the rails, the whole glasshouse 4 cm lower to pay for it, the screen top moved forward 5 cm to keep the glass as far from your eye as the Land Rovers'); *rear lamps* lower (0.56 to 0.84) and the reverse (left) and fog (right) lamps are in the same unit under the indicator, not in the bumper; *exhaust* a double-skinned chrome can (outer wall seen from both sides, a dark liner, a rolled lip, a baffle) so you cannot see through it; *bumper* is cut away for it (two long pieces and a short piece over the gap); *headlamps* lose the black box behind them: big clear lenses bulging out of the nose with a silver bowl drawn on the face; *badges* the grille slot is taller than the badge now, and there is one on the tail above the pull; *seats* are the Recaro-like sports ones; *cluster* is centred in its housing (dials shifted 13 mm) with cheeks under the hood so it sits in a pocket (its place is a fixed point, so it did not move); *lights controls* the knob and two fog rockers are in a plate at the driver's end of the dash under the end vent, as the photo. I read "left/right lights at the far end of the dash" as the lights' switches and the end vents; **not sure that is what was meant**.
- **Second look, ten points** (Jamie, with the photos in the folder I had not opened: `2229789...` red front three-quarter, `684486...` purple rear, `skeu4...` rear bench, `corsaglsint.webp` the dash from the seat). *Front*: the wing slabs ran to the nose and stuck out past a bonnet that narrowed to a dome, with a grey slab end behind the lamps. The wing stops 0.28 m short of the nose now (`WING_END`) and a crowned-free `loft` apron, rounded in plan (`planHalf`, 0.22 radius) and running up under the bonnet's edge, closes the front from bumper to bonnet; the bonnet narrows by the same rule; the indicator is a small lens low on the corner, not a ball. *Trim overlapping*: the body-side moulding ran through the arch trim on the wing and the quarter (the wing's is gone; the quarter's stops short of the arch), the front bumper corners sat across the arch trim (they start behind it now) and the rear ones are gone. *Roof*: it ran 10 cm past the header (ends 3 cm past the rails now); the headlining is a crowned sheet 3 mm under the roof's underside (it was a flat board 12 cm down, floating when seen from outside); the visors hang under it; the back rail is closed across the top of the hatch and every rail end has a ball over it, so no tube ends in the air at the back window. *Tail lamps* are tall teardrops of bright red at the corners, bulging and wrapping, with a dark outline, a break across and an amber lens at the foot with the small reverse (left) or fog (right) lens in it, as the photo. *Cluster* is redrawn after the dash photo: the rev counter and the speedometer side by side in the middle, the small fuel gauge at the lower left and the small water gauge at the lower right, the **indicators' tell-tales at the two ends** (each flashes with its own side), the other lamps in small pods (charge and oil left, beam and side lights right, engine, handbrake and belt under the dials), and drawn 1.8 cm lower inside its group (the group's place is the fixed point, so the dials are lower in the pocket but the checks hold). *Lock*: the key's barrel is on the column's centre side, where the dash photo shows it; I could not tell for certain this is what was meant. *Buckles* are in the tunnel gap beside each front seat, 5 cm above the bolsters, where you can see them.
- **Third look, five points** (Jamie). The "metal overlap" at the front arch was the arch roof's black box (0.26 wide) poking out through the leaning wing (ray-picked from the render: `.scratch/pp/pickc.mjs`); it is 0.2 wide and well inside now. The arch trim runs down to where the arch meets the sill line, so no sliver of paint shows under its ends. Headlamps are black glass when off like every other vehicle's (the pale lens and its drawn reflector bowl are gone), and the indicators are bigger amber lenses low on the front corners. The rear bumper's two long pieces are rounded (0.06). A header piece, its top following the roof's underside, closes the gap above the screen. The cluster's tell-tale boxes, lamp pods and small gauges are 8 mm or more inside the binnacle's edge.
- **Fourth look, five points** (Jamie). The cluster's bottom lamp strip is 6 px higher (it touched the binnacle's edge). The headlamp lenses are 30% shallower and 1 cm further back. The roof's back edge has a header over the hatch's top rail, as the front's. The rear side windows: the quarter glass ran only a third of the way to the hatch with a wide painted panel behind it; it runs to 0.1 m of the hatch's rail now (as the purple car's photo) with a slim swept pillar behind it, and the hatch's edge trim is a thin 14 mm rod in the pillar's face instead of a 37 mm pipe standing out of it. **The clock** was a picture drawn once at 12:45: it redraws with the game's dash clock (`state.clock`, the same one the Land Rovers' and the UTV's dials use) when the minute changes, and goes dark with a flat battery (a test checks both).
- **Fifth look, three points** (Jamie). The trim hanging out at the back was the hatch edge's thin rod and the C pillar panel's lower corner, both ending in the air above the quarter's curved top edge; the rod stops 12% short and the panel's corner comes down to the body's line. The short bumper piece over the exhaust was a plain box that took no wear like its neighbours; it is rounded (0.05) like them. The cluster's right-hand lamp pod: the two glyphs sat against the pod's right edge; the pods are 36 wide with smaller glyphs (0.68) nudged for the beam's rays and the oil can's drip so each reads as centred in its half.
- **Sixth look, the rear side windows** (Jamie: "you're missing a lot"). My checks had been blind to it: the side-hole test counted a hit on the far side's glass as a hit. The new test (`no gap round the glass`) fires rays at the rear quarter and door windows from six angles (square on, above, below, from the front and the back) and fails unless the first thing hit is on the near side (x past 0.55). It failed at the back edge of the quarter glass: the C pillar panel stands 1.6 cm proud of the glass (the glass is in the lean plane, the panel is outside it) and their edges met flush, so there was a slit you could see through at an angle. The panel's front edge laps 3 cm over the glass now. The "trim overlapping above the curve" was the body-side moulding on the quarter (the photos show it on the door only: gone) and the quarter's foot seal running on to the curved corner (shorter now, in the glass's plane).
- **Seventh look, the rear side windows again** ("you have fixed neither"). I had been guessing; this time I painted the glass opaque magenta in the running game (`EVAL` in `.scratch/pp/cshot.mjs` sets every 0.3-opacity material to magenta) and shot it from outside and in. The quarter glass stopped 3 cm under the roof rail, leaving a dark slit along its whole top edge (the earlier test never reached that high: its rays stopped at y 1.3, the glass top is at 1.33). The glass's top edge follows the rail's own slope up into it now, and the gap test covers the full window height. The rod along the hatch's edge, whose cut end hung out over the quarter's curved top, is gone (the hatch glass's rubber already marks that edge), and the C pillar panel's back edge laps 3 cm over that rubber as its front edge laps the glass.
- **Not done**: the arches are still a half ring, not a flared lip with its own panel; the headlamps' lenses do not wrap round the corner as far as the photo's; I have not seen the new buckles in a render (the roof was in the way of my camera) and the lights' pod at the driver's end is as before.

## The Series III at its real size (TODO 100, first half)

From the photos in `.scratch/quads/series/` (a side-on, a front three-quarter, the tub and cab from behind, the cab's inside). Settled up front with Jamie: **a Series III 88 pick-up**, **the 1.15 scale removed** ("do this all properly this time"; the 200TDi keeps its scale until its own round), **the white roof kept as a truck cab**, **doors that open**, **a tub whose tailgate drops on `T` and takes cargo**, colour unchanged (Bronze Green).

- **Its own model** (`seriesBody.js` the numbers, `seriesModel.js` the builder; `buildLandRoverModel` hands the Series to it and still builds the 200TDi). The old Series branches in `landRoverModel.js` are now unreachable: clear them when the 200TDi is rebuilt.
- **Size**: the old model was 3.90 x 2.01 x 1.82 and then scaled up 15%. It is 3.69 x 1.76 x 1.95 now (real 3.64 x 1.68 x 1.97, wing mirrors left out as the makers' figures do), and `landrover` is in `IN_PROPORTION`. The spec lost `scale`; `wheelRadius` is 0.38 and `suspensionRestLength` 0.2, the model's own. `REAL_SIZES.landrover` is a Series III 88 hard-top (3640 long). `measureModel` now skips parts named `mirror`.
- **Where things are** (worked from the side photo with the wheelbase, 2.235 m, as the rule): the doors' front edge is 0.7 m behind the front axle (the old model had it 0.54), so the bonnet is 1.2 m long; the wing tops are 1.12 m with the bonnet's crown at 1.2 and a scuttle deck behind it with two vent flaps; the screen rakes back 0.27 rad; the roof stands 1.94 m up; the tub is 1.04 m long with its sides level with the bottom of the door glass (1.27 m) and its floor at 0.92. The grille is recessed in a body-colour panel between the wings and the headlamps sit in the wings' faces with the sidelamp above and the indicator below, outboard. The bumper is galvanised, with the number plate on its face. The spare wheel stays on the bonnet, over its rear half as in the side photo.
- **Fixed points moved on purpose** (regenerated, only `landrover` changed): `dash` 0.17 m back (z 0.31, was 0.48) because the bulkhead is 0.17 m further back; the wheel, seats, pedals and levers did not move, so the wheel stays about 0.47 m ahead of the hips as Jamie had it. `towHitch` z -1.84 (was -2.02, the tub is shorter). The choke and hand throttle come out of the dash's new face (`userData.knobZ`, which `poseLandyControls` reads).
- **Doors** (`doorLeft`, `doorRight`, `poseSeriesDoors`): groups hinged on their front edge, 1.25 radians out, driven by the same `DoorSwing` as the UTV's nets (the left or right side only, for 2.4 s after you get in or out and while a girl boards, never above 0.5 m/s). The glass fits against the screen's raked side post, which stays on the body. `mudRest` keeps the dirt with them.
- **The tub** (`tailgate`, `Tailgate` in `tailgate.js`, `poseSeriesTailgate`): the gate is a group hinged on the floor's rear edge, `T` drops it flat (0.9 s, not while moving faster than 0.8 m/s; it swings shut if you drive off above 2.5 m/s) and its two chains are stretched and aimed between the pins on the tub and its corners. Six cargo spots (`bedCargo0..5`) sit on the floor between the wheel humps and a `bedProxy` box lets you look in and put something there: the UTV's loading (`addBedCargo` in `game.js`, shared by both, one `CargoHold` each). Nothing tips out, so it is not item 185 yet: no girl loads or unloads it, and the farm does not remember what is in it.
- **Second round** (Jamie: the wing and the panels behind the door overlapped and flickered, the handbrake and gear levers went through the dash, the dash pod was hidden behind the wheel and should be part of a metal dash the whole width of the cab, the choke went through the wheel, the rear glass stopped short). Side panels now tile: the hinge pillar, the scuttle's side, the wing side, and behind the door the B pillar, quarter and rear post each start where the last ends, so no two share a face. The dash is one raked metal fascia (`SERIES_DASH`, 0.45 rad back) with the instrument pod set into its face, a padded roll along its top against the bulkhead, the glovebox lid, the badge and switches; the wheel has three spokes (left, right, down) so the dials show through its open top. The levers are 0.2 m further back and shorter (gear 0.3, handbrake 0.29 in all) so none reaches the fascia; the choke and hand throttle come out of its face at x 0.2 and 0.29, clear of the wheel's rim. The rear window's glass fills the opening from the lower panel to the bar above. **Fixed point**: `dash` moved again (the pod is in the fascia now, 0.11 m from where it was); the levers are not fixed points.
- **Third round** (Jamie: the door's glass held by nothing at the front, the wipers poor, the choke and hand throttle coming out of the glovebox, the dash should be straight, olive and have a big gap under it, where the radio went, and the Land Rover badges). **Door**: a raked body-colour upright down the front of the window, on the door, lying just behind the screen's post (its front half is inside the post, so they never share a face); it holds the glass's front edge and swings with the door. **Wipers**: the kit's `buildWiper` (a rounded arm and a rubber blade) parked along the foot of the glass from a spindle by the centre post, in place of the two thin tubes. **Dash** (`SERIES_DASH`, after `sddefault.jpg` and `dab_radio.jpg`): the fascia is gone as a whole-width slope. It is a straight olive shelf the width of the cab (0.15 m deep, its lip 7 cm tall under the front edge), the instrument panel (still raked 0.45 rad, the pod set in as before, so the `dash` fixed point did not move) hangs under it in front of the driver with the switches at its door end, and the rest is open down to the bulkhead. **Radio**: an olive housing under the shelf's lip, standing 4 cm proud of it, with a head unit in its face (orange digits, blue presets, a knob); a felt tray with a lip under it where the glovebox lid was. Its face is in the clean materials. **Choke and hand throttle**: the choke at x -0.66 at the driver's end, the hand throttle at x 0.6 over the tray, both coming out of the bulkhead on 0.3 m shafts that still reach it fully pulled. **Badges**: the Series' (and the 200TDi's shared badge) say MOORLAND, the maker's made-up name that `MAKER_FOR` already names, not Land Rover. **Not seen**: the dash at night, the radio's face close up in the game, the doors opened by a girl boarding.
- **Fourth round** (Jamie: lamps too bright and not round, the hand throttle to the right of the radio, sidelamps with the headlamps, alpha fighting on the spare). **Lamps**: the Series' indicators are the UTV's dark amber and its reverse lens the UTV's smoked grey until lit (`addSignalLamps` takes them), the sidelamps are smoked and light with the headlamps (`quad.userData.sideMaterial`, lit in `setHeadlights`), and the rear lamps are round, as in the photo of the real one: an indicator over a tail lamp over a red reflector, with the nearside's round reverse lamp under them. **Hand throttle** at x 0.05, on the driver's side of the radio. **Spare wheel**: its rim's underside sat exactly on the bonnet's top (1.215) and the strap's underside on the tread's top; it now sits 5 mm into the bonnet and the strap 3 mm into the tread.
- **Instruments and controls** (after crops of `sddefault.jpg`). **Wheel**: a deep round cup hub (open towards you, the badge on its floor) on three flat metal spokes, as in the photo, in place of the black boss and tube spokes. **Switch panel**: a black plate at the door end of the instrument panel with the kit's chrome toggles (`buildToggle`), two small lamp lenses, a push button and the lights' pull knob, turned to face you. **Pedals**: ribbed rubber pads hanging on arms from a pivot under the dash, not bare boxes. **Gear lever**: a rubber gaiter with a ring, and a plate the transfer and diff levers come through. The dials themselves (speedo, combined fuel and water gauge, lamp strip, key) were already after the photos and are unchanged. **Not done**: the orange hazard lens at the shelf's left, the felt console between the seats, working switches (the toggles and pull knob are only for show).
- **Dials, key and lights knob** (Jamie: alpha fighting at the grille's foot, redo the speedo and gauge from the photos, a control that moves with the lights, a key that moves like the UTV's). **Grille**: the valance under the grille panel had its face on the same plane as the grille frame's (pz + 0.035) where they overlap in height; it is thinner and 1 cm further back (face at pz + 0.025). **Pod** (`buildSeries` in `dash.js`, after the crop of the real cluster): the lamps are a column down the left edge (main beam, charge, oil, handbrake) each in a black pocket, not a strip along the top; the speedo is smaller with the indicators' green lamp and the choke's amber one in the foot of its dial, as on the real one; the fuel and water gauge is round and the speedo's size (both r 31, centred on the pod's middle, and the lamp column too), with FUEL and WATER written in it. The photo is too small to read its scales, so the 4+4 ticks and E / F, C / H stay. **Key**: the UTV's (a bezel, a chrome barrel, a steel blade with teeth, a black bow with a ring), at 0.6 scale at the pod's right-hand end, OFF at the top, ON 0.72 rad clockwise, START 1.45, eased by the dash's update; the canvas key it replaces is gone. **Lights knob**: the pull knob on the switch panel comes out 12 mm for the lights and 24 mm for main beam (the Series' dash update moves it, from `lightLamps`). A test checks both. **Not seen**: the key turning and the knob moving in play.
- **Belts** use the kit's old Land Rover pattern (`buildSeatBelt`): the loop at the top of the B pillar and the reel low on the quarter panel, the buckle in the seat's crease; the middle seat is a lap belt.
- **Tests**: `tests/seriesModel.test.js` (the doors swing the right way about their hinge, the gate lies flat with its chains joined, the cargo spots are between the humps), `tests/tailgate.test.js`.

## The tractor as a Massey Ferguson 35 (TODO 98)

From eleven photos Jamie put in `.scratch/quads/tractor/` (a studio three-quarter, two side-ons, a front, a rear, four from the seat looking down on the dash and a close-up and set of the real gauges). Asked up front, and answered: **an MF35** (the old model had the proportions and wheels of an earlier TE20 but the MF35's red-and-grey paint), **three gears kept** (the sound, drivetrain and the girls' climbing are built round them), **no cab**. Jamie's brief: "pay close attention to the controls and instruments; they sit straight in the metalwork on the dashboard, there's no plastic or anything".

- **Smaller, to the photos.** `mf35Body.js` (pure numbers, tested) is the source: rear wheels 0.585 radius (an 11.2-28; it was 0.72), fronts 0.355 (5.50-16), wheelbase 1.83, rear track 1.24 (it was 1.76), the bonnet's ridge at 1.19 m (it was 1.35), the exhaust to 1.92. `REAL_SIZES.tractor` is now an MF35 (2,970 x 1,650 x 1,920 to the exhaust, wheelbase 1,830, track 1,270, tyre 1,170) and `tractor` is in `IN_PROPORTION`: length +3.7%, width +5%, height +0.6%. The old model measured +56% on height (it was only ever measured to a 1.32 m figure that left the exhaust out). The spec's `wheelRadius` follows (0.585).
- **Instruments in the metal.** The dash (`buildTractor` in `dash.js`) has no housing now. The cowl behind the bonnet is the fuel tank and the panel in one: its top slopes to the driver at 21 degrees (`MF_PANEL`), the model lays a V-nosed pressed steel plate in the body's own red on that slope, and the dials, the brass ignition plate and the lamp lenses are drawn on it with chrome bezels (two rings each, the second the rolled lip) standing out of it. Four dials (a big rev counter in the middle with its hours drum, oil pressure left, water temperature right, a small fuel gauge beyond) and four lamps; the oil pressure needle is new (it follows the revs); the made-up maker's name is on the rev counter ("Hartley"); no real names. The dash is scaled 1.5 so the rev counter is the real 95 mm. The lights switch (a chrome bat on a bush), a data plate, the stop knob (a black pull knob low on the panel's right) and the hand throttle (a thin steel lever over a quadrant set into the cowl, as in the photos) are on or beside it. The gear levers (the main one the game poses and a static high / low range one) stand straight out of the grey casting through bolted cover plates with a rubber gaiter, not a plastic housing.
- **Rest of the machine.** Bonnet and nose are two lofts (the engine's grey under the bonnet's lower edge, with manifold, pump and filters), the grille two banks of slats round a strip, the headlamps round in red cups on the nose's sides (the lens keeps the shared lamp material, so the beams work), a red air cleaner stack with a round cap, the exhaust outside the bonnet on the left from the manifold, a red pan seat with a grey cushion and a low back, grid footplates with hangers, pedals (the clutch on the left, two latched brakes on the right), grey pressed-steel wheels with no punched holes and a cast hub on a ring of studs, and the front axle a drilled beam with bolts. Wear is as before (primer on the lips and bonnet sides, rust, oil, mud), over a red and grey that now matches the photos. The bale spike, drawbar, lift arms, PTO stub and every named part are unchanged.
- **Second round** (Jamie: grille not the shape of the front and a black bar through it; rims should be inboard of the tyres; the oil cooler poor; what is the seat on; controls for everything; tail and brake lights; a lights switch; instruments trailing off the panel, and does oil pressure work; lamps overlapping the speedo; the hand throttle from nowhere and needing a stick end; headlamps too far into the body). **Grille**: now the nose's own outline pulled in (`MF_NOSE_FACE`, the same superellipse as the nose loft's end), a frame, a dark back, slats whose ends follow the outline and a plain strip; the black bar was the oil-drip box stood in front of it, now under the nose. **Wheels**: the rims were bulging out past the tyre; every wheel part (rim, disc, hub, studs) is now inside the tyre's outer face, with the disc set in and its middle further in, and the mud drum open-ended and narrower so the disc shows (a test checks the faces). **The "oil cooler"** read as the injection pump and the dynamo, which were boxes: both are cylinders now with end caps, the dynamo with a belt pulley. **The seat** stood on nothing: it is now on a pressed-steel bracket bolted to the gearbox cover with a flat arm running back and the spring standing on the arm. **Controls**: added the accelerator and two latched brake pedals (`accelPedal`, `brakePedals`), a hand brake lever (`parkBrakeLever`) and the stop knob pulling out when used, all posed by `poseTractorControls`; the second (range) lever was dropped as it has no use here. **Lights**: `barLights: 'switch'` gives it a lights switch on `P` (a chrome bat on a brass bush on the panel, named `lightsRocker`; it starts on), and the guards' lamps are real tail lamps (`addTailLights`: lit with the headlamps, brighter on the brake). **Dash**: the oil pressure dial is gone (nothing drives it; the fuel gauge took its place at the left), and the four warning lamps moved to y 112 and centred under the rev counter, and the plate narrowed so every bezel is inside its edge (a test checks). **Hand throttle**: a bracket bolted to the cowl, a toothed quadrant plate with tick marks, a chrome pivot, and a black ball on the end of the lever, all on the slope. **Headlamps**: moved 4 cm out on a bracket off the nose's side, the cup in front of the body, not into it.
- **Third round** (Jamie: the wheels lost their nuts and became flat discs, a floating thing near the hand throttle, a bar going through the rear fender from the seat; he suspected I had not checked the screenshot, and he was right: I had not looked closely at the wheels after the last round). **Wheels**: the cause was the rim's flanges and its barrel being closed cylinders, which capped the whole wheel with a flat disc over the dish, the hub and the nuts. They are now rings (flanges) and an open barrel, and the dish is an open funnel (double-sided) wide at the rim and narrowing in to a cast hub standing out of its middle, with a raised flange carrying eight chrome hex nuts (six at the front) and a chrome cap, all inside the tyre's outer face. A test checks the nuts stand proud of the flange and nothing closed lies across the dish. **The floating thing** was the air cleaner: a thin red pole with a ball on top, standing beside the hand throttle and reading as a second lever. It is now a short, fat stack in a collar on the bonnet with a flat-topped, rolled-lip cap, as in the photos. **The bar through the fender** was the bale spike mast's upper braces, which ran from high on the rails down into the side of the lift housing, so from the side and above they appeared to come out of the seat area and pass through the guard; they now start at 1.5 m on the rails and meet the lift housing's back face at z -1.31, behind the guards. The mudguard stays are also one stay each now (the two diagonal braces went), ending inside the shell's edge, not 1.5 cm outside it. Looked at close-up from the side, behind and above.
- **Fourth round** (Jamie: something black still floating on the right beside the hand throttle in the seat view, and the air cleaner poking out of the body). The black thing was the accessory pod's steel post, stood at x -0.3 on the cowl, which is only 0.44 wide there, so it stood in the air beside it. It is now 0.16 in on the cowl's top, and it is hidden until a pod is fitted (`Quad.fitPod` and `removePod` show and hide it, `userData.podPost`), since a bare stub reads as something floating. The air cleaner stood on a collar at the bonnet's flat-topped height, but the bonnet's rounded side is lower than that 15 cm out: it is now 15 cm out with its foot below the bonnet's top, so the collar sits in the surface. A test checks both.
- **Fifth round** (Jamie: the pod post floating, the dash lamps too small for their symbols, the frame at the back poking out of the guards, the yellow control flat, a black bar under the throttle floating, the tyre through the top of the rear guard). **Post**: 15 cm tall now, foot in the cowl, and the pod's mount at its top. **Lamps**: lens radius 8 mm (was 4.4) with the symbols at 0.62, spaced 26 mm apart and centred under the rev counter. **The yellow control** was the ignition switch, a brass disc drawn flat on the dial face: it is real geometry now (a brass base and rolled bezel, a dark key barrel, a chrome lever that turns when the key does, `ignitionSwitch`). **The black bar** was the throttle quadrant's tick marks: the cowl's rounded section (the bonnet's, exponent 3) falls 2 cm below its flat top 20 cm out, so everything standing on it hung that far in the air; the cowl is squarer now (exponent 6, 5 mm), which also seats the quadrant and the bracket. **The tyre**: I had cut the guards' clearance from 0.17 to 0.10 and the body sits 9 cm lower on its springs than the model's rest, so the tread came up through the shell; it is 0.18 again. **The frame at the back**: the bale spike's upper braces still crossed the guards' silhouette from behind and above even though they are inboard of them; each is now a stay down the back of the rail to the lower link's end, behind the guards.
- **Sixth round** (Jamie: headlamps not connected to the body, the black bar under the hand throttle still there and at the wrong angle, and why the throttle keeps moving when nothing is pushed). **The throttle moving** was the accelerator pedal's pose: `poseTractorControls` took `quad.throttle`, which includes the *hand* throttle's setting (`throttleInput = max(foot, hand)`), so the foot pedal dipped whenever the hand lever stood open, and with the engine's own idle changes it kept shifting. It takes the foot pedal's own travel now (`throttleKey`), so only your foot moves it. **The black bar** was the throttle's quadrant plate lying at the *dash plane's* angle, while the cowl's real top out at x -0.2 and z 0.09 is curved and leans differently; `cowlTop(x, z)` (mf35Body.js) now gives the loft's true surface height and slope there and the plate (now a thin dark slot, 14 mm wide, flush with the cowl, no raised slab) and the bracket lie on it. **Headlamps**: each cup was held by a stub on the nose's side that left it standing off the body; the arm now runs from inside the nose's side out to the cup's back, and the cup is 3 cm nearer the body, so it is carried by the nose, not hung beside it.
- **Fixed points moved on purpose** (the tractor's only; the girls and the camera on it checked by the existing driving and mount tests, not by eye): the steering wheel's centre from (0, 1.561, -0.151) to (0, 1.2, -0.132) and its grips from 0.2 to 0.22 out (a real MF35 wheel is about 0.45 across and sits near the bonnet's height, not 40 cm above it), `riderHands` with it, and the dash from (0, 1.43, 0.3) to (0, 1.082, 0.219) (the panel's plane, in its own frame). The rider's anchor (0, 1.15, -0.8) and the camera's (0, 1.86, -0.84) did not move, so the seat is about 7 cm higher than the photos' and the player's eyes where they were. `tests/fixedPoints.json` regenerated for the tractor only.
- **Not checked**: a girl or you driving it (hands on the lower, bigger wheel; her legs on the lower footplates), the first-person view from the seat, how the lower mudguards sit under a girl riding on one, the front wheels' steering arms turning, and the lamps lit at night. Not done: check chains, wheel weights, a toolbox under the seat, wiring.

## A better tractor model (TODO 4)

Settled with Jamie: it stays an old Fergie (red and grey, no cab, since the sound, dash, gearbox and the girls' climbing are all built round one); better silhouette, better running gear and wear, all three; and front wheels that visibly steer.

- **Silhouette** (`tractorModel.js`): the bonnet is a four-station loft tapering to the nose with a centre seam, side louvres, a radiator cap and an oil bath air cleaner on a pipe; the grille is a chrome frame with black slats and a brass badge; the headlamps sit on stalks; the tank is a separate domed loft behind the bonnet with a filler cap; the gearbox has a clutch bell at the engine end, a hydraulic lift housing behind the seat, a PTO stub, lift arms with rods to the lower links and a top link; the seat is a pressed pan with a turned lip, drain holes, a thin cushion and a coil under it.
- **Running gear:** every wheel is plain pressed steel like an old Fergie's: rim flanges (the outer one rusty), a dished disc with a ring of punched holes (6 at the back, 5 at the front), a cast hub and plain bolts. It first had cast spokes and chrome nuts, which Jamie said looked too posh. The mudguards first floated on one short rod that reached neither the shell nor the body; each now has a bracket on top of the final drive housing, a stay up to a plate under the shell and a brace each way. The front axle has radius rods and a track rod. The front wheels already steered (`Quad` yaws each wheel group); what was missing was anything to see turning, so each front wheel group now carries a yoke and a steering arm on its inside face, which swings with the wheel.
- **Wear:** red oxide primer and rust patches on the bonnet's side, the grille surround, the crown of the bonnet and the outer mudguard lips; oil on the gearbox, the sump and a drip down the grille; mud on the gearbox, the front pedestal and in the drum between the rear lugs (the drum is its own mesh, brown, under the black lugs). All fixed geometry in materials of their own, so it applies whatever body colour the tractor has.
- **Unchanged on purpose:** the physics layout, the anchors, the dash and control positions, and every named part `Quad`, the mounts and the girls look up (`tests/tractorModel.test.js` checks the names, the anchors and that the width and height are still the old ones, since the parking spaces were sized for them).
- **Checked:** unit tests, and one headless look from the front and rear three-quarter views showing the shape, the wheels, the stays and the rolled lips. **Not checked:** the wear patches and oil at close range (they are small and subtle from where I looked), the front steering arms while turning, or the cab-side view from the seat.

## A real two-wheeler model (TODO 7)

Jamie's calls: the mini moto only for now, with the lean machinery in a pure module the ATC can reuse; balance by torque on two real wheels; power and brake mid-corner decide slides and tucks; a fall throws you off with the engine dead and you right it; a foot goes down at walking pace; the girls ride it with a gentle lean and never fall. This replaces the hidden outriggers and the show-only lean in "The mini moto (TODO 10, part one)".

- **Physics**: `MOTO_LAYOUT` is now two wheels in a line. `BikeLean` (`src/vehicle/bikeLean.js`, pure) takes the chassis's lean and rate (`QuadPhysics.lean()`), the bars, speed, throttle, brake and the tyres' grip, and returns a roll torque, the front wheel's angle and a grip factor per tyre. `Quad.balanceBike` runs it each step and `QuadPhysics.rollTorque` applies it about the chassis's forward axis, so the roll angle is Rapier's own.
- **Lean drives the turn**: the bars ask for a lean (full bars is 40 degrees at 9 m/s or more, less when slower, 0.6 of that for a girl). The front wheel is steered to what that lean turns at, `atan(wheelbase g tan(lean) / v squared)`. From 2.5 m/s up to 5 m/s the steering hands over from the bars to the lean; below 2.5 m/s (foot down) and reversing it is the bars alone and it cannot fall.
- **Grip**: a tyre holds a lean of `tan = gripMu (0.4 + 0.6 grip)`, `grip` being the mean of the touching wheels over dry grass (a rider feels the ground and rides to it, so mud counts for less than raw friction). Full throttle at a full lean uses 55% of the rear's; a full brake uses 65% of the front's. Over the limit a slide builds (about 0.2 to 1.5 a second, by how far over) and takes grip off that tyre, so the rear really steps out in the physics; easing off settles it. A front slide also pulls the lean further over (a tuck). At a full slide, or a lean past 62 degrees, it falls.
- **Falling**: it is pushed over to the side it was leaning until it lies at about 88 degrees, the engine is switched off, `isTipped` is true (so the usual "Right the mini moto" works, with `T` or `E`), and you are thrown clear (`Game.throwOff`, like jumping off, to the low side if there is room). The nearest girl within 40 m says something (`youCameOff`, both banks; **needs re-recording**). Nobody gets hurt and nothing breaks.
- **Who can fall**: only you. A girl riding asks for 0.6 of the lean and cannot slide or tuck; an unattended bike (parked, or carrying on after you jumped off) holds itself upright. A falling girl is a later idea.
- **View**: the camera is held 45% level against the lean (`bikeCameraLevel` in the player params), so a 40 degree lean tips the view about 22 degrees.
- **Tunables**: `DEFAULT_BIKE_PARAMS` and `BIKE_PARAM_RANGES`, in the tuning panel as "Two-wheeler lean". The ATC will want its own geometry (three wheels, tipping rather than leaning) but can use the grip and fall bookkeeping.
- **Measured** (`tests/bikeLeanSim.test.js`, the real Rapier chassis on flat ground, no Three.js): it stands parked and rolls straight with under 0.08 rad of lean; a left bar gives a left lean and a left turn (right likewise); a hard brake in a corner puts it down; the same corner holds on grass and drops it at a quarter of the grip; with `safe` it never falls.
- **Not checked**: the feel on the real farm. I loaded the game headless and saw the moto standing upright with no errors, but I haven't ridden it, so the lean numbers (40 degrees, 55%, 65%, the 62 degree fall) are the first guesses from the simulation. The camera tip, the fall tumble and the girl's lines are unseen and unheard. The tyre gauge now lists two wheels for the moto.

## A more Gator-like UTV (TODO 12)

Jamie wanted item 12 done before the full-detail item 108: an interim model that looks much more like a mid-2000s Gator, with a digital cluster and a centre console. Settled with Jamie before building: `B` stays the lever (it moves on the console, F / N / R); `G` and `U` as on the other switched vehicles; `P` for the light switch; lights only from the switch (key on with it off is dark); `T` still tips the bed and the console lever shows it; a seat belt lamp that flashes; the horn gets a button.

- **Body** (`utvModel.js`): the hood has a raised centre, louvres and the maker's name in yellow along each flank, the front has a slatted grille, a badge and chrome-bezelled lamps with amber side-lights, mudflaps behind the rear wheels, a sun peak on the roof, and "STAG 4x4" moulded into the tailgate and each side of the bed. The maker ("DEERFIELD") and model are made up; nothing is a real maker's name or logo. Seats, camera, hands, steering column and the bed are where they were, so the girls' seats and the climbing moves are untouched. Labels are canvas decals (`decal`, `stick` in the model file), so they're the one thing `mergeStatic` leaves alone.
- **Cluster** (`dash.js`, `utv`): a grey-green LCD with the speed in big seven-segment digits, a boxed gear letter (`forwardGearShown`: F, N, R), fuel and water bars, hours, the clock and 4WD and LOCK tags, over a row of lamps: GLOW, CHARGE, OIL, PARK, BELT, LIGHTS (green) and MAIN (blue). The OIL lamp now also lights for a low sump while running, as the red's does, since the UTV has a sump.
- **Console**: a raised pod between you and the glovebox with the F / N / R lever in its gate, a 2WD / 4WD rocker, an orange diff lock button, the light rocker and the bed's tip lever, and a printed legend. The rocker and the button are named `driveSwitchRocker` and `diffLockButton`, so Quad poses them as it does the red's and blue's. The cup holders moved along to make room.
- **4WD and diff lock**: the spec has `driveSwitch` and `diffLock` and `fourWheelDrive` 0, as the Land Rover's. It now starts every game in 2WD, as the other switched vehicles do, and the girls' remember-or-get-stuck logic (`updateFourWd`) already covered any switched vehicle, so they use it with no new code. I haven't measured how often that now leaves a girl stuck in the UTV.
- **Lights** (`lightSwitch.js`, tested): `LightSwitch` cycles off, low, high on `P`. `lightLook` gives the level and colour (dipped 0.7 and warm, main beam 1 and whiter) and how much to stretch the faked beam: main beam is 2.2 times as long, 1.5 times as wide, 1.9 times as bright and has 1.6 times the bloom. They need power (key on, battery up), so a flat battery is dark. The tail lamps now share the headlight supply (they were always lit dull red before) and brighten when you brake. `P` is the quads' pull-start, so the UTV, which has no cord, ignores it for that. Under "UTV lights" in the tuning panel; the switch is under Running on the Vehicles tab and as `lights` in the console (`drive` too, for any switched vehicle).
- **Seat belt lamp**: first built as a 6 second flash after the key went on; replaced by the seat belts below.
- **Horn**: `L` as before. The wheel's pad sinks while any horn sounds, yours or a girl's, through `quad.hornHeld`.
- **Left out**: the girls don't use the light switch (it stays as left), no indicators or hazards (item 19), no roof or doors beyond what was there.
- **Checked**: `npm test` (the switch, the look, the lamps, the belt lamp, the spec; the beam test now covers the main beam), and headless shots of the UTV from the front, side and rear, with the lights on dipped and on main beam, the view from the seat, and close-ups of the console and the cluster, with no page errors. **Not checked**: the lever, rocker and tip lever moving in play, the horn pad sinking, how the UTV drives in 2WD, the girls and the lights, and the tail lamps at dusk.

## No crash of vehicles landing at the start (2026-10-09)

Jamie: the vehicles all dropping into place made a deafening noise when the game first loaded. Every vehicle spawns a little above the ground; before the sounds loaded during the loading screen, they had landed by the time the sound was switched on, so nobody heard it. Now the sound is ready first. `Game.settleVehicles` runs `SETTLE_SECONDS` (2) of physics at the end of loading ("Settling…"), so they're on their wheels before anything can be heard, and throws away the landing thuds and knocks that queued meanwhile. Measured (`.scratch/pp/dropcheck.mjs`, seed 12, 11 vehicles): without it, 19 knocks (3 loud) in the first three seconds and vehicles still moving at 1 m/s; with it, none, and nothing faster than 0.06 m/s. It adds about a quarter of a second to loading (about 1.9 s to "Ready").

- **Belts across the girls, a smaller cluster (UTV):** the belt path was drawn behind the girls' bodies. Measured from the skinned meshes of both girls sitting in the middle and left seats (`.scratch/pp/measure.mjs`): chest front at about z 0.04, waist 0, shoulders -0.02, in the model's ground frame. The sash now goes over the shoulder at z -0.2, across the chest at 0.06 to 0.08 and the lap at about 0.03, then back to the buckle; the lap strap lies across her lap at 0.03. A girl only counts as sitting (so only starts the two seconds before her belt goes on) once she is in the seat (`onTrailer`, or `driving`), not while she walks over or climbs in: headless, the belts came on about six seconds after the request, once she had sat for two. The cluster is scaled 0.95 instead of 1.2, and the F / N / R letters are bigger again (0.014 on the pod; on the cluster the unlit ones are now plain rather than ghosted). Checked in screenshots of the girls belted in and the view from the seat.

- **A smaller console pod and symbols on the dash (UTV):** the pod was mostly empty space, so it is now 0.215 by 0.09 m (from 0.28 by 0.13) with the rockers a little smaller (0.027 by 0.032) and closer (0.036 apart), the gear slot at the near end with F, N and R beside it, and the names printed small underneath. The cluster's warning bulbs are now symbols (`warnSymbol` in `dash.js`): a coil for the glow plugs, a battery, an oil can, a P in a circle, a seated figure with a belt across it, and a headlamp with beams. Out, each is dull grey; lit, each is in its colour with a soft glow behind it, and the belt figure flashes with the dash clock as the lamp did. Checked in screenshots from above; not at your eye level in a game.

- **Badge on the switch pod (UTV):** the pod is 0.1 m tall now, with the maker's name (made up) and the model in a band across the top between two antlers, a yellow line under it, and the switches and gear slot moved down below it. Checked in a screenshot from above.

## Things in the UTV's bed (2026-10-09)

TODO 13. It copies the little tipping trailer's tub rather than inventing anything new.

- **Who can go in:** the same four things the trailer takes (the three jerry cans, the toolbox, the jump leads, the oil bottle; `heldLoad()`). The hose is left out: it is a hose, not a thing you stand. Bales are not pick-up items, so they wait.
- **How:** `CargoHold` (`vehicle/cargoHold.js`, tested) parents the item's model to a spot on the bed, so it tips with it, and unparents it to send it flying out when the bed is up past `slideAt`. Picking one up again just takes it off its spot; nothing has to be told. The UTV's own bed group carries six anchors and an invisible `bedProxy` (`utvModel.js`: `BED_CARGO_SPOTS`, `BED_PROXY`). I did not move the little trailer onto `CargoHold`; it has its own copy of the same code and a test, and can be moved over later.
- **Spots:** two rows of three in the clear between the wheel wells (0.9 m between them), cans and the toolbox turned along the bed. The test checks every item fits every spot. The jump leads' coil is the widest and may overhang a well by a couple of centimetres at the outside spots.
- **Looking:** the bed's box has priority 1 and is only there while your hands hold a load, so it beats the UTV's own box (which would try to fill its tank from a can). Empty-handed you look at the thing in the bed and take it.
- **Tipping out:** items land 1.6 m behind plus up to half a metre, a little to one side, in 0.6 s (`UTV load` in the Tune tab).
- **Left out:** the load adding to the UTV's weight (no effect on how it drives), and the girls putting things in it.
- Not checked in the running game: the look of the items in the bed, the prompt wording, and the tip-out. Unit tests only.
