## A better Land Rover model (TODO 16)

The Series III already had its chassis, leaf springs, grille, dash and tub, but its body panels were flat sheets with no wheel arches: the front wings' outer sides and the tub's sides sat over the tyres and hid them from the side.

- **Wheel arches** (`archPanel` in `landRoverModel.js`, tested): the wings' outer sides and the tub's sides are now extruded shapes with a round arch (0.42 m, round a 0.38 m tyre) cut from the bottom edge, plus a rolled flare (`archFlare`) standing proud of the panel. The tyre shows through. The wing tops and inner sides are 0.05 m longer to meet the new panel. A test checks the panel's size and that no vertex falls inside the tyre.
- **Small things**: mudflaps behind the rear wheels, front and rear number plates (white and yellow), turned-back bumper ends, rain gutters along the roof edges, and the bonnet's hinges at the screen end.
- **Second pass (Jamie's list).**
  - *Tub*: the floor was at 0.78 m with a box over each arch, so the tyres (tops at 0.96 m) poked through it. The floor is now at 1.02 m (`TUB_FLOOR`, tested against the wheel tops), with a low hump over each arch, the sides 0.1 m taller, the tailgate raised to match and a rear body panel under it carrying the lamps and the plate.
  - *Cab joints*: the doors ran 0.05 m short of the bulkhead and 0.1 m from the cab side, the window frames stopped at 1.66 m and the roof's underside is 1.72 m. The doors now fill their openings, the window frames and the screen's top rail run up into the roof, and the door glass is cut to a trapezoid to follow the screen.
  - *Windscreen*: raked back 0.2 rad (about 11 degrees), its frame in a rotated group; the doors' front uprights are the screen's side posts, so the two meet exactly. The wipers, a rear-view mirror and the dash's shelf were moved to suit.
  - *Badges and plates*: canvas labels (`label` in `landRoverModel.js`): a green oval "LAND ROVER" on the grille panel and the tailgate, FRM 88L on a white plate at the front and a yellow one at the back.
  - *Trim*: black pebbled, stitched vinyl on the seats (the driver's split, foam showing, taped), a grained dash top and a ribbed rubber floor, all drawn in canvases when the Land Rover is built, so the model can no longer be built without a canvas (its test stubs one).
  - *Belts*: `LANDY_BELTS` and `addBelt`, named as the UTV's so `poseUtvBelts` poses them. The driver and the left seat have a diagonal and lap belt from the B pillars, the middle one a lap belt between two stalks. In the game the same `SeatBelts` runs as the UTV's (`updateLandRover`): `;` for yours, the girls buckle themselves, the BELT lamp flashes and the bong sounds while you sit unbelted with the key on. A real Series III had neither; they are there because the UTV's are.
  - *Handbrake*: the thin tube is now a ratchet lever with a grip and a release button, up when the parking brake is on (`Space`, eased), by the driver's seat. The BRAKE lamp was already wired.
  - *Choke and hand throttle*: the knob's shaft was 0.03 m long and the knob pulled out 0.05 m, so it floated clear of the dash. The shaft now runs 0.16 m back into the dash and the travel is 0.1 m, and a test checks the shaft stays inside the dash fully out.
  - *Dash*: the binnacle is squarer and 1.5 times the size it was (about 0.3 m wide), with a strip of lamps along its top (ignition, oil, handbrake and belts as symbols, then CHOKE, 4WD and DIFF) above the speedometer and the combined gauge, and the key at the end. Oil is red now, not amber. I could not find a good reference for the exact Series III layout, so this is from memory: worth a look against your own photos. No main beam or indicator lamps yet: the Land Rover has no light switch, and indicators are TODO 19.
  - *Also*: a rear-view mirror, and hinges and a catch on the tailgate.
- Not changed: the colliders, seats, controls and the sound. Not verified in the running game or by screenshot (the model needs a canvas for its dash, so only the arch geometry is unit tested); worth a look for z-fighting at the flares.

## Land Rover, third pass, and the UTV's empty seat

- **Gaps**: the B pillar was 0.12 m deep and the cab's rear panel starts 0.06 m behind it, leaving a slot you could see through; it is 0.22 m now. The wings' outer sides, tops and inner sides start at the bulkhead (z 0.6) instead of 0.7, and a hinge pillar and a sill under each door close the rest.
- **Badges and plates**: label canvases are drawn in pixels now (the first pass drew text at a fraction of a pixel, which the browser spaced badly, so characters overlapped); the registration is sized to fill the plate. The tailgate badge is on two lines, LAND over ROVER. A small badge is on the dash and in the middle of the steering wheel.
- **Dash**: four lamps, ignition, oil, handbrake and choke (with a choke symbol, drawn like the others). No belt lamp (a Series III had none), and no 4WD or diff lock lamps. The Land Rover's belts still work and the girls buckle up, but there is no bong either: `updateLandRover` ignores the nag.
- **Knobs**: the hand throttle is on the left of the choke, both over the edge of the gearbox tunnel at the dash's face, raised to 1.095 m, with light heads (grey and red) so they show on the black dash.
- **Gear lever** (`gearLeverPose`, tested): a normal H, first top-left (forward, away from you), second below it, third top-right, fourth below that, and reverse top-left of first. Before, the two columns were the wrong way round and first and third were back towards you.
- **Exhaust**: the pipe ran through the rear wheel. The silencer is 0.34 m from the middle and the pipe runs straight back inside the wheel to come out under the tailgate, where it shows.
- **UTV belt nag**: with the key on and nobody in the driver's seat the lamp flashes and the bong sounds after the usual 1.5 s (`SeatBelts.emptyFor`), whoever is nearby, since someone is expected to be sitting there. A girl who has just sat down is left her two seconds. The Land Rover's `SeatBelts` has the same logic but nothing reads it.

## The 200TDi (a second Land Rover)

Decided with Jamie: a 90 County rather than a Discovery for now; a faded pastel green; both Land Rovers can be on the farm together; the sound is the UTV's for now, with its own idle and top end; the newer dash and centre controls; a square back with the spare wheel on the rear door; back seats later.

- **A variant, not a copy** (`variant: 'defender'` in `buildLandRoverModel`, `DEFENDER_SPEC` in `quadSpecs.js`, `landRover: true` and `defender: true`). They share the chassis, wheels, arches, belts, handbrake, steering wheel, tow hitch and exhaust, and the spec flags every Land Rover path in the game already looked at. The Series spec is renamed "Series III" (its id is still `landrover`); the new one is `landrover200`, "200TDi".
- **Body**: the roof runs the length of the car in the body colour with black gutters; the cab's rear panel and the tub are replaced by side bodywork with a window band and the arches cut in it, a rear door with a window and a carpeted load floor over the wheel humps. The spare is on the door, 0.1 m proud (the model reaches 2.18 m behind its middle; the physics box is unchanged). No bonnet spare, a raised ridge on the bonnet instead, black wheel flares, silver wheels, a front plate and badge as the Series. Nothing rides in the back yet.
- **Interior**: grey cloth seats with headrests, a lighter grey dash moulding and carpet, a moulded console with the gear, transfer and diff lock levers coming up through rubber gaiters (the transfer lever black and the diff lock yellow, the Series' being yellow and red), a gaiter on the handbrake, a black four-spoke wheel. No choke or hand throttle: the knobs exist hidden because `poseLandyControls` looks them up by name.
- **Dash** (`buildDefender` in `dash.js`, style `defender`): a rev counter with the red zone from the engine's redline, the speedometer to 100 mph with a six-digit odometer, the fuel and water gauge, a lamp strip with glow, charge, oil, handbrake, belt and diff lock, and the key. It reads the same dash state as the others.
- **Drive and engine**: a clutch and the same four gears as the Series, synchro on all (`crashGears` 0), 1,750 kg and 8,400 N of drive (against the Series' 1,350 and 7,000), about 62 mph. The engine is the UTV's diesel (glow plugs, the key stops it, petrol soaks it) retuned: idle 800 (770 to 830 a game), maxRpm 4,400, redline 4,300, `loadDragRpm` 2,000 so a manual diesel stalls if you dump the clutch high. Rattly: roughness 0.10 to 0.16 (the Series' 0.04 to 0.10), `cycleJitter` 0.05, `pulseStrengthJitter` 0.16 and a vibration `steadiness` of 0.35 against the Series' 0.85. Its sound is the UTV's Gator bank with `pulseRefRpm` 800: the pace follows the revs, so it ticks faster and rattles more than the UTV; a recording of its own is a TODO item.
- **In the game** (`game.js`): `landys.landy` and `landys.defender` each hold `{ spec, unit, mount, ride }`; the old `landyUnit`, `landyMount` and `landyRide` are gone. `GO_KINDS` is the list of vehicles the girls take for a go ('tractor', 'utv', 'landy', 'defender'), `mountOfKind` and `rideOfKind` find the seats, and the 200TDi shares the Series' lines for the girls (so Amy still says "it is a Series": TODO). Each has its own riders group in the Tune tab ("Land Rover riders", "200TDi riders"), its own seed for wear, quirks and faults, and the diesel has no carb to go wrong. The belt bong plays on the 200TDi only, and fades with distance (`beltBing(at)`).
- **Checked**: unit tests (the spec, both models building with a stubbed canvas, the 200TDi's named parts); headless: both models from outside and from the seat, the 200TDi starting on its glow plugs and idling at 787 rpm, and Jess walking to it, driving it and bringing it back (seed 9). Seen: a girl asked to take a vehicle that is out in a crop field (the wheat or barley field) walks towards it and stops about 55 to 75 m short; I haven't checked whether the tractor and the UTV do the same out there, but it will make Jess's go on a vehicle in a crop field fail. Not checked: a girl taking the 200TDi with a flat battery (it has no handle, so she will say it won't go), riding in its bench, and the Series and the 200TDi both on the farm at once beyond loading.

## The 200TDi, second pass

Jamie's list after trying it.

- **Five-speed.** `gears: 5` on its gearbox, with `topSpeed5`, `torque5` and `launchLoad5` (new gearbox params, ranges added, `GEARS` and the `5` key). Tops 5.0, 8.6, 13.6, 21 and 32 m/s (11, 19, 30, 47 and 72 mph), each step about 1.55 times the last; `maxSpeed` 32 (the first go was a four-speed with 28, about 62 mph). The Series still has four. The gear lever's H for five (`gearLeverPose(gear, gears)`, tested): first and second on the left, third and fourth in the middle, fifth top right, reverse below it.
- **Lights.** Both Land Rovers now have the UTV's three-way light switch (`lightSwitch: true`; `P` from the seat: off, dipped, main beam), so their lights are off until you switch them on and need the key. The Series' starting handle is also on `P`, but only from the front on foot, so the handle takes precedence there (`pullDown` in `quad.js` checks the crank first). Tell-tales: the Series has only the blue main beam lamp (`beam`, a headlamp with four straight rays); the 200TDi a green lights lamp and the blue main beam one; the UTV keeps its one lamp that changes colour. The lights' brightness and reach are the UTV's "UTV lights" group in the Tune tab, shared.
- **Dash** in a different style: charcoal moulding with a squarer corner, white figures and orange needles, the rev counter and speedometer larger and the fuel and water gauges separate and stacked at the right, the lamps in a row along the bottom (glow, charge, oil, handbrake, belts, lights, main beam). The diff lock lamp is gone.
- **Steering wheel**: a thicker, softer rim on two spokes with a padded hub and the badge (the Series keeps its thin four-spoke one). **Plate**: H471 DKP, front and back (the Series is FRM 88L). **Badge**: a small black "200Tdi" plate on the left of the rear door, opposite the maker's. **Rear lamps**: a tall black housing on each corner with the red stop and tail lens over the amber indicator over a clear reverse lens (the Series has two small lamps). **Headrests** are on two steel posts into the backrest, on the driver's and the left-hand seat only.
- **Less bouncy**: the first go had `suspensionStiffness` 36, compression and relaxation damping at the Series' 3 and 2.6, `wheelShake` 0.9 and vibration gains of 1.9, 0.2 and 0.26 (pulse, heave, pitch). It has 32, 4.4 and 3.8, travel 0.18, `wheelShake` 0.55, and 1.4, 0.12 and 0.16 with the heave damping at 0.45. Not tried on a long run: all of these are in the 200TDi's Vehicle and Vibration groups if it still looks wrong.
- **Revs** (for the record): idle 800 warm, dealt 770 to 830 each game; full throttle 4,400, dealt 4,350 to 4,450; redline 4,300; stalls below 350; the starter turns it at 230 and it catches at 1,000; it rises at 3.8 and falls at 3.0 per second. All are in the "200TDi/Engine" group in the Tune tab (the sliders for `maxRpm`, `redlineRpm` and `idleRpmWarm` now go down to 2,000, 1,800 and 300 so a diesel's values are on them), and the per-game spread is `idleRange` and `topRpmRange` in the spec.
- **The girls' wheel** gets a slice for each Land Rover on the farm, "Series III" and "200TDi" (get in, drive it, start it, and get out when they are in), checked in a run: Amy got into the 200TDi and out again, and the wheel offered the right thing.

## Better instrument pods (TODO 18)

Settled with Jamie: all five (the red and blue dashes, the tractor's, and both accessory pods); looks and behaviour only, no new readouts; words on lamps and gauges become symbols; "HONDA" comes off the red's dash; the pods keep their mounts.

- **Symbols instead of words.** `warnSymbol` moved from `dash.js` to `dashParts.js` (it was the UTV's) and gained four symbols: a fuel pump, a thermometer in water, two axles with a shaft for 4WD and the same with a locked ring for the diff lock. It takes a `scale` for dial faces and `glow: false` for the LCDs. Oil, temperature and fuel lamps on the red, blue, loan, tractor and both pods, the tractor's WATER and FUEL dial labels, the Land Rover's combined gauge, and the 4WD notes on the loan quad's and the UTV's LCDs now use them. N and R stay as letters (`letterLamp` for the pod). Units stay as words (MPH, ODO, TRIP, HRS), as on a real dial.
- **Analogue pod.** One row of four: the speedo and rev counter large at each end, fuel and temperature small between them, each in its own chrome bezel, with the lamps along the top (N, R, temperature, fuel, oil, then 4WD and diff lock on a quad that has them, centred). The housing is wider (210 mm against 176) and a little shorter. Fixed positions are `DIAL_Y`, `BIG_X`, `MINI_X` at the top of `dashPod.js`.
- **Digital pod.** Its OIL, FUEL, TEMP and 4WD words are symbols too.
- Not done, as agreed: backlighting (item 33's dusk), new readouts. The analogue pod's needles now lag the real speed and revs (0.4 and 0.3 of the gap per frame, like the stock dashes) instead of snapping; a key-on sweep is not done.
- Checked: unit tests, and a headless run (clicking through the overlay) of the red quad with each pod, the tractor with the analogue pod and the tractor's and loan quad's stock dashes. Not checked: the blue's own dash by eye (it wasn't dealt that game), the Land Rover's combined gauge, or the pods while moving.

### Better instrument pods, second pass (Jamie's feedback)

- **Analogue pod:** the big dials are larger (35 mm, was 28) so the pod is only as tall as they are, and the lamps are small and sit in a row over the two small dials between them. The centre text (MPH, ×1000 r/min) moved up and shrank, the odometer, trip and hour drums sit lower, and the ODO / TRIP / HRS words beside the drums are gone (they collided with the 60 and 0). All seven lamps are always on the face (N, R, temperature, fuel, oil, 4WD, diff lock); on a quad with no drive switch the last two just stay out.
- **Digital pod:** the 4WD and diff lock symbols are always drawn, dim when out or when the quad has no switch.
- **N and R** are lit-up letters (`glyphLetter`, no lamp behind them) on the red, blue, loan and green dashes and the analogue pod, to match the symbols. The `letterLamp` helper from the first pass went.
- **Tractor dash:** the rev counter is the big dial on the left, water temperature and fuel side by side on the right, and the GLOW, CHARGE and OIL symbols and the key in a line under them. The dial symbols are gone (an old tractor wouldn't have them; C / H and E / F are enough). It looks old: a worn dark housing, dull pitted bezels (`agedSteel`), brown-black faces with faded cream figures, and yellowed glass with a haze, scratches and specks over each dial (`agedGlass`, fixed so nothing shimmers).
- **Debug panel:** the repeated `reading 'mode'` page error was the Vehicles tab's `lights` and `drive` controls reading `quad.lights` and `quad.drive` on a picked vehicle that has neither; both now read through `?.`.
- Checked: unit tests and a headless run (past the overlay) of both pods on the red quad and the tractor's stock dash. Not checked: the blue's dash, or the analogue pod on the tractor after this pass.

## Indicators and a reverse light (TODO 21)

- Keys: `,` left, `.` right, `=` hazards, on the UTV and both Land Rovers (the quads have no indicators). Pressing the key for the mode already on switches it off. They need the key on and a charged battery, like the dash.
- Logic in `indicators.js` (pure, tested): 1.5 flashes a second with the lamp lit for 55% of each, which sits inside the legal 60 to 120 a minute. A left or right indicator cancels itself once the bars have gone beyond `cancelTurn` (0.35 of lock) the way it points and come back inside `cancelStraight` (0.12). Hazards never cancel. Tunables are in the Tune tab under "Indicators".
- Lamps: `addSignalLamps` (`quadModel.js`) gives each vehicle its own left, right and reverse lens materials, sorting the model's existing orange lenses by which side of the middle they are on (front and back of a side flash together), so no model needed its lamps rebuilt. `setSignalLamps` lights them. The Series III and the UTV gained a clear reverse lens by the tail lamps (the 200TDi already had one, unlit until now). The reverse lamp lights when reverse is selected with the key on, from `quad.reverse`.
- Dash: a green pair of arrows on the UTV's, the Series' and the 200TDi's (the 200TDi's lamps were respaced from 28 to 25 to fit it), flashing with the lamps. The quads' pods and the tractor have no indicators, so none.
- Sound: a dry relay click, higher on than off (`indicatorClick`, volume `indicatorVolume`), fading with distance like the belt bong. It plays for any vehicle left flashing, not only yours.
- Debug: Vehicles tab has an `indicators` selector (off, left, right, hazard) and the console has `indicators` on the same vehicles. It is not a fault, so "Put it right" leaves it alone.
- **Second round**: the lamps did not light on the Land Rovers because the mud coat clones every body material for its own shader and had cloned the new lenses; it now skips them (as it does the headlamp and tail lenses). The dash arrows were pointing inward; the Series' one lamp is now "<- ->" and lights for either side, while the UTV's and the 200TDi's have a left and a right arrow that flash on their own side (the 200TDi's lamps were respaced again to 21.5). An indicator stalk sits on the left of each steering column (down for left, up for right, eased, centred for hazards) and a hazard switch, red with a white triangle, on the Series' and the 200TDi's dash top and under the UTV's lights rocker, which glows with the hazards only. The keys are `,` `.` and `=` (slash and apostrophe both clashed with Firefox). The Series III now has a single round reverse lamp on the driver's side so it differs from the 200TDi's pair of square ones.
- Not checked: how the stalk and the hazard button look and sit (placed from the model's numbers, not seen).
- Left out: the girls do not use the indicators when they drive, and nothing flashes the lamps on the rear view. No light cast on the ground from the reverse lamp (the lens glows only).

- **Third round**: the Series' reverse lamp moved to the left (nearside) as the driver sits on the right. The stalk now swings about an axis worked out from the column's rake (`addSignalStalk`), so down is towards the floor and up towards the roof; it is in the column's own material, has the "< >" symbol on its end facing the driver, and is a thin round one with a button on the Series and a flat moulded one with a wide end on the UTV and 200TDi. The hazard switch is built facing +z with the triangle's point up and then turned to face the driver (`faceAlong`): on a black shroud on top of the Series' column, in the UTV's console pod (made 7 cm wider, other switches kept where they were; red cap in the same surround as the rockers, no plate) and, on the 200TDi, drawn on its cluster above the key. Its red cap now lights because the mud coat skips it (it had copied it, as with the lenses). The pod's legend text was dropped by some browsers because the decal drew fonts a fraction of a pixel high under a big scale; `text` in `utvModel.js` now draws at the real pixel size. "Pointed down" for the UTV switch I took as the same point-up triangle with its flat edge at the bottom.

## Seats that ride differently (TODO 124)

Decided with Jamie (all as recommended): you feel it too, the girls react with lines only (no new pose), and a hard enough bounce is a slam.

- `src/vehicle/seatRide.js` (pure, tested). Each seat is a damped spring driven by the frame's vertical acceleration (`UpAccel`, from the model's height plus the vibration heave, clamped at `accelMax` 60), so a bump pushes it the other way and it rings. The kinds and their starting numbers (gain, Hz, damping, extra pitch share, travel, slam point): pillion 0.9, 3.2, 0.35, 0.6, 5 cm, 0.9; rack 0.7, 4, 0.4, 0.4, 4 cm; front (the cab seats) 0.5, 3, 0.5, 0.2, 4 cm; rear (the 200TDi's back bench) 1.1, 2.1, 0.12, 0.7, 9 cm, 0.8; bed 1.2, 5, 0.2, 0.7, 12 cm, 0.75. The back bench has the lowest, least damped spring and the biggest travel, so it bounces and rings; the bed is short and sharp.
- Pitch: the seat's distance behind the middle times the vibration body's pitch, times the kind's share, added to its height (rear is +z in the model), so the back rides the pitch harder than the nose. Not the physics pitch, which the anchors already follow as part of the model.
- Slam: the seat has run into its stop (`slam` of its travel) faster than `slamSpeed` 0.25 m/s, at most every `slamGap` 0.7 s per seat. The travel is a hard stop (it bottoms out or tops out). In the bed, an upward slam is her being thrown up.
- `Quad.updateSeats` moves the seat's anchor and its matching camera anchor (the pillion's, the bench's `cameraLeft`/`cameraMid`, the rear seats'), so a person sitting there, you included, moves with it. The driver's seat is left alone, so driving is as it was. Offsets are divided by the model's scale. `quad.seatSlams` lists the step's slams.
- Reactions: `Game.updateSeatSlams` finds the girl in that seat (on the pillion or rack, or in a bench slot of that vehicle) and says `seatPillion`, `seatRack`, `seatRear`, `seatBed` or (thrown up) `seatBedAir` with chance 0.6 when the slam is over 0.3 strength; the voice's own cooldowns space them. Lines in `seatLines.js`. There is no reaction for the front seats, and no new pose (no grab handle).
- Panel: each vehicle has a "Seats" folder next to its Vibration (the vehicle's own tuning on the Vehicles tab).
- Checked headless once: every vehicle in the seed built its seats (pillion and rack on the quads, the two front seats and the bed on the UTV, the front seats on the Series III), shaking the frame by hand moved the anchors a few millimetres, and there were no page errors. Not checked: how it looks and feels in a real ride (the numbers are physically reasoned but not tuned by hand), the lines in play, the cattle grid throwing a girl up in the bed, or the 200TDi's back bench in that seed (it was not in it).

She brings a free vehicle with working lamps within 70 m, a cab (the Land Rovers, the UTV) before a quad, then the nearest, and parks it 6 m off on the side it comes from, facing you, kept inside the field. - It holds her (`hold('workLight')`) while it lasts, and ends when you ride off, walk 25 m beyond the spot, after 15 minutes, or "That'll do for light, thanks" on her wheel (also on the cab's own wheel when she's sat in it). ## Wet and binding brakes (TODO 1)

Nothing needed settling; the numbers were chosen here.

- `src/vehicle/brakeState.js` (pure, tested in `tests/brakeState.test.js`): `BrakeState` per quad, with `wet` (0 to 1) and `binding`. Wet rises while moving above `soakSpeed` 0.8 m/s with water deeper than `soakFrom` 0.25 under a wheel (the same `puddleAt` the air filter uses, which includes the stream and ford), at `soakRate` 0.8 a second at full depth, so the ford soaks them in about a second and a half. It falls at `dryRate` 0.03 a second driving, plus `dryBraking` 0.35 a second at full brake pressure, so dabbing dries them in a few seconds and plain driving in about half a minute. Stood still they stay wet.
- Wet scales only the lever's brake by `1 - wetLoss (0.8) * wet`. The parking hold, the handbrake and the rolling resistance are unchanged, so a wet quad still stays put on a slope.
- Binding is a drag, not a brake: `bindDrag` 1.1 m/s2 times mass is added to the physics `hold` (the same path as the bog's drag), because the brake path cuts the drive and a binding brake shouldn't. It frees after `bindMetres` 6 of riding (each quad's own is 0.6 to 1.5 times that, from the seed) with a knock from `rideAudio` and no drag after. Each quad starts binding with `bindChance` 0.2, from the game's seed, so `?seed=` repeats it. A binding brake does not come back in a game; the quad stood all game is the start.
- Debug: "wet brakes" and "brake binding" in Problems, wetness in Running, `brakes` in the console tree, both in `problemSummary` and cleared by "Put it right"; "Brakes" tuning under Driving.
- Not done: the girls don't comment on it, and the barn find's seized brake bench job is separate and unchanged. Not checked in play: how the 0.8 loss and 1.1 m/s2 drag feel, or the clunk's sound. Unit tests only.


## The old three-wheeler leans in (TODO 5)

Jamie's calls: the rider's weight shift is automatic from the steering (no new key, Z is already the choke off), the girls ride it gently without using the lean, and the model's tilt is the chassis rolling as it is pushed. The item's premise was a little off: the ATC never rode on the two-wheeler model (`bikeLean.js`, the mini moto's), it is the three-wheel raycast vehicle, so this is its own small module.

- `src/vehicle/riderLean.js` (pure, tested; tunables in "Three-wheeler rider lean"): the rider's weight goes off the middle by `maxShift` 0.25 m at full bars and `fullLeanSpeed` 6 m/s (less below), eased at `shiftRate` 1.5 a second so it follows the bars a moment late. Its roll torque (`riderMass` 75 kg times g times the offset, about 184 N m at most) goes through `QuadPhysics.rollTorque`, leaning the chassis to the inside against the corner's outward roll. A girl driving manages `girlShift` 0.75 of it; nobody aboard, reversing and standing still give none. Switched on by `userData.riderLean` on the model.
- The ATC's `centreOfMassY` went from -0.06 to 0 so that sitting still tips it sooner. Real Rapier on flat ground (`tests/riderLeanSim.test.js`, bars ramped in over 0.3 s, speed held): without the lean it rolls over from 8 m/s, which is the old 13 m/s pulled back to about 8; with the lean it is up at 11 and over at 14. Between 12 and 13 m/s is the edge.
- Nothing is drawn for the rider's shift; the visible lean is the chassis rolling under the torque, which the model already follows. The girls' poses don't move her body. Not checked in play: how it feels, or a girl riding it round the yard.

## Loading a quad onto the bale trailer (TODO 6)

Settled with Jamie before building: the bale trailer (not the little one); the bales move to the front so a quad fits behind them; `T` folds the ramps; both a push and a winch; an `E` strap; every quad but the tractor, UTV and Land Rovers; the girls stay out of the loading itself but say a line or two.

- **Not a physics job.** A loaded quad is moved by hand along the ramp, never driven or rolled by Rapier. `vehicle/quadLoad.js` (pure, tested) is a small machine in the trailer's own frame: `approach` (the winch drags it across the ground to the foot, turning it nose first), `ramp` (up or down, held by the winch cable or just pushed), `aboard` (on the bed). `HayTrailer` works out a world pose from it every frame (`poseLoad`) and sets `quad.pinned`; `Quad.fixedUpdate` then only holds its body there (`QuadPhysics.pin`) and runs nothing else, so there is no fighting the suspension, brakes or engine on a moving trailer. `quad.loadedOn` is the other half, and `Companion.mayRide` says no to a loaded quad.
- **Ramps.** Two aluminium planks hinged at the back edge, stowed under the bed, 2.4 m long (an 18.9 degree slope up to the 0.78 m bed). `T` toggles (on foot within 5 m of the foot, or on the tractor hitched and under 0.8 m/s); they won't fold up while a quad is on them or being fetched. `rampSeconds` 1.4.
- **Winch.** Hold `X` with the ramps down, within 9 m of the foot, not looking at the green or the tractor (their `X` is a tap and keeps working): the nearest quad within `winchReach` (7 m) that `mayLoad` allows is hooked. It is dragged at 1.1 m/s, then wound up at 0.7 m/s; let go and the cable holds it where it is. `Shift` + `X` pays it out, down to the ground, where it is let go. The cable is a plain line from a drum against the back of the bales to the quad's nose.
- **Push.** The existing `V` push. A quad pushed straight on to within 1.5 m of the foot (0.4 rad and 0.7 m tolerance, either end first) takes the ramp, and climbs at 0.55 m/s only while pushed; let go and it runs back down (slope times gravity, at most 3 m/s) and is released at the foot. Pushing is `quad.pushDir` times which end leads.
- **Dead quads only** (`mayLoad`): not running, nobody on it or heading for it, not tipped, not on the winch or chain, not the tractor, UTV, Land Rovers or the barn find under its tarp. The mini moto and the three-wheeler go on like the rest.
- **Strap.** `E` on a quad that is aboard and still toggles it; two orange ratchet straps appear over it. Unstrapped it keeps going when the trailer speeds up or brakes: past 2.2 m/s squared it slides (0.8 of the excess), slows at 2.5 m/s squared, stops against the winch drum at the front ("thump") and, with the ramps down, goes out of the back and down them. With the ramps up it stops at the back. Strapped nothing moves. So gentle driving never matters and a hard stop does.
- **Weight.** A loaded quad's mass goes through `towEffect` for the tractor (it feels 15% of it, as with the tipping trailer: about 7% of its drive for a 290 kg quad). The empty bale trailer still takes nothing, as before.
- **The bales and the seats.** The bed was two layers of bales at the back and the front with a well between. Now the front holds two rows of two across, the back row only one layer high; the girls' seats are the back edge of that low row, so the seat anchors are turned round and they sit facing back over the clear bed, leaning on the high bales when they lie back. All their climbing and bumping works from the anchor's own frame so it is unchanged. The clear bed is 2.3 m; a full-length quad has about 0.4 m of play on it between the back of the bed and the winch drum.
- **Lines** (`npc/loadLines.js`, merged into the main sets): `loadAboard`, `loadStrap`, `loadSlid`, three each, one girl within 25 m picked from the seed.
- **Debug.** Tune: "Loading ramps" (every number above, ramps re-pose on change). Vehicles: "on the bale trailer" under Running, and "Winch it onto the bale trailer" / "Pay it back off" actions; console `vehicle.<id>.trailer`, and `load` / `unload` verbs. They use the real winch and pin (the winch input is held by the game until it is aboard).
- **Checked:** unit tests (`tests/quadLoad.test.js`: geometry, lining up, winch up and down, push and roll back, strapped and unstrapped on the bed, ramps folding), and headless: a switched-off red quad winched across the grass, up the ramps and strapped, with the cable, straps, ramps and bales all drawn as meant, then paid out down the ramps and released. **Not checked:** pushing it up with real keys, towing it behind the tractor with real driving (and so how much it slides), the girls sitting facing back on the new seats (the code is unchanged but I did not see it), how it looks with a smaller quad, the mini moto or the three-wheeler on the ramps, and the three girls' lines by ear (recorded in the background).

## Where the vehicles start, spread out (2026-10-11)

Jamie: too many vehicles started near the barn and caused traffic, and some started on the little tipping trailer. Agreed: at most four in the yard, spread further out; the tractor, UTV and Land Rovers never in the yard and out wider; new spots mostly on the home side.

- **Measured before** (500 deals, `.scratch/spawnstats.mjs`): 4.7 vehicles in the yard on an average farm of 8.4, all seven slots full in 1 game in 7, about one of them a big vehicle; slots 4.4 m apart along two rows. In 1 game in 10 a vehicle started within 4.5 m of the tipping trailer: the "by the paddock" spot (-53, 30) with its 3 m nudge reached it. The straw bales spot (18, 10) could land on a bale too.
- **The yard:** eight slots spread round it, 8 to 12 m apart (a test keeps them over 8), of which `spawn.yardMax` 4 are used; the big vehicles are always away. Gaps between vehicles 4.5 m (was 2.8) and 6 m for a big one (was 4.6).
- **Away spots:** "by the paddock" is gone (no clear ground near it: the paddock, the trailer and the track); "by the straw bales" moved to (28, 14). New: out from the yard (-14, 64), by the south hedge (8, 70), by the east hedge (70, 40), by the north hedge (30, -70), in the far corner (-68, -50), down the west hedge (-72, -22). Twelve in the grass field and five out east. The hill pasture was tried and is too steep (25 to 68% at its foot).
- **Keep-out** (`spawnKeepOut`, `spawnBlocked` in `world/spawns.js`, pure): circles round both trailers, the tank, the bowser, the hose, the bell, the barn and its doorway, the silage, the paddock, every straw bale, the trees behind the barn, the jump and its run-up, the drag strip and the field's bog, plus 3 m off the track's edge. Placing a vehicle tries up to eight nudges that miss them, then uses the spot's middle. A test checks every spot is clear nudged any way, over 3.3 m from a hedge and under 15% slope, and that 200 deals of twelve never start one on anything.
- **Measured after:** 3.4 in the yard on average, none big, nearest neighbour in the yard 13.8 m on average and 5.2 m at the closest, no starts on anything.
- **Not changed:** where a girl leaves a quad when she brings it home (`homeParking.js`, east of the row by the barn), which is play rather than the start. **Not checked** in the running game: how the spread looks, the girls fetching quads from further away, or the big vehicles' new spots by eye.

## Chain drive (TODO 16)

- It is a fault kind, `chain`, in `faults.js`, so it gets the farm's save, the Problems list, "Put it right", the girls' fault lines and the history for free. Only a spec with `chainDrive: true` (yellow, mini moto, ATC) has one: `Faults.hasChain`, set by `Quad`. Other quads never start it and history never deals it to them.
- Two things wear it. Slack (`levels.chain`) starts at 0.15 like any fault, then grows with riding at 900 s from sound to hanging. Oil (`kept.chainLube`, 1 fresh to 0 dry) runs out over 1,500 s of riding; a dry chain starts stretching up to three times as soon and stretches three times as fast (`chainDryWear` 2). Both are in `kept`/`levels` and saved by `farmState.js` (`kept` joins the saved fault fields).
- What slack does: a lash taking up to 12% of the drive at its worst; a slap (the `knock` sound, harder with more slack) when the ground gives a vertical jolt over 4 m/s2 and slack is over 0.3; and from 0.8 slack with the throttle over half open, a chance a second (up to 0.25 at full slack) of jumping the sprocket, after which `driveFactor` is 0 until it is put back. The jolt is the change in vertical speed per second, read in `Quad` before the faults step.
- The fixes are two: the toolbox (`N`, a rough fix like the others: back on, oiled to 0.6, slack left at the field-fix level and capped) and a new bench job. The job (`chain`, eleventh, on the minus key because the digits ran out at ten) has an inspect step that says how slack and dry it is. Adjust, nuts or oil done badly: slack comes back to 0.35 (adjust, nuts) or the chain is left half oiled (oil). Only chain quads can start it (`jobAvailable` takes `hasChain`).
- The girls: Amy and Jess each have a `faultChain` line (a slapping chain, said when riding) and a `faultChainOff` line (said when the engine is running and it has come off), plus a `histChain` line for a chain that was already loose. Same notice rules as the other faults (`faultNoticeFrom`, twice at most).
- Not done: a squeak for a dry chain and a visible droop on the models. The slap is the only sound.

## Crash damage (TODO 30)

- Two fault kinds, `rod` and `rim`, in `faults.js`, so they get the save, the Problems list, "Put it right", the girls' fault lines and the history for free. Unlike the others nothing starts or worsens them with time: a knock does (`Faults.damage`), and `CRASH_KINDS` marks them as bench-only (a rough fix returns false, and there is no toolbox button). The level is how bent: a second knock only raises it, and moves it to the wheel that took the worse. Which wheel is saved (`rodWheel`, `rimWheel`).
- Spotting it is `CrashWatch` in `crashDamage.js` (pure). There is no collision callback in the physics to hang this on, so it watches the velocity: speed knocked off against the way the quad was going, added up over `window` 0.08 s, counts as a hit over `hitMinDv` 3.5 m/s; a fall brought up short, added up the same way (a suspension spreads a landing over several steps), counts as a landing over `landMinDv` 5 m/s. Hard braking tops out near 0.8 m/s in that window, so it cannot trip it. A step over 0.1 s, or nobody aboard (`rider` or `driver`), resets it, so a teleport or stopping a parked quad by hand is never a crash; one crash counts once (`cooldown` 1.5 s).
- What bends (`damageFor`): a hit picks the wheel nearest the obstacle (the way the quad was shoved, taken into its own frame). A front wheel can have its rod bent (`rodChance` 0.7 at the worst, half that if the hit is more sideways than head-on), any wheel its rim (`hitRimChance` 0.6). A landing can only bend a rim, on a random wheel (`landRimChance` 0.45). The lightest hit that counts has 0.4 of those chances and level 0.35; the worst has all of them and level 1.
- Effects at full: a rod pulls the bars 0.07 rad towards its side (a flat tyre's is 0.09) and toes its wheel 0.14 rad, for show; a rim wobbles its wheel 0.09 rad once a turn (a little rolling too) and costs 5% of the drive. Both are shown on the wheel in `Quad.update`'s wheel loop. Wheels that can't be hurt (the mini moto's outriggers) are never picked, and a rod is only on a wheel the layout calls `front`.
- Bench: one job, `crash` (thirteenth, on `[`, since the digits, minus and equals are used), mends whichever is bent. Its look step says which wheel, in words. The `true` step has a window, and a hold too short, too long or nuts left loose leave both at `crashBotched` 0.3. The girl at the bench doesn't do it.
- The girls: `faultRod` and `faultRim` (said when it's being ridden and bad enough to notice, as a flat tyre is) and `histRod` and `histRim` (a quad that starts the day bent). New lines, so the voice banks want re-recording. Nobody reacts at the moment of the hit; that is the existing shout and flinch work, not touched.
- Panel: the two levels in Problems, which wheel, and a "Hit a post head-on" button that calls the real `crash` method. Tuning per quad under "Crash damage". Console: `faults.rod`, `faults.rim`.
- Not done: a sound for the knock, the girls reacting at the instant of the hit, a rim leaking air, and bending anything on the tractor or Land Rover specially (they take the same rules).
- **Not checked** in the running game: the thresholds against real gatepost hits and the ramp landing (picked sensibly, not measured), and how the toe and wobble look on the models. Unit tests only.

## The girls' cars (TODO 161)

Settled with Jamie before building (2026-10-12):

- Amy's is a Mk1 Fiesta (1976 to 1983) in faded pale blue with a beige interior, tidy and cared for, with L plates on (item 167 has her pass her test in it). Jess's is a 1993 Corsa B 1.2i three-door: fuel injected (no choke, no carb faults), tatty and dressed up, primer on a panel and a loud big-bore exhaust. No real maker's badges on either.
- Built like the Land Rovers: their own spec, procedural model, dash, sound bank, wear, quirks and faults; front-wheel drive, manual boxes, key and starter; dashes and controls correct-ish for the real cars; a stereo in each, for looks only for now.
- Four seats each, two in front and two in the back, with belts, got in and out of as the 200TDi's are: the back reached past a tipped front seat. No opening doors yet (later, with the Land Rovers'), no wipers yet.
- A new hard-standing to park on. Road cars: low, on road tyres, slithering on wet grass, bogging easily and scraping on bumps. The girls take them out only on the yard and the farm road; you can take them anywhere.
- Each girl takes her own car for a go and only ever drives her own. Borrowing: take it without her and she minds when she sees, more if it comes back damaged or bogged; with her in it she's fine but remarks on your driving. Amy minds a lot, Jess less unless you're slow.
- Seats as the 200TDi's.
- Sound from BBC Sound Effects recordings Jamie downloaded: RemArc licence, not CC0, allowed as a named exception for this private game (ASSETS.md says so at the top, and CLAUDE.md points at it), to be replaced if the game is ever made public. Exterior recordings only, as the Land Rovers have no interior sound.
- New TODO items 183 to 188: arriving down the lane in the morning, driving home at the end of the day, Jess too fast down the lane, opening doors, wipers, working radios.

What was built:

- **Specs** (`FIESTA_SPEC`, `CORSA_SPEC` in `quadSpecs.js`, `car: 'fiesta' | 'corsa'` and `owner`), sharing `CAR_VEHICLE`, `CAR_ENGINE` and `CAR_VIBRATION`. Both at `scale` 1.15 like the Land Rovers, so they don't look small beside the girls. Fiesta: 830 kg, 4,000 N, four gears to 8.5, 15, 24 and 37 m/s (19, 34, 54, 83 mph), idle 1,010 to 1,090 (its recording's 1,084 to 1,187), 6,000 rpm, a pull-out choke. Corsa: 930 kg, 4,100 N, five gears to 8, 14, 21, 29 and 40 m/s (89 mph flat out), idle 860 to 900 (its recording's 854 to 909), 6,300 rpm. Both `cylinders` 4. Road tyres are their grip params: `grassFrictionSlip` 1.4 (a quad's 2.2), `mudFrictionSlip` 0.42, `tyreGrip` 0.6, `mudGrip` 0.24, `mudLoad` 0.3; soft springs (`suspensionStiffness` 24) and a low `rollInertia` (170) so they lean. Numbers are first guesses, not driven yet.
- **Front-wheel drive** needed no new physics: the layout's back wheels are `driven: false` and the spec's `fourWheelDrive` is 1, so only the front pair is driven (quadPhysics already honoured both).
- **Injection** is a new engine param, `injected`: the mixture gets exactly what a cold engine needs whatever the choke, no carb boils when hot, the choke keys do nothing on it, its quirk features drop the creeping choke, and its carb fault can't start. `coldIdleRpm` (450 on the Corsa) is the injection's own fast idle, falling away as it warms.
- **Models** (`vehicle/carModel.js`, one builder with a table per car, tested): the body side is one extruded profile with both wheel arches cut up into the sill (a test keeps every point clear of the tyre), with the bonnet and tail deck as skins under the same upper line, a glasshouse leaning in from the waist (A, B and C pillars, glass, a roof with a headlining), square lamps, a black slatted grille and black bumpers with a chrome strip on the Fiesta, oval lamps, body-coloured bumpers, a grey primer wing and a big chrome tailpipe on the Corsa. Inside: cloth trim drawn on canvases (beige check, grey fleck), carpet, door cards, two front seats whose backrests tip (`seatBack_left`, `seatBack_driver`), a back seat for two, four diagonal-and-lap belts named as the UTV's so `poseUtvBelts` poses them, a raked column and wheel (two spokes and three), a floor gear lever in the H of `gearLeverPose`, a handbrake between the seats, pedals, the Fiesta's choke knob, the stereo, and Amy's L plates and road atlas and Jess's air freshener and back-window sticker. Same named anchors as the 200TDi (no `seatMid`), so Quad, the mount and the girls' seat code work unchanged; `UtvMount` skips a seat whose anchor isn't there, `UtvRide` takes the front's seats (`front: ['left']`), and `pickRearSeat` only picks seats a vehicle has.
- **Dashes** (`dash.js`, `fiesta` and `corsa`): the Fiesta's is a big speedometer to 100 mph with its odometer, fuel and water dials beside it and the lamps underneath (charge, oil, choke, handbrake, indicators, main beam), white needles; the Corsa's has a rev counter to 7,000 with the red from its redline, a speedometer to 120 mph, stacked water and fuel gauges and a strip of lamps including a new engine management symbol, red-orange needles. The binnacle stands out of the dash face so it is seen through the top of the wheel, about 30 degrees below your eye line, which the -12 degree pitch you get in with puts on screen (checked by screenshot from the seat).
- **Sound** (`engine-bank-fiesta`, `engine-bank-corsa`, recipes in `tools/slice_engine_audio.py`): every BBC file carries its own description, so all 26 were sorted by what they hold. Neither car has a recording with high revs, so: the Fiesta idles on its exterior idle (62 to 88 s, whole cycles of about 109 ms) and revs on its depart (labelled 2,600 to 4,200) and the CC0 Mini's blips above; the Corsa idles on the Carlton's warm idle before it departs (34 ms between firings, 875 rpm) and revs on the same car pulling away (875 to 1,770 rpm) and the cold engine held at about 2,170 rpm (the hold's firings come every 13.8 ms). Both play a whole cycle every fourth firing (`pulseDivide` 4 with `cylinders` 4). The exterior starts catch in about a fifth of a second, too short to loop, so the starters are the interior recordings' (about half a second of cranking each). The shutdowns are the "approach and stop, switch off" endings. The Corsa has a stall of its own: the worklet plays a bank's `stall` one-shot for a stall where it has one (the cold Carlton dying), else the shutdown as before. Both banks preload with the others (2.3 and 3.0 MB). Rendered offline (`.scratch/cars-sound/renders/`) for Jamie to judge: at idle the Corsa's beat sits at its firing rate (28 Hz at 880 rpm); full throttle is about 11 dB over idle on both.
- **The hard-standing** (`layout.carStanding`, `world/carStanding.js` pure, `carStandingBuild.js`): first tried off the barn's south-west corner, but the land there falls a metre and more below the yard, and a level slab left a step a car couldn't climb. The flattest clear ground near the barn (searched against the spawn keep-out, the track and the slope) is the yard's east side at (-31, 60), 28 m from the barn doors, with 0.3 m of fall across it: a 10 by 6.8 m slab there, levelled with a 2 m shoulder, drawn as weathered concrete in bays with oil under each car. It counts as tarmac for grip (`fields.tarmacAt`), grows no grass, and keeps quads from starting on it; two quad slots moved off it, to (-54, 52) and (-24, 54).
- **In the game** the cars live in `landys` beside the Land Rovers (`car: true`), so their mounts, rides, seat tips, belts, wheel slices, sheltering from the rain and saves come for free; `GO_KINDS` has them, and `CAR_KINDS` keeps them out of the big-vehicle go (Jess never takes Amy's car). Their own go is an offer (`carGo` in `OFFER_KINDS`, `npc/carGo.js`, weighed by `carGoWeight`: Amy 0.8, Jess 1.3), every `every` 480 s per girl from `firstAfter` 200 s, always an outing down the farm road and back (`roadSpots` in `outings.js`: the end of the road or halfway along it; no distance limit), at her own pace (Amy 5 to 11 m/s at 0.6 throttle, Jess 8 to 16 at full) with a driver's gear changes. Only the owner gets "Drive it" on the wheel; asked about the other's car she says so (`carNotMine`).
- **Borrowing** (`Borrowing` in `carGo.js`, tested): taken without her and seen (within `seenRange` 45 m): `favourTaken` -0.5 and a line; brought back with a new fault or bogged: `favourDamaged` -1; taking her along: `favourRidden` +0.3. Sat beside you in her car, Amy says something above 18 m/s, Jess after 20 s under 7 m/s, and either when you stall it.
- **Lines** (`npc/carLines.js`): off, setting off, watching, back, not mine, taken, back, back damaged, ridden, stalled, too fast (Amy), too slow (Jess only).
- **Panel**: "Their cars" (Girls: things they do), "Fiesta riders" and "Corsa riders" (Driving) in the Tune tab, a line in the Girls tab's shared section and the console's `cars`, and `car-go` in each girl's "Make her do" (`girl amy car-go`). Each car's own groups are in the Vehicles tab as any vehicle's.
- **Checked**: unit tests (models, specs, front-wheel drive, arches, controls, the car go and borrowing); one headless run: the game loads without errors, both cars stand on their wheels on the slab, and screenshots from outside, from above and from the driver's seat of each (the dashes read). **Not checked**: driving either (grip, roll, bogging, scraping, top speeds), a girl's go in one (getting in, the route down the road, coming back to the slab), riding in the back, borrowing and the lines in play, the sound in the game by ear, and the spawn sag (guessed at 0.06 m, not measured).

### The girls' cars, second pass (Jamie's list)

Black arch liners close off the inside (the brown in the arches in play is the mud coat on them).
- **Shorter boots**: the Fiesta's tail is 8 cm shorter with a more upright hatch, the Corsa's 9 cm.
