- Jamie, after item 87: that kit covered the quads' outsides but not the tractor, the Land Rovers, the cars and the UTV, which need their insides too (gear knobs, handbrakes, dash pieces, steering wheels, seats, belts, mirrors, lights, handles). Built straight away as item 340, by the model that built 87, while it had the context.
- Same pattern as 87: every piece a module in `src/vehicle/detail/` with tests; the vehicles' own detail items place them (98 to 100 and the cars'). What changes the game now is the wear: the cabs' insides (the materials a model lists as `cleanMaterials`, kept free of mud) now take the wear layer without the mud, and touch wear marks where hands and feet go.
- Built (`src/vehicle/detail/`, tests in `tests/cabKit.test.js`):
  - **Shared** (`parts.js`): a default palette (`cabMats`: plastics, bakelite, vinyl, leather, rubber, webbing, chrome, steel, alloy, glass, mirror, lens colours) that every builder takes overrides of through `mats`, and `rounded`, `merge`, `mesh`, `keepGroup`.
  - **Steering wheels** (`steeringWheel.js`): five styles (`bakelite` with finger grips, `banjo` with sprung wire spokes, `flat` two-spoke, `utility` with a big pad, `sport` with a mark at twelve), 2 to 4 spokes, and a `worn` option that makes the rim at quarter to three a polished, paler material. The wheel lies in its group's XZ plane on the column (+Z twelve o'clock), as the cars' and Land Rovers' do. The grips' names stay with the vehicle (they are fixed points); the builder gives their positions.
  - **Seats** (`seats.js`): `bucket` (bolsters, a pleated middle, optional headrest), `bench`, `single` and a tractor's `pan` (dished pressed steel with drain holes, a low lip, a flat spring). Upholstery is pleats side by side with a 4 mm gap, so the wear layer finds the seams as nooks (grime in the seams) without anyone drawing them; piping along the cushion's front and the back's top.
  - **Belts** (`belts.js`): webbing as a flat strip (`ribbonGeometry`), the run over a seated person from the shoulder mount and sill to the buckle (`beltRun`), and `buildSeatBelt` with the `stowed` and `worn` children the existing belt poser expects, plus the D-loop, reel, buckle and stalk; `lapOnly` for a plain lap belt.
  - **Levers and pedals** (`cabControls.js`): five knobs (ball, pear, mushroom, T-handle, billet), a lever with a crank and a ribbed gaiter, handbrakes (`centre`, a Series' `pistol` grip, a `ratchet` with its toothed quadrant), pedals (`pad` with ribbed rubber, an `organ` throttle, a `tractor` pad) and `pedalLayout`.
  - **Switchgear** (`switchgear.js`): toggle, rocker, pull knob, rotary knob, warning lamp lens, gauge bezel and glass (the face stays the dash's canvas), vents (slatted or round) and a glovebox with its lid named for opening.
  - **Mirrors** (`mirrors.js`): `door`, `arm` (a Series' or tractor's flat glass on a bent rod), `wing` (round chrome), `interior`; `mirrorOffset` says where the glass is so it can be aimed.
  - **Lamps** (`lamps.js`): round and rectangular headlamps with a parabolic reflector behind a fluted lens (the fluting a shared canvas), indicators, a red, amber and clear tail cluster, a work lamp on its bracket. Each lens is its own material named `lens` (or `lens_red`...), so the existing light code can light it.
  - **Fittings** (`fittings.js`): door handles (`push`, `pull`, `lever`, `flap`), a grab handle, an over-centre bonnet catch, a strap hinge, grilles (`slats`, `bars`, diamond `mesh`), a parked wiper, a window seal round a rounded rectangle.
  - **Trim** (`trim.js`): vinyl, cloth, striped cloth, carpet, rubber mat, headlining, door card and grained plastic, each a 128 px tiling canvas used as map and bump. A textured mesh isn't merged, so trim goes on big surfaces (seats' panels, floors, liners), and it needs UVs (boxes and planes have them; the kit's lofts don't).
  - **Road wheels** (`roadWheels.js`): pressed steel (plain, `slots`, `holes`), a domed `hubcap`, a full plastic `trim` with vents, and `alloy` in `star`, `twin` or `turbine` patterns; to sit inside the kit's road tyre.
  - **Touch wear** (`touchWear.js`, drawn in `effects/wearLayer.js`): patches found from parts every model already names: a lever's knob (gearLever, driveLever, parkBrakeLever, spikeLever, handThrottle, chokeLever) at its top, the clutch pedal at its foot, footwells and pegs where the boots go, and the seat's edge (both sides of a quad's saddle, the door side of a cab seat). A vehicle can add its own in `userData.touchWear`; at most 8. Plastic and vinyl rub pale and shiny, paint wears through to steel, bare metal polishes, rubber goes smooth and grey; all polished (lower roughness), scaled by the vehicle's wear (a new one barely shows it). The quads found 5 patches each, the cabs 3.
  - **Cab insides wear now**: a model's `cleanMaterials` (the seats, dash, floor, lining and belts in the cars and Land Rovers) used to be left out of the mud coat altogether; they now get its shader with the mud switched off, so they take the wear and touch wear and stay clean. Their copies go in the coat's `bodyCopies`, so the seat frost still tints the material the seat really uses.
- Not done here: putting any of these on a vehicle (each cab's detail item does, replacing its own steering wheel, seats, belts, levers and lamps with the kit's while keeping the fixed points), touch wear on the steering wheel itself (the wheel is under `handlebars`, which the mud coat leaves alone; the kit's `worn` rim does it instead).
- Checked: unit tests (38 for the kit, and every vehicle finding its seat's touch patch and the cabs their levers'); one headless load: no shader errors, 24 wear programs compiled (5 for clean cab insides), every vehicle's touch patches set. Not checked: how any of it looks.

- **The loan quad as a Tamura Ursa 450 (TODO 96, done).** From thirteen photos Jamie put in `.scratch/quads/tamura-loan/` (a modern Kodiak 450 side-on, the 2003 Kodiak's front, a 700's LCD, the 450's switchgear, the engine bay, the rear and a green one's front end). Answers up front: a late Kodiak 450 as a dealer would lend it (my recommendation, over reusing the blue's Grizzly body), kept in neutral silver, with the dealer's sticker (`dealer` decal) and no wear. **Jamie's call: no choke**, since the real Kodiak 450 is fuel injected. `CHOKE_STYLES` gets `'none'`; the loan's engine is `injected: true` with `coldIdleRpm: 350` (the Corsa's mechanism: the engine meters its own cold start, no carb to flood or boil, no bench jobs for a carb or boot, no Q and Z); the pod is built without its lever (`buildGrizzlyPod(MAT, { choke: false })`), `Quad` skips posing a choke that is not there, the debug panel and the console leave out the choke setting for an injected engine, and the HUD hint already did. A wrong lever left in the pod would have been an unreachable one.
  - **Body**: a new `kodiak` style (`kodiakBody.js`, `kodiakDetail.js`, branches in `quadModel.js` where it shares the Grizzly's: `gz`). The decks' faces stay where the Grizzly's are, so its grille, bumper and tail panel fit as they are. What is its own: the strip along each deck's edge is black and the wall below it painted (`paintedSkirt` in the deck numbers, `flareGeometry`'s `part`), round lamps in black bezels low in the face's corners, the bumper in black steel, five-spoke black alloys, the shared utility exhaust's can left bright silver (`buildExhaust`'s `bright`; a first go built a second muffler beside the one the utility body already has), a dark seat, and the dealer's sticker. Its lights rocker is live: `barLights: true`, `P` as on the blue. **Proportions**: real sizes corrected to 2050 x 1170 x 1190, wheelbase 1250, track 930, tyre 635 (it was the 400 Kodiak, 1985 long, and the old model was 17% long and 16% wide); now length +4.7%, width +5%, height -2.4%, track +3.2%, tyre -6.1%, and `loan` is in `IN_PROPORTION`. No fixed point moved.
  - **Second round** (Jamie: lamps like the blue's but newer, a better-shaped front, black and silver pieces fighting near the front wings, the exhaust through the frame, no fuel tap, the black trim to continue round the side panels, the bars too high, and check the carb's troubles do not reach it). **The carb audit** found four things that still reached an injected engine (the Corsa's too, which had them): carb icing (`CarbIcing` was on unless diesel), spitting back through the carb (`spitPropensity`), the bench's "Carb mixture" row and the quick "clean the carb" job, and the debug panel's float bowl readout. All now skip an `injected` engine; the dripping float and the sediment bowl go with the tap (`noTap`), and the dirty-carb fault, the carb and boot bench jobs and the hot-start boil already did. **No fuel tap**: `TAP_STYLES` gets `'none'`, the spec has `noTap: true, startTap: 'on'` like the Corsa and the tractor, the model fits no tap or bowl, the HUD hint drops `F`, and `Quad` skips posing a tap that is not there. **Front**: the decks' faces lean 7 cm (out at the bottom, back at the top) and the corners are rounder (`cornerR` 0.15), so the grille and lamps stand 3 to 5 cm forward of the face's top edge (`KODIAK_FACE`); the lamps are the blue's almond made slimmer and sharper (`kodiakLampOutline`) with a pale daytime strip along the top of each, the bumper unchanged. **The fighting** was the black strip along the deck's edge sinking into the rolled edge where it ran down the face's corner (the red's flare is wide enough not to, the Kodiak's is 4.5 cm): it now tapers to nothing by the bottom of the face (`flareTaper`). **Black all round**: the inner wings and the decks' end walls are black plastic (`blackWalls`, as the real one's), a black sill runs along each side between the two arches' trims, and the side panel goes down to it, so the lower body reads as one black moulding with the painted fenders on top. **Exhaust**: the long pipe at x -0.27 passed through the side panel and the rear rails; the Kodiak's header is hidden inside the covers as the real one is, and only a short stub and a bright can (z -0.65 to -0.95, clear of the rails and the cross beam) show. **Bars**: **a fixed point moved on purpose**: the loan's handlebars from y 1.0 to 0.96, as the red's were (grips 1.068 to 1.027, dash 1.1 to 1.06, riderHands with them), `barsY` taking 0.96 for the Kodiak; `tests/fixedPoints.json` regenerated, which also recorded the moto's and the three-wheeler's seats and pegs that had not been captured before; nothing else changed. Not checked: the girls' hands on the lower bars.
  - **Third round** (Jamie: black parts at the back fighting too). Two causes: the frame's rear cross-tube (`buildFrame`, y 0.74 at z -0.9) poked through the top of the rear deck's rolled face on every quad whose rails end at -0.84 (the loan and the blue, and the green's), so it now sits at the rails' own end (`Math.max(-0.9, tail)`); and the black strip's taper left a hatched patch at the rear deck's corners, so the Kodiak's now dies away a third of the way down (`flareTaper` 1.7, clamped at nothing). Looked at from behind and from both rear quarters.
  - **Seen**: renders of both sides, the front, the rear and the bars from above. **Not seen**: a girl or you riding it, the dealer sticker close up, the Ursa name on the tank close up, the engine bay (the real one shows its engine behind the footboard; this one shows what the Grizzly's does), or how it sounds with the quick fast idle.
## Reversing a trailer (TODO 78)

Settled with Jamie before building: both trailers (the tipping one and the bale trailer) jack-knife; a pure hitch model, not a Rapier body; a fold jams the drawbar until you pull forward; Amy guides you back and gets cross when you turn the wrong way; the girls driving with a trailer on are left out (Jess "can't" and Amy "can" is for a later item); a debug readout and jack-knife and straighten actions, no parking bay.

- **The model** (`vehicle/trailer.js`, `rollAxle`, pure and tested). The old `followHitch` dragged the axle straight at the pin, which is stable in either direction, so reversing could never fold. `rollAxle` moves the axle only along the trailer's own heading (the way real wheels roll) for the hitch's move, split into steps of at most 0.12 m, so forward it still pulls straight and reversing any sideways error grows. A hitch that jumps more than 3 m (a respawn) falls back to `followHitch`. `TrailerMotion.update` takes the puller's yaw as a fourth argument; without it the old dragging is used, so every old call and test is unchanged.
- **The fold.** `hitchAngle` is the trailer's yaw less the puller's. Past `jackknifeAngle` (1.05 rad, about 60 degrees; the "Trailer" tuning) the trailer is held rigid at that angle to the puller (`jammed`, and `justJammed` for the one step it happens) and the puller's reverse drive is cut (`Quad.towJam`, read in the drive force in `quad.js`, set by the trailer each frame and cleared on unhitching). It lets go as soon as the free angle is back inside the limit, which is any forward motion: I tried a hysteresis band and it held the fold for ever, because the clamp itself keeps the angle at the limit. A toast tells whoever is riding, and the girls get a quiet `bang` notice.
- **Amy** (`npc/trailerGuide.js`, pure and tested; `trailerGuideLines.js`). An offer (`trailerGuide` in `OFFER_KINDS`, `trailerGuideWeight` 1.2 for Amy and 0 for Jess) once you have been reversing a hitched trailer for 2.5 s (accumulated, and decaying at half speed while you pause) with her free and within 35 m. She walks to 3.6 m behind the trailer's back end and 2.6 m off to the side nearer her, facing it, and holds `trailerGuide` while she does. The call comes from the angle: a trailer with its nose to the left has its back to the right, and the back of the puller has to chase it, so `right` ("right hand down") for a positive angle, `left` for a negative, `straight` under 0.1 rad, `stop` past 75% of the jam angle or jammed. A test drives a simulated reverse by her calls and checks it straightens from either side, and folds when you do the opposite. A new call comes after 1 s, the same one again after about 4 s while you are still going. Turning the wheel the wrong way for her last call (a quarter of full lock, for 1.4 s, while reversing and out of true) is a `cross`; the third is `guideCrossHard`, and on the fourth she gives up (`guideQuit`) and the 90 s cooldown starts. It ends when you stop reversing for 7 s ("Lovely" if the last call was straight or stop), get off or unhitch.
- **Debug and console.** Vehicles: "trailer hitch angle (deg)" in Running (with "folded"), "Jack-knife the trailer on it" and "Straighten the trailer on it", and "Put it right" straightens a folded one. Girls: "guiding a trailer back" in Day, and Amy's "guides you back with the trailer" in Make her do. Tune: "Guiding a trailer back" in the girls' things they do. Console: `vehicle.<id>.hitchAngle` and `folded`, and `trailerGuide`.
- **Left out:** the girls driving with a trailer on and reversing it into a spot, a place to park to (no bay), jack-knifing when the puller is a physics-driven quad going sideways at speed, a scrape sound for the fold, and Jess's remarks on it.
- **Checked:** unit tests (rolling the axle, straight reversal, folding on a held lock, no folding going forward, letting go, the calls and where she stands, a simulated reverse following and opposing her calls, her offers, crossness and ending). **Not checked:** how it feels to reverse in play (the 60 degrees and the rate it grows are guesses from the geometry), her walking round to the spot and standing clear of the trailer, the stop call arriving in time at a real speed, or the new lines by ear.

## Things walk off a shaking quad (#9, was TODO 114)

- **What:** `src/vehicle/looseThings.js` (pure, `tests/looseThings.test.js`, tunables "Things walk off") turns a vehicle's shake level (`unit.vibration.intensity`, the smoothed level the rest of the game already reads) into slow movement of what is loose on it. Not `creep.js`, which is the tractor walking off on its own.
- **The mug:** `Game.updateLooseMugs` moves it across its rack from the quad's shake, a heading that wanders so it does not go straight. Past `rackX` 0.24 / `rackZ` 0.2 m it is off: `Tea.shaken` ends it like a fall, and its owner says `teaShaken` (new lines, not the drive-off one, since nobody drove off).
- **The bed:** `Game.updateLooseBed`, for the UTV's bed and the Land Rovers' tubs. Each thing in a `CargoHold` shuffles within `bedSlack` 4 cm of its spot and twists up to `bedTwist` 0.25 rad. A jerry can goes over (onto its side) after `tipSeconds` 12 of shake over `tipFrom` 0.9, a lull easing the count back at half speed; once over it stays over until it is picked up and loaded again (`CargoHold.load` clears the state, which lives on the model's `userData.loose`). The trailers are not covered.
- **Numbers are guesses:** `shakeFrom` 0.3 and `tipFrom` 0.9 were set without measuring what each engine's `intensity` is at idle, cold or warm; `rate` 0.025 m/s per unit of shake puts a mug off the rack in about two minutes of a hard shake. Not checked in the running game: the look of a can on its side (it sits up half its width and may poke into the next spot), or how long a real cold single takes.

## Tyre wear that shows (#25, was TODO 130)

Settled with Jamie: wear by distance, surface and wheelspin, per axle (kept as the tread already was); about eight tyre sizes on the specs; stock on the spares shelf and ordered by the post van; a bench job for new tyres; a tyre rack in the barn, simple but in keeping with the rest; bald tyres lose grip (as before) and puncture more easily, no new girl lines; the lugs shrink on the model and the toolbox gauge reads the tread; no save version bump.

- Wear (`TyreSet.wearWheel`, `tyres.js`, tests in `tests/tyreWear.test.js`): per wheel, per step, from `Quad.fixedUpdate` next to the ground wear, for wheels in contact. Tread lost = metres times `wearPerMetre` 0.00007 (about 14 km from new to bald), times `wearTarmac` 2 on tarmac (`world.roadMud.tarmacAt`, blended) and `wearMud` 0.5 in mud or a puddle, times `wearDriven` 1.3 or `wearFree` 0.7, plus `wearSpin` 0.004 a second for a driven wheel at full wheelspin. An axle's tread takes its tyres' shares (a bike's one tyre takes all). Not run in play: those rates are worked out for about two or three days of driving a new set, not timed.
- Puncture: `Faults.update` takes `punctureFactor` (1 with new tyres, up to `baldPuncture` 3 with a bald worst axle, quadratic in the loss) and divides `punctureEvery` by it. Grip loss is the existing `treadDryLoss`/`treadMudLoss`.
- Sizes: `tyreSize` on every spec (`TYRE_SIZES` in `tyres.js`): kid (kids' quad, mini moto), quad (red, blue, green, loan), sport (yellow, orange), trike (ATC), utv, landrover (both), car (both), tractor (front and back share one). The barn find inherits its donor's.
- Stock: each size is a part on the `Spares` shelf (`tyreKid`... `tyreTractor`, counted in single tyres, packs of 2), in `ALL_PART_IDS` but not `PART_IDS`, so the parts rack's number keys and the girls' "running low" remarks stay with the five small parts. Start: 4 quad tyres, 2 each for kid, sport and trike, none for the rest (the big vehicles need an order). Saved like the other counts, so no save change.
- The bench: "Fit new tyres" is a seventeenth job on `.` (`newTyres`, `benchJobs.js`). Offered only when the worse axle is under `replaceBelow` 0.95. It takes that axle's tyres of the vehicle's size from the rack (a step with `part: '@tyres'`, resolved by `tyreJobFor`), sets its tread to 1 and pumps to stock; botched, it leaves a slow leak as the tyre job does. Not part of a full service and not something the girls choose.
- The rack (`tyreRackModel.js`, `SparesShelf` mode `'tyres'`): on the annex's south wall in the corner left of the helmet hooks (the wall's only free run), two decks of four bays with the tyres lying in stacks (the game's own tyre geometry, 14 segments, an InstancedMesh per size, at most 5 shown), a tag per bay with the size and count. Same panel, order keys (1 to 8), parcel and van as the parts rack, so the one `isOpen` the game already checks covers both. About 30k triangles in the worst case, drawn only when in view. Looked at in screenshots: the rack, its tags and the stacks sit clear of the helmets and the bay.
- Tread on the model: `treadTyre` remembers how it was built; `wornTyre(geo, look)` builds the same tyre with the lug tops lowered (`WEAR_LOOKS` 0, 0.4, 0.75, 0.92 of their height) once per look and shares it, and `Quad.showTread` swaps the tyre mesh's geometry when an axle's tread crosses 0.7, 0.4 or 0.15 (`wearLook`). The mud coat's wear bake skips tyre meshes (it would otherwise replace the geometry under the swap), so tyres have no rub-through edges, which rubber had little of. Only the vehicles with a quad's wheel (everything that is a `Quad`); the trailers' tyres do not wear. Looked at once headless on the red: a bald rear is clearly smooth against new lugs.
- The toolbox's pump shows "Tread: Front 82%  Rear 30%" (orange below 30%). Debug panel: front and rear tread are settable in the Tyres folder, bald tyres show under Problems, and "Put it right" makes them new without touching the rack. Console: `frontTread`, `rearTread` under a vehicle's tyres, and every tyre part's count under `spares`.
- Not checked: how the wear rate feels, the lug look on the other vehicles' tyres (the cars and tractor use their own geometry through the same `treadTyre`, so they should follow, but only the red was looked at), and frame time with the rack in view.

## A quad better in some places (#27, was TODO 132)

Settled with Jamie: a hidden strength per quad, small, picked from each quad's character; the girls learn it from riding, it nudges which quad they take, and they argue over it. No hint in the UI.

- **The strength** (`src/vehicle/strength.js`, pure, tests in `tests/quadStrength.test.js`): `strength: 'hill' | 'mud' | 'tarmac'` on a spec, worth `amount` 0.1 (10%, shared tuning "Quad strengths", `strengthAmount` on a spec scales it). Hill: drive up to 10% more going up a slope, in proportion up to a sine of `hillGrade` 0.12 (about 7 degrees), nothing downhill. Mud: wheel grip up by `amount` times how muddy that wheel is. Tarmac: grip up the same way on tarmac, and `tarmacDrive` 0.5 of it as drive. Applied in `Quad.fixedUpdate` (the drive force) and `QuadPhysics.applyControls` (per-wheel grip). Small enough to feel only over a few rides.
- **Picks, from the specs:** red hill (the steady low-geared manual), blue mud (the 4x4 utility), green mud (the heaviest, with the bayou body), yellow tarmac and orange tarmac (the two on sport tyres). The kids', moto, three-wheeler, loan, tractor and cars have none. The red is the only hill quad, so a girl asked "which is best up a hill" has one right answer. Easy to change: it is one line per spec.
- **What a girl knows** (`src/npc/quadOpinion.js`, pure, tested): per quad and kind of ground, seconds she has spent riding it (driving, or sat behind) on that ground, judged by `groundKind` (a slope, mud 0.4 or more, tarmac 0.6 or more, over 1.5 m/s). With under `minSeconds` 20 she has no view. After that her view is the truth blended with her own hunch (`hunchOf`, a steady hash of girl, quad and kind, up to `hunch` 0.08 either way) by `1 - exp(-seconds / tau)`, `tau` 150. So early on the two disagree, and about 25 minutes of riding a quad on a kind of ground makes them both right. Because there is nothing to compare with one quad, she only names a best once she has a view of two.
- **Saved:** the seconds, per girl, in `captureGirls`/`restoreGirls` (`opinions`), at `kept` 0.5 and capped at `cap` 1,200 s a quad and ground. Older saves load with none. Not `💥 breaking`.
- **It nudges her choice:** her own pick of which quad to drive (the `drive` offer in `companion.boredOffers`) is weighted `1 + pull * keenness`, `pull` 0.6, keenness being how good she thinks the quad is at its best as a share of the strength. Never forced; it only tilts the dice. The ownership rule (her own quad first) still wins.
- **They argue:** two banter triggers (`strengths` with roles kind and starter, `strengthsAgree` with kind; `BANTER_TIMING` gaps 240 s and 400 s) when both are stood idle within 12 m and each has a pick for that ground. The starter alternates. `strengths` counts as a dig for falling out like the other bickers. 15 exchanges in `strengthLines.js`, none naming a quad (a line is one recording; the quad is dealt per game), pointing at the ground instead.
- **Debug:** Vehicles tab Running shows "hidden strength" and the console's `strength`; each girl's Why folder shows "thinks of the quads", with "has ridden every quad" and "forgets which quad is best" in Make her do (`learn-quads`, `forget-quads`); the console's `quadViews`. Tune: "Quad strengths" (Driving) and "Learning the quads" (the girls).
- **FAQ:** one spoiler answer.
- **Not checked:** how the strength feels (it is meant not to be felt in one ride), how long a real game takes to settle the girls' views, and the lines by ear. All the numbers are first guesses.

## In-game names for every vehicle (#296 and #297)

Jamie's answers: name vehicles by their in-game maker and model, never by colour and never by a real make or model; the quads keep the names on their badges; four were missing, so **Moorland Tor** (the Series III), **Moorland Crag** (the 200TDi), **Thorne Finch** (the Fiesta) and **Ashby Griffin** (the Corsa; Jamie asked that the two cars not both be birds, so the Corsa is a griffin); the girls say the short model name, the panels, FAQ and wheel show the maker too; one PR for both issues; spec ids stay; the real-name check covers the girls' lines and the FAQ.

- **One source** (`src/vehicle/names.js`, tested in `tests/vehicleNames.test.js`): `VEHICLE_NAMES` has each id's maker, model and what the girls say. `fullName` ("Kestrel Farm 300") is the spec's `name`, `sayName` ("Farm 300", "Bruin", "mini moto", "tractor") its `label`, `thePhrase` ("the Farm 300") what `QUAD_PHRASES` and the achievements use. Descriptive names stay descriptive when they were never a colour: the kids' quad, the mini moto, the old three-wheeler, the loan quad and the tractor. `modelOnly` drops the maker for the logbook's column ("Farm 300"), but keeps "Hartley 35", since "35" alone isn't a name.
- **Spec ids are unchanged** (`red`, `blue`, `landrover200`, `corsa`...): farm saves, the girls' opinions, achievements and the console all key on them, so this is not a save-breaking change. The colour words in code and tests are ids.
- **The girls' lines**: the five quads' favourite and "I'd rather be on" lines, the knack, coolant, banter and gossip lines, the vehicle-go lines (Yak, Tor, Crag), the cab-shelter and sulk lines ("the Moorland", since either truck will do), the cars' lines (Finch, Griffin) and Jess's "the little one" for the kids' quad. 544 lines were new to the voice banks (the nicknames' versions of the shared templates were already recorded); they were recorded and committed on the branch.
- **Nicknames** (`NICKNAMES`, "the tomato", "Smurf", "Kermit") are left: they are what Jess calls a quad, and they were chosen for its standard paint. A repainted second-hand one keeps its nickname's joke wrong; leave it until there is a reason.
- **The test** fails if any girl's line, any FAQ text, the vehicle notes or the controls help contains a real make or model (Honda, Yamaha, Kawasaki, Suzuki, Land Rover, Defender, Fiesta, Corsa, Vauxhall, Massey Ferguson and so on; "ford" the river crossing is not on the list), or if a girl calls a quad by its paint ("the red one", "the blue quad"; red diesel, a red handle and a needle "in the red" are fine).
- **Also renamed:** the debug panel's tuning groups ("Yak bed", "Tor tailgate", "Crag back seats", "Finch hatch", "Griffin riders"...), the girls' buttons, the wheel's "Yak" and "Get in the Yak", the tyre shop's "Moorland tyre", the gamepad notes, the second-hand ads ("blue Kestrel Farm 300"), and docs/game.md. **Left on purpose:** code comments, DECISIONS, `realSizes.js`'s `like` (the published figures each vehicle was checked against), and the audio provenance in docs/how-its-built.md (real recordings the engine sounds were made from).
- **Not checked in the running game:** how the toasts, the wheel, the ads and the panel read with the new names, and the new lines by ear.

## Night riding with lamps (#33, was TODO 138)

Settled with Jamie before building: fitted at the barn bench, per vehicle; the quads and the Land Rovers only (not the tractor, the UTV or the cars); their own switch on 9 (L is the horn, P the headlights); one extra pool light per fitted vehicle at the same priority as its headlamps; the girls jumpier in the dark with new lines; kept in the save.

- **The lamps** (`src/vehicle/spotlamps.js`, pure, tested). `Spotlamps` per vehicle: `canFit`, `fitted`, `on`, `aim` and an eased `level`. They need the key on and a battery (`hasPower`), dim with a weak one (the same `lampSupply` as the headlamps) and add `load` 0.8 of a headlamp pair to the engine's `lightLoad` (capped at 1). A narrow, bright beam: 900 candela, 0.16 rad half-angle, 95 m reach, against a headlamp pair's 140, 0.55 and 40. Nothing I measured; chosen to read as a pencil of light down the field. "Spotlamps" in the Tune tab.
- **The bench job** (`spotlamps` in `benchJobs.js`, the last of `JOB_KINDS`). Four steps: stalks (a hold), lamps (a press), wiring (a hold with a window) and the aim (a hold with a window). A flaw in the wiring or the aim, or a botched job, fits them at `spotBotchedAim` 0.35: pointed `badPitch` 0.16 rad too low and `badYaw` 0.07 rad off to one side, scaled by how far the aim is from true. I left out a part from the shelf: it is a job, not a stocked part, so there is no new rack, order or key. The bench's keys were full, so `Slash` was added to the overflow keys. The job is offered only to a vehicle that can have them and has none; it is not offered on a barn find.
- **The pool** (`effects/vehicleLights.js`). A lit pair is a second candidate, `<id>:spot`, sorted straight after the same vehicle's headlamps (a stable sort, with the same mine / lighting / distance), so yours and the one lighting the way for you keep first call. The pool went from six to eight lights, still made at the start and never added to, so a fitted vehicle doesn't squeeze the headlamps out. The pair's light sits between the lenses and points along the lens's forward, turned by the aim.
- **The look** (`spotlampModel.js`). Two round lamps on short stalks above the headlamps (or either side of the middle for one central headlamp), hidden until fitted, lens emissive and a halo when lit. The stalk goes 3 cm into the headlamp's height so it never floats; `tests/spotlamps.test.js` builds every vehicle's model and checks the lenses are above and in front of the headlamps and the stalks' feet are below the top of them. Not looked at in a render.
- **The key.** 9 (`Digit9`), working while you ride a fitted vehicle: "Spotlamps on" / "off". On the gamepad's list and the controls help; free of the gears (0 to 4) and the camera and wave keys (5, 6).
- **Remembering.** `captureVehicle` keeps `{ fitted, on, aim }` (null if none fitted) and `restoreVehicle` puts it back only on a vehicle that can have them, so an old save loads as before and nothing is lost: not breaking.
- **The panel.** Vehicles tab: Running has fitted / on / how brightly; Problems has the aim (1 is true), in `problemSummary`, and "Put it right" aims them true but leaves them fitted. The console has `spotlamps`, `spotlampsOn`, `spotlampAim`.
- **The girls in the dark.** Two new params in every girl's reaction tuning: `reactDarkRange` (a startling reaction is noticed from this much further off, as a share of its usual distance, at full dark) and `reactDarkChance` (added to its chance). Amy 1.2 and 0.35, Jess 0.6 and 0.2, the default 0.8 and 0.2. Only the startles (flinch, step back, gasp) use them; covering her ears and cheering don't. When she does jump after dark (`darkness` over 0.5) she says `darkStartle` (`duskLines.js`, four lines each, a 25 s cooldown). It is a reaction to a notice, not an offer, so it follows `reactions.js`'s rule that a reaction stays a reaction.
- **Not done:** the rabbits freeze for the headlamps only, not the spotlamps; the girls don't fit them or ride with them on; no beam cone is drawn (the lenses, halo and the real light only). Not checked in play: how the lamps look on each model, how bright the beam feels or what the eight lights cost a frame (`perf.mjs` not run).

## A vintage motorbike in the barn (#38, was TODO 143)

Settled with Jamie before building: a new two-wheeler after a 1949 rigid-frame Bantam (reference photos in `.scratch/bantam`), always in the barn under a tarp as a restoration, reusing the barn find's job system; the starting ritual is a tickle on the carb, the spark lever and a kick; the lever does something in the engine model; the girls can drive it, solo; new sound from CC0 Freesound recordings; a feature.

- **Which bike.** The Ashby Plover 125 (`PLOVER_SPEC`, id `plover`, maker Ashby, a made-up British maker: the real name stays off it, and so does the model's, a bird in place of the bantam). A 123 cc-style two-stroke single with a clutch and a three-speed foot-change box, `manual: true` with `gearbox.gears` 3, so it uses the manual quads' drivetrain unchanged. It is not in the fleet pool (`EXTRA_SPECS`): it is built by `addPlover()` in `game.js`, like the rusty quad, and so it appears in `NOT_FOR_SALE`. Tyres use the `kid` size for now (the barn stocks no 19 inch tyre; a size of its own means a shelf, a part and a catalogue line, left for later).
- **Model** (`bantamBody.js` the numbers, `bantamDetail.js` the parts, `bantamModel.js`, tested in `tests/bantamBody.test.js`). Wheelbase 1.31 m, 19 inch wheels (R 0.31) with a ribbed tyre in front and a road one behind, forks raked 27 degrees (the bars' origin is on the fork's axis), a single loop of tube, a sprung saddle, valanced fenders swept as arcs about each axle (`fenderGeometry`), a tapering chrome silencer and the kickstart on the right (the right-hand side is `-x`). Measured against the real bike's figures: length +1.6%, width -0.9%, height +1.7%, tyre -0.8%, all inside the 8% in `IN_PROPORTION`; 30,276 triangles against the 35,000 small-vehicle budget. Its fixed points are in `tests/fixedPoints.json` (the grips at 0.25 m either side, the rider's anchor 0.8 m up and 0.24 m behind the middle). It has the kit's tyre, spoke and cable pieces but not the kit's decals beyond the maker's badge: the cream tank panels are a drawn texture a millimetre off the tank. Looked at from the right, front-right, rear-left, above and the seat; the tank's cream panel is flat on a rounded tank, so it stands a little off the surface at its ends.
- **Dash.** A new `smiths` dash (`dash.js`): a round speedo to 70 mph sweeping 270 degrees with a drum odometer, a red ignition lamp and the key, in a cup on the headlamp shell's top. Its needle follows the road speed with a little lag, as the Bayou's does.
- **The spark lever** (`src/engine/ignitionTiming.js`, pure, `tests/ignitionTiming.test.js`). `engine.advance` 0 (retarded) to 1 (advanced), read by an engine with `manualTiming`. `idealAdvance` rises from `idleAdvance` 0.3 at tickover to `topAdvance` 0.9 at the top, and 0.2 (`startAdvance`) cranking. `timingError` is the lever less that, with `pingFromAdvance` 0.12 of too much forgiven; `timingPower` loses up to `powerLoss` 0.5 of the power at 0.6 out either way, `retardHeat` and `heatPerError` add heat when it is late, `timingRough` adds roughness and `retardPop` adds overrun bangs. `kickBackChance` is nothing at or below `kickAbove` 0.45 and 0.85 at full advance. `startFactor` scales the chance a kick catches (full at 0.2, none 0.35 either side). Every number is a tunable with a range, in the engine's Tune group. Measured with 120 seeded cold bikes kicked up to six times: 90% catch with the lever at 0.2 and two tickles; 0% with no tickle and no choke; 0% with six tickles (flooded); 0% with the lever at 1, with 5.3 kick-backs a bike on average. The ordering is what the tests hold, not the numbers.
- **The kick** (`Engine.pull`). A kickstart is the pull-start with `kickStart` set: `O`, hold and let go, `pullSeconds` of turning. With `manualTiming` and `kickStart` a pull can kick back (`kicked` 0.6 s, `pullLeft` cut to 0.12 s, the events `kickBack` and `backfire`); `quad.js` says why, and the game has the nearest girl within 15 m say `oldKick`. The kick plays the `handCrank` one-shot (the Land Rover's handle did already; `handCrankOn` is now raised for any `kickStart` too). The pedal's pose in `quad.js` follows `pullLeft` (down and back and home on a spring), flings forward on a kick-back and draws back a little while the key is held. A seized kickstart (`BarnFind.kickSeized`, `quad.kickLocked`) does nothing and says so.
- **The tickler** (`Engine.tickle`, `tickled`). Each press adds `tickleAmount` 0.45; it enriches the mixture by `tickleRich` 0.9 a unit as if it were choke, and drains over `tickleSeconds` 45 s standing, five times quicker running; past `tickleFloodAbove` 1.4 it wets the plug at up to `tickleFoulRate` 0.12 a second while the engine is not running. Cranking with the throttle open clears it, as for any engine. `Y` presses it (`Y` has no other use on a two-stroke), by the carb from the seat or standing beside the bike; `[` and `]` move the lever the same way. The spark lever's keys share the bale arms' and the Land Rover's hand throttle's, which a bike never has.
- **Restoration** (`barnFind.js`). `CLASSIC_JOBS` are `fuel`, `carb`, `plug`, `points`, `brake`, `tyre` and `kick`; `pickClassicFind` draws `classicMinJobs` 4 to `classicMaxJobs` 5 of them from its own stream (`farmSeed ^ 0xba41`), always, and `BarnFind` is otherwise the rusty quad's. `jobsFor` takes the battery out for it, as for any pull-start-only quad. Two new bench jobs (`benchJobs.js`): "Clean and gap the points" (file the pitted contacts; set the gap in a window, botched leaves the plug fouling as the fuel job's botch does) and "Free the seized kickstart" (a brake-like soak, tap, work, kick). `game.js` now updates every barn find (`updateBarnFind` loops over `rustyUnit` and `ploverUnit`), and the bench works on whichever one is not yet running and is nearer you. The progress is kept in the farm save with the rest; the first start is toasted differently and ticks "Kick it over" on the bucket list, not "Barn find". "Full set" does not need it ridden. It stands at `layout.plover`, nose to the north wall, in the open floor a metre east of the barn find and the bales (my first spot, along the west wall, put the bale in the girls' way when they walked to it, found by sending one to drive it and watching her go in circles).
- **The girls** (`startingAttempt.js`). A girl starting the old bike tickles it (`tickleChance` 0.85, one or two presses, cold only), leaves the choke alone if she did (the two together drown it, which she did in the first run in the running game), puts the lever back to `startAdvance` with `leverChance` 0.7, kicks, thinks between kicks (`rethink`: lever back if it is advanced, tap on, choke only if she did not tickle) and with the throttle open if it is flooded. Between them they are `tickle`, `retard` and `kickedBack` events with lines in `oldBikeLines.js` (three each for Amy and Jess) and `oldKick` for a kick-back on you. When a girl is driving it the lever follows `idealAdvance` as she goes. Checked in the running game: Amy walked to it, mounted, tickled once, retarded, kicked, caught it in about ten seconds, stalled it pulling away once, and rode off in first, working the lever. I drove her with `requestDrive`, the call the wheel's "Drive it" makes; the wheel menu itself I did not open.
- **Sound** (`engine-bank-plover`, `ASSETS.md`). Idle pulses, mid revs and the stop with the decompression lever are AchimEngels' 1938 98cc Express SL 102 (Freesound 168847, CC0), which the sound brief had already picked as the ATC's alternative, with the DT 125's top end behind it as the ATC does, and the kickstart's swing is 3bagbrew's Honda 125 (574322, CC0): 171 idle grains (a median engine rpm of about 1,690, so the bike's tickover is 1,400 to 1,700 to sit on it), 143 accelerating and 25 decelerating. Played through the offline renderer: idle 0.033 to 0.037 rms and full throttle 0.21 against the ATC's 0.041 and 0.18. I did not listen to it (no way to from here): Jamie needs to. `tools/render_engine_sound.mjs plover out.wav` makes a WAV, and now kicks a bike with no starter motor over (after its ritual) instead of failing. The first draft of its idle matched the bank at a 0.68 speed ratio, which is why the spec's idle is 1,550 and not the 1,150 a real Bantam has.
- **Loading.** The old bike is built every game, so its tarp and rust cost every load: the first build took Ready from 2.5 s to 3.6 s in the dev build (`loadtime.mjs`, origin/main against this branch, two runs each). The tarp's grid of heights was ten thousand rays through every triangle (587 ms); it is now one pass laying each triangle on the grid (`sampleHeights` in `rustyLook.js`, the same thirteen sample points round each grid point, 28 ms) for every barn find. The rust patches, cracks and webs (`applyDamage`, 416 ms) go on in `dressFinds()` after the load, one vehicle at a time, under the tarp that hides them (a restored farm whose tarp is already off hides it at once). Ready is 2.5 to 2.7 s again. `perf.mjs` says the spawn view is over its budget on origin/main already (3,238 calls, 3.92 million triangles, 12.1 ms a frame) and 3,325, 4.06 million and 12.6 ms with this branch: the old bike is about 3% of the triangles and does not change that.
- **Debug.** Vehicles tab: Running has the spark lever (settable), what the revs want and the timing error, the kicked-back seconds and the tickler's fuel (settable); Problems has the flood; "Put it right" clears it; Do something has "Tickle its carb" and "Kick it over". The console has `sparkLever` and `tickled`. The radial menu has the tickle and the lever.
- **Not done:** a tyre size of its own, a pillion (one saddle), the girls' nerve on a bike this size (they ride it like any manual vehicle, with the lean model holding them up: a `mini moto` style refusal was not built), a voice line for the first start of this bike in particular, a lever animation on the bike when a girl drives (the arm follows the engine's setting but her hand does not go to it), the kick pose of the rider's leg (the pedal swings and the rider stays still), a second sound for the kick (it reuses the Land Rover's one-shot's slot with a Honda's), and a rider's-eye check that the lever and tickler are reachable on a pad.
- **Checked:** unit tests (engine, starting, model, bank, barn find); the smoke test; four screenshots of the model on its own and five in the running game (under its tarp, the tarp off, from the seat, a girl starting it and riding it by log). Not checked: the sound by ear, the kick pose, the tarp's drape over the saddle's springs, and the model against the photos once more by someone who owns one.

## The old bike's lamps, lights switch and spark lever (#345, a follow-up to #38)

Found by playing it: the tail lamp and headlamp floated clear of the bodywork, the spark lever was not clipped to anything, the headlamp never looked lit and there was no way to switch the lamp off.

- **Floating parts.** The tail lamp had been put at the top of the fender's old tail, 11 cm above the fender's real rear end, with a tube to nowhere; it now sits on the fender's tail (the arc stops at -105 degrees, z -0.99, y 0.22) with the plate hanging from its underside. The headlamp's chrome rim and glass were 4 cm ahead of the shell's rim (the dome is 111.6 degrees of a sphere, so the rim stands 0.368 r times the stretch ahead of its centre); both now sit on the rim. The spark lever's pivot was 1.4 cm clear of the bar (the bar bends there, so there is no straight run to clip to); it is now on the clutch perch's inner face. The rear fender's stay ends on the seat stay.
- **A test for it** (`tests/bantamJoined.test.js`). Every visible mesh's surface is sampled every 4 mm in the model's frame (wheels where the game puts them) and meshes whose samples come within 6 mm are one piece; the test fails if the bike is more than one piece, and names the loose ones. Put back the old tail lamp position and it names the lamp and its lens. The tyre is left out (it is a hollow shell round the rim, which sits inside it without touching its surface), as are the faked beam and the decals a millimetre off the tank. I had a first version that kept only the last mesh in each hash cell and passed on the broken bike; the check is that it fails on the old positions.
- **The headlamp never lit** because `weatherModel` (the barn finds' rust and dust) cloned every material, the lamp's glass included, while the game lights `userData.lensMaterial`, the original, which no mesh used any more. `weatherModel` now points the model's own material handles (`lensMaterial`, `seatMaterial`) at the clones. The rusty quad had the same fault and is fixed with it. Checked in the running game, engine running at tickover: level 0.29, glass glowing amber, beam and halo showing.
- **The switch.** `barLights: 'switch'` on the spec, as the three-wheeler's: `P` is off or on, starting on, a `lightsRocker` in a chrome housing clamped round the left bar inboard of the clutch lever, posed by `poseBarPod`. The lamp still runs off the magneto, so it is dark with the engine stopped, which is why a bike that is switched on and not running shows nothing. `P` is in the stood-beside hints, the controls list, the FAQ and the docs.
- **Checked:** unit tests; a screenshot from in front, behind and the seat with the engine running. Not checked: that the rocker is reachable on a pad (`P` is on the radial menu already).

## Snorkels and wading (#39, was TODO 144)

Decided with Jamie: both Land Rovers only; it raises the depth the engine starts gulping at and cuts the gulp rather than making it immune; fitted by a bench job like the spotlamps; a visible pipe hidden until fitted; one line from each girl.

- **Logic** (`src/vehicle/snorkel.js`, pure, tested). `Snorkel` per vehicle: `canFit` (only the Land Rovers), `fitted`, `seal`. `effect()` gives `waterGulp` (`engine/water.js`, a new optional fourth argument) `{ depthFrom, scale }`: the depth the engine starts taking water at goes from the engine's `waterDepthFrom` 0.55 to `depthFrom` 0.9, and what does get in is `gulpScale` 0.2 of the usual. A badly sealed one adds back `leakShare` 0.3 of the remainder scaled by how unsealed it is: the botched seal 0.35 gives 0.36 of the usual gulp. "Snorkel" in the Tune tab.
- **What that means in the water** (arithmetic in `tests/snorkel.test.js`, not tried by feel). The puddle value is 1 in the middle of the ford, so it is never fully safe: crossing its 7 m at 10 m/s takes in about 0.5 of water fitted (a splutter; it stalls at 0.75) against a drowned engine unfitted, and with the stream up (the 2.2 gulp multiplier) it still drowns. Any puddle shallower than 0.9 takes nothing at all.
- **The bench job** (`snorkel` in `benchJobs.js`, last of `JOB_KINDS`, offered only to a Land Rover without one, so it is in the big bay's list). Four steps: the hole (a hold), the pipe (a press), the sealant (a hold with a window) and the hose (a hold with a window). A slip in either window, or a botched job, fits it at `snorkelBotchedSeal` 0.35. Like the spotlamps it is a job, not a stocked part. The bench's keys were full, so the comma was added to `OVERFLOW_KEYS`.
- **Kept** in `captureVehicle`/`restoreVehicle` (`snorkel`, null when not fitted; an old save has none and loads the same, so not a breaking change).
- **The look** (`snorkelModel.js`). A black pipe 9 cm wide on the nearside (the driver's left, +x) wing's flat top just ahead of the screen, up to the screen's top, a bend, a short run forward and a scoop open to the front. Its foot is sunk 3 cm into the wing. The model builders now set `userData.variant` so the right wing is used. `tests/snorkel.test.js` fires a ray down onto the wing under each foot and checks the foot is just inside it. Not looked at in a render.
- **The girls.** `snorkelDone` and `snorkelBotched` in `benchLines.js` (Amy: sensible warnings about the deep water; Jess: delighted, or shrugging off a leak), always said when the job ends. Recorded with the rest.
- **The panel and the console.** Vehicles tab: "snorkel fitted" in Running, the seal in Problems, and "Put it right" seals it but leaves it fitted. Console: `snorkel`, `snorkelSeal`.
- **Checked:** unit tests and the smoke test. **Not checked:** how the pipe looks on either Land Rover, whether it clips the windscreen frame or the door glass, the feel of the ford fitted and unfitted, and the girls' lines by ear.

## Brakes that fade on the hill (#47, was TODO 152)

Nothing needed settling; the numbers were chosen here.

- `BrakeState` (brakeState.js) gains `heat` (0 to 1). It rises at `heatRate` 0.12 a second at full brake, scaled down below `heatSpeed` 3 m/s, so a held brake is cooked in about eight seconds. It falls at `coolRate` 0.02 plus `coolAir` 0.012 per m/s, only while the brake is off, so dabbing cools it and a held brake does not. Stopping from speed adds a fifth of a bar at most and is gone in a few seconds.
- `fade` is a smoothstep from `fadeFrom` 0.45 to full heat, and `grip` now multiplies the wet loss by `1 - fadeLoss (0.8) * fade`. Only the lever's brake fades; the parking hold, handbrake and rolling resistance do not. Heat is not saved: a loaded farm starts cool.
- The fix is engine braking that depends on the gear. In the manual gearbox, off the throttle and faster than the gear's top speed, `overrunForce` holds back by mass times `overrunHold` 4 m/s2 times how far over (up to twice the gear's speed), scaled 1 in first, about 0.6 in second and 0.25 in the highest (`topSpeed(highest) / topSpeed(gear) / 4`, clamped). So first settles a little over twice its top speed on a steep bank, and the higher gears barely help. The automatics have none, so they rely on the brake and cooling. Applies to every manual (tractor, Land Rovers, cars, manual quads).
- Amy and Jess on the back say `brakesFading` (hillLines.js, via `companionVoice.js`) when `heat` passes `fadeWarn` 0.6 with the brake held, naming the low gear. It is not a skill she teaches.
- Debug: "brake heat" in Running, "hot brakes" in Problems (cleared by "Put it right"), `brakes.heat` in the console, `heat*`, `fade*`, `cool*` under "Brakes" tuning and `overrunHold` under each Gearbox folder.

## Chain and sprockets you can see wear (#48, TODO 153)

Settled with Jamie: all four chain-driven vehicles (the sport quad, the mini moto, the old three-wheeler and the Plover); a jumped chain hangs loose off the front sprocket; a dry chain goes rusty; a FAQ item; no reference photos (built from real 428 chain proportions).

- **Shared kit.** `chainShape.js` (pure, tested in `tests/chainLook.test.js`) holds the geometry: the outer tangents between the two sprockets, the closed loop the rollers follow, links spaced evenly round it at a 12.7 mm pitch, the sprocket outline and `chainWear(faults)`. `detail/chainDrive.js` turns that into meshes and returns a drive with `set(wear)` and `spin(angle)`. Each model calls `buildChainDrive` where its old plain-disc sprockets and tube runs were (`quadModel.js` `buildSwingarm`, `tz65Detail.js`, `atcDetail.js`, `bantamDetail.js`); the rear sprocket moved out of the wheel's `spin` group on the mini moto and the Plover so it stays on the chain.
- **What shows.** Slack hangs the lower run first (about 1% of its length snug, 12% at full slack, growing with the square) and the top run once slack passes 0.3. From slack 0.35 to 0.9 each tooth wears from a stubby trapezoid to a thin fin leaning the way it turns. A chain that has come off stays round the back sprocket and hangs from it to a loose end below the front one. Dryness (`chainDryness`) lerps the chain and, less so, the sprockets from dark oiled steel to rust.
- **Live, not baked.** `Quad.update` calls `set` every frame; it rebuilds the links only when slack (in steps of 0.04), the hook (steps of 0.1) or off change, and the colour when dryness (steps of 0.05) does, so it follows the toolbox and bench fixes at once. Sprockets turn with the rear wheel, the front faster by the ratio of their circles. The chain's materials are in the model's `liveMaterials`, so the mud and wear coat leaves them alone (it copies materials, which hid the rust, and its baked nooks would be missing from rebuilt links).
- **Cost.** About 1.2k triangles for a chain and 0.7k for the two sprockets on the mini moto, one box per link. The `small` triangle budget (mini moto and three-wheeler) went from 35k to 38k.
- **Not changed:** the chain still ends at the rear wheel's rest position, so on hard bumps the hub moves off it a little (it did before); nothing new to tune, so no panel or console additions (slack and oil were already in Problems and `chain.*`).

## A fuel gauge you can't trust (#56, was TODO 161)

The two lying-gauge quirks (reads high, sticks) already existed with Amy's logbook line and the girls' warnings; what was missing was a way to find out for yourself. Decided with Jamie: a new key, not `E`, and it works on every quad, with Amy dipping before she rides. The quirk stays a random deal on any quad with a gauge, not a fixed old quad.

- `U` on foot, looking at a quad (`Game.dipTank`, in the foot context of `controlMap.js`, a prompt chip, the radial ring and the FAQ). `U` is the diff lock when riding, which is another context, so it doesn't clash. It plays the `latch` cue and gives a toast.
- Pure logic in `src/vehicle/tankDip.js` (tests in `tests/tankDip.test.js`; "Tank dip" in the Tune tab): `readTank` words the real level (dry, barely wet, a quarter, a third, half, three quarters, nearly full, full) with a reading noise of 0.03 of the tank and a "down in the reserve" note from the tap's reserve litres; `gaugeLies` is a difference of 0.15 of the tank. The quad keeps `shownFuel`, what its gauge read with the lies in it, for the comparison; a quad with no gauge or pod just gets the reading.
- Amy (`Game.amyDipsTank`, in her pre-ride check, only when the walk-round found nothing): if she believes the quad has a lying gauge (`CompanionVoice.knowsQuirk`, the same belief `watchQuirks` warns from, so she can be wrong) she dips with chance 0.9 and says `tankDipLow`, `tankDipLies` or `tankDipFine`. A real quirk found is written in the logbook as the quirk's existing entry; Jess never dips. When you dip with Amy near and the gauge is far off a lying quad, she writes it too.

## Your own car (#229, was TODO 338)

Settled with Jamie up front (on the issue): built from his photos in `.scratch/quads/309/` as a basic three-door 309 GLD in silver-grey with grey cloth, bottles in the door pocket, CDs and some dents; this item is the car and the morning, with driving the girls' cars in (#99) and parking where you left it (needs places in the save) later; lifts about one day in four and both one in eight; only you drive it; named the Vauclair 39D.

- **The model** (`p309Body.js`, `p309Model.js`) is the Fiesta's builder reshaped (wing, door and quarter, a glass hatch over a deck and tail panel, which suits the 309's bustle), so it kept every gap and fighting fix the Fiesta's eight rounds found. Measured off the silver side-on photo with the 2.47 m wheelbase as the rule: 4.00 x 1.66 x 1.38 m against 4.05 x 1.63 x 1.38, in `IN_PROPORTION`. Its test is the Fiesta's set; it found the boot block in the rear tyres, mudflaps and rubbing strips flush with the panels, and the CD cases sharing a plane. The size check now skips `aerial` as it skips mirrors (published heights leave both out).
- **Dents** are a disc of paint 1 mm off the panel whose normals bow in like a pushed-in bowl (`dentGeometry`), so they read as dents without cutting the panel. The first go had them bowing out and they read as bumps; a test now holds every normal towards the middle.
- **Wear** is 0.12: 0.3 and 0.2 read as rust spots all over the silver, which shows them where the Fiesta's brown hides them.
- **The diesel.** Glow plugs, the key stops it, waxing and bleeding as the 200TDi. Its bank (`engine-bank-309`) is the BBC's own 309: the exterior idle at 748 to 803 rpm (so the spec idles at 760 to 800 and plays it at its own pace), the depart for revs, its own switch-off, and the Jumpy's diesel crank, since the BBC's start catches at once. The interior six-minute run is unused.
- **The morning** (`farm/arrival.js`, pure and tested): dealt from today's stream (`this.seed`), not the farm's, since a lift is a day's chance like the weather. A girl you gave a lift sits in your passenger seat (the second behind her) and gets out 1.5 to 4.5 s after you; the others sit at the wheel of their own running cars (a `driver` slot on the cars' mounts, used only here) for 12 to 60 s, then switch off and get out. A lifted girl's car is not built that day; the save carries its last entry across (`keepAway`), so nothing is lost. The engines start already running and warm (`EngineModel.runWarm`).
- **Knock-ons.** Starting on the pad gave you "End of the road" for nothing: places you start the day in count only once you have left them (`earnedPlaces`). The frame budget measures on foot, so it gets you out of the car first.
- **Lines**: `carYours` (asked to drive it: left open on her wheel so she can say so), `liftThanks`, `yourCarRide` (now and then while you drive her in it).


## Your car: fixes from the first look (#491)

Jamie's list after #229, all fixed, each with a test in `tests/p309Model.test.js` (or `arrival.test.js`, `controls.test.js`, `carsParking.test.js`) that would have caught it.

- **Mud inside.** The mud coat keeps mud out of a cab's clean materials but puts it in every nook of a shared one, and the inside used the outside's black, grey and steel (the vents are all nooks). The inside has its own copies (`IN`), listed as clean; the pedals take their materials as arguments now (`addPedals` `armMat`, `ribMat`). The bottles and CD cases keep their own looks.
- **The cluster**: laid out from one table (`P309_CLUSTER`) with room between pockets, dials and lamps. **The clock** runs key on or off (Jamie).
- **The tray** ran into the centre vents: 0.19 to 0.59 across now, the glovebox with it, three CD cases in it.
- **Dents**: those on a door are in the door's group, so they swing with it (`onDoor`); the rear one moved off the arch. A test keeps every dent clear of the arches and the shut lines.
- **The fuel flap** is a painted panel with a notch, not a black outline. **The parcel shelf** sits under the glass and the deck, behind the seat back and inside the back belts (`P309_SHELF`). **The struts** are 0.6 m out from the middle at both ends and their hatch end is on a right angle in from the glass; a test samples them through the hatch's swing against the leaning pillars.
- **No choke** on a diesel: `hasChoke` (controls.js) answers for the key hints, the choke keys and the choke quirks, so an injected engine and a diesel both have none. The car's key hint says to wait for the glow lamp.
- **Parked in the row**: your car at the west end beside the Finch and the Griffin, 5.2 m apart, the row 0.8 m nearer the road, and the pad's radius 9 m (was 8) so every car's footprint is on the tarmac with half a metre to spare.
- **Left running**: a girl getting out of her own car of a morning doesn't always switch off: Amy 15% of mornings, Jess 45% (`amyLeavesRunning`, `jessLeavesRunning`).


## Working mirrors (#61, was TODO 166)

- **A picture on the glass.** A mesh a model names `mirrorGlass` gets a plane just behind its back face (`mirrorView.js`), showing a small render from a camera at the glass looking back along the vehicle, turned 9 degrees outwards for a door or wing mirror, straight back for the rear-view one. Their pictures are not flipped: a camera looking back already puts what is on your left behind you on the left.
- **Where.** The Land Rovers (door and wing mirrors, and the rear-view one on both), the UTV's two arm mirrors and the three cars. The Series' and the Defender's black heads, and the cars' rear-view bars, are the glass (the live picture lies over their back face). Not adjustable (Jamie): a fixed angle fits "no readout, find out by feel" and needs no key.
- **Only yours, only ahead.** Only `riddenUnit` has live mirrors, and they draw only while you look within 75 degrees of straight ahead. Every other vehicle keeps the dull glass.
- **Cost.** One mirror per pass, in turn, every second frame, so each of three refreshes about every 6 frames (10 Hz at 60). 160 pixels across, 120 m of view, the sun's shadow map left as the last full frame drew it. A pass drew 93 to 212 calls in the Griffin and the P309 on the pad (the main view is about 800 there), about 0.3 to 0.6 ms of JS at 3 microseconds a call, so about 0.15 to 0.3 ms a frame on average. Tunables under "Mirrors" (size, every, range, fields of view, lookAngle, enabled).
- **Left out of the picture.** The vehicle's own model and any girl sat inside its footprint (1.3 m either side, 2.6 m either way of its centre); a trailer, other vehicles and the girls behind are drawn.
- **Unlit on purpose.** The picture's material is a basic one, like a screen: it shows an already-lit view of the world.
